Verified via a standalone harness (scratchpad only, not committed): already-tight input left byte-for-byte identical; mis-spaced `@field : JvmField` correctly tightened both sides to `@field:JvmField`; all of `@get:`, `@param:`, `@delegate:` (the non-keyword-lexed one, confirming the text-match approach actually works, not just the keyword-lexed ones) correctly tightened; a plain `@Override` with no colon left completely untouched (falls out of the state machine at the very first "next token isn't a known target" transition, never risks misfiring on an ordinary annotation); an unrelated `:` in a function parameter list (`fun y(z : Int)`) left untouched, confirming the state machine doesn't false-positive on every bare `:` in the file; and a comment interposed between the target and its `:` (`@field /* c */ : JvmField`) correctly blocking the rewrite on that specific gap (left as `@field /* c */ :JvmField` — the *other* gap, after the colon, still tightens independently, same per-gap-independent evaluation as every other pass in this file, not a bug). No shared-class change. Full C/C++/Java suite 32/32. Not yet wired into `Formatter.formatOne` — verified standalone only, same caveat as every other Step 3 item so far. |
| RDD_KEY_109 | Kotlin lambda-with-receiver/function-type nesting exemption + arrow spacing — STYLE_KOTLIN.md §17/§17.1 | Two separate pieces, one shared-class touch and one Kotlin-only file. (1) `ComplexityPaddingEvaluator.isLoose` (shared, used by `MiscRule.enforceComplexityPadding` for STYLE.md §3.1's tight/loose `(...)`/`[...]` padding across all languages) was extended with a new `isReceiverFunctionTypeParens` check: a `(` immediately preceded by `.` (OP, confirmed via a standalone token-type harness before writing any code — `.` and `->` are both lexed as `TokenType.OP`, not `PUNCT`) whose matching `)` is immediately followed by `->` is a lambda-with-receiver's own invocation-site parameter list (`Type.(Params) -> Ret`), and that whole matched span is skipped over (not recursed into) rather than counted as "a nested `(` is present" when evaluating whether some *enclosing* `(...)`/`[...]` is loose — needed because otherwise e.g. `val block: (StringBuilder.() -> Unit) = {}`'s outer parenthesized-type-annotation parens would incorrectly get loose-padded to `( StringBuilder.() -> Unit )` just because the receiver's own empty `()` happens to appear inside, even though STYLE_KOTLIN.md §17 is explicit this is "a single function-type token, not a nested-paren construct." Confirmed a pure no-op for C/C++/Java: neither language has a `.` token immediately followed by `(` in this shape (Java has no `Type.() -> Ret` syntax at all), same reasoning as `RDD_KEY_100`'s `TIGHT_PAREN_KEYWORDS` `"when"` addition. Deliberately does *not* recurse into the skipped span's own content, so a function type genuinely nested inside one of its own parameters (`((Int) -> String, Boolean) -> Unit`) still falls through to the default loose-triggering path unchanged — STYLE_KOTLIN.md §17's own documented "Known gap," left exactly as it already was, verified via harness to confirm no accidental fix-by-side-effect. (2) New `KotlinSpecificRule.enforceArrowSpacing` (Kotlin-only, new file section) — a flat whole-file pass forcing exactly one space on each side of every `->`, covering both the function-type arrow (§17) and a lambda literal's own parameter-list arrow (§17.1), "one consistent arrow-spacing rule across all three constructs" (the third being `when`'s arrow, §4) per §17.1's own text. Explicitly excludes every `when`-branch selector arrow via a new `collectWhenBranchArrowIndices` helper (walks the same `when(...) { ... }` block-finding shape `formatWhenExpressions` uses, then locates each branch's own arrow index via the existing `findTopLevelArrow(tokens, b.labelStart, b.bodyStart)`) — necessary because `formatWhenExpressions`/§4/RDD_KEY_101 owns that arrow's spacing as column-aligned padding (potentially many spaces, not one), and this new flat pass must never collapse that regardless of eventual pipeline wiring/ordering (neither method is wired into `Formatter.formatOne` yet). Same conservative bailout as every other pass in this file: a gap containing a comment, a NEWLINE, or a frozen token on either side of an arrow blocks forcing a space on that side — critical for the multi-line lambda-body case (`{ item ->\n    item.transform()\n}`) where forcing a space before the newline would introduce trailing whitespace. Verified via a standalone harness (scratchpad only, not committed): empty and populated receiver-parens correctly exempted from complexity padding (both stayed tight); the Known Gap case's nested function type confirmed still loose/unchanged (no regression risk there); mis-spaced lambda arrow (`{ x,y->x+y }`) correctly normalized to `{ x,y -> x+y }`; the multi-line lambda-arrow case confirmed zero trailing whitespace; an already-correctly-spaced function-type arrow left unchanged; and a `when` block's own arrows confirmed untouched by `enforceArrowSpacing` alone, then correctly column-aligned once `formatWhenExpressions` ran on top — confirming the exclusion mechanism actually prevents the conflict it was designed for, not just in theory. `make test` 32/32 both before and after the shared-class (`ComplexityPaddingEvaluator`) change, per the mandatory before/after discipline for any shared-class touch. Not yet wired into `Formatter.formatOne` — verified standalone only, same caveat as every other Step 3 item so far. |
| RDD_KEY_110 | Kotlin `for` loops and ranges — STYLE_KOTLIN.md §10 | Two halves, only one needed new code. The (b) half -- `in`/`until`/`downTo`/`step` "recognized as ordinary word-operator tokens for nested-bracket detection to see through them correctly" -- turned out to already be true with zero code changes: confirmed via a standalone harness that `in` is already lexed as `TokenType.KEYWORD` (in `TokenizerCore.KEYWORDS_KOTLIN`) and `until`/`downTo`/`step` are plain `TokenType.IDENTIFIER` (Kotlin soft keywords / ordinary stdlib infix functions, not special-cased anywhere in the tokenizer), so none of them are ever mistaken for a `(`/`[` by `ComplexityPaddingEvaluator.isLoose` -- `for(i in 1 until 10 step 2)` stays tight exactly as `for(i in 1..10)` does, and a genuinely nested call inside a for-head (`for(i in getStart(x)..getEnd(y))`) still correctly triggers loose padding, confirming the existing generic detector already "sees through" these word-operators without needing any keyword-set addition, contrary to the Step 1 scoping table's original guess that this needed new work -- reclassified from (b) to (a) in the scoping table, no shared-class touch, verified-only (same posture as K2.1/RDD-less verification, though this row keeps its own key since it's paired with the genuinely new §10 work below). The (c) half -- `..`/`..<` range operator's own tight spacing -- did need new code: new `KotlinSpecificRule.enforceRangeOperatorSpacing`, a simpler one-sided sibling of `enforceNullSafetyOperatorSpacing`/§5/RDD_KEY_102's state machine (no spaced-variant operator to handle, unlike `?:` alongside `?.`/`!!` -- both `..` and `..<` are tight, full stop), collapsing the gap on either side of a range operator to zero width. Kept as its own method (not folded into `enforceNullSafetyOperatorSpacing`) since it's a distinct STYLE_KOTLIN.md section with its own RDD entry, matching this file's existing one-method-per-section convention. Verified via a standalone harness (scratchpad only, not committed): already-tight `1..10` left unchanged; mis-spaced `1 .. 10` and `0 ..< 10` both correctly tightened to `1..10`/`0..<10`; and, separately, `MiscRule.enforceComplexityPadding` re-confirmed via the same harness that `until`/`downTo`/`step`-bearing for-heads stay tight while a for-head with a genuinely nested call (`getStart(x)..getEnd(y)`) still correctly loose-pads the outer `for(...)`. No shared-class change for the range-operator method itself (`.`/`..`/`..<` were already tokenized as `OP` from an earlier session's tokenizer work, per the tokenizer's own comment block -- nothing to add there either). Full C/C++/Java suite 32/32. Not yet wired into `Formatter.formatOne` -- verified standalone only, same caveat as every other Step 3 item so far. |
| RDD_KEY_111 | Kotlin `enum class` with members blank-line "emphasis" around the mandatory `;` — STYLE_KOTLIN.md §2 | The Step 1 scoping table's own note flagged this as "not yet verified... needs a real fixture to confirm `insertNamedConstructBlankLines` produces it as-is or needs extension," pointing at the shared `BlockStructureRule` method. Verified via a standalone harness that `insertNamedConstructBlankLines` (shared, already works for Kotlin's `enum class` for free per Step 0/`classifyNamed`'s existing "keyword before `class` is `enum`" check) only produces the blank line right after the body's opening `{` and right before its closing `}` -- it does *not* insert the blank lines around the entry-list-terminating `;` itself, which is a separate, additional piece of formatting the scoping table's guess had conflated with it. That additional piece already exists, fully implemented and already correct for Kotlin's token shape with zero changes needed to its logic, as `JavaSpecificRule.separateEnumConstantListTerminator` (+ its `findEnumConstantListTerminators`/`isEnumBodyBrace` helpers) -- confirmed by running it directly against Kotlin-tokenized input and getting byte-for-byte the STYLE_KOTLIN.md §2 worked example. That class is per-language, not one of the shared classes listed in this file's "Hard Constraint" table, so a Kotlin call path into it would cross the C/C++/Java-vs-Kotlin file boundary this codebase otherwise keeps strict (and `Formatter.java`'s only caller of it is inside the Java-only pipeline branch, unrelated to Kotlin's not-yet-wired dispatch). Rather than promoting/relocating it into a shared class (a larger, riskier refactor of working Java-only production code, and this class's own other helper names -- `matchBraceForward`/`nextSignificantIndex`/`prevSignificantIndex` -- aren't even present in `BlockStructureRule` today, so the move would drag in more than just this one method), mirrored it as a new `KotlinSpecificRule.separateEnumConstantListTerminator` (+ `findEnumConstantListTerminators`/`isEnumBodyBrace`/`prevSignificantIndex`) instead -- same shape as this session's other §-items that structurally mirror an existing Java/C++ per-language method rather than reusing or refactoring it (§14/RDD_KEY_106 mirroring `CppSpecificRule.enforceRequiresClausePlacement` is the closest precedent). Reused `KotlinSpecificRule`'s own existing `lineIndent` helper (already present from §14's work) in place of duplicating the Java version's separate `lineIndentAt` method, since the two are functionally identical. Verified via a standalone harness (scratchpad only, not committed): the full two-step pipeline (`insertNamedConstructBlankLines` then this new method) reproduces the STYLE_KOTLIN.md §2 worked example byte-for-byte; an enum with no trailing members and no `;` at all is left completely untouched; an enum with a trailing `;` but no members after it (legal, no separation needed) is left completely untouched; and a trailing comma before the `;` is preserved exactly as-is, confirming the two are handled as fully independent details per the style doc's own explicit text. No shared-class change at all (unlike RDD_KEY_109's §17 work) -- the only reused piece was this file's own pre-existing `lineIndent` method. Full C/C++/Java suite 32/32 (untouched, no shared file modified). Not yet wired into `Formatter.formatOne` -- verified standalone only, same caveat as every other Step 3 item so far. |
| RDD_KEY_112 | Kotlin expression-bodied functions — STYLE_KOTLIN.md §9; `: ReturnType`/`= expr` tail wrapping tied to §7/RDD_KEY_104's signature-breaking work | The "preserve as-is" half of §9 was already free (same reasoning as §8's accessor preservation). The new behavior is the three-tier line-length fallback the style doc's worked example spells out: fits fully inline as-is; else break the parameter list first (delegating straight to `KotlinSignatureRule.render`, §7's own method, unchanged); else if that's still too long and the tail is expression-bodied (`= expr` present), wrap `= expr` onto its own line indented one level, mirroring §7.1's named-argument `=`-wrap rather than inventing a new rule. Implemented as new `KotlinSignatureRule.FunctionTail` (a small value class parsing the tokens after a signature's closing `)` into an optional `: ReturnType` and optional `= expr`, stripping the `:`/`=` operator tokens themselves the same way `KotlinParam` already strips them from its own `: type`/`= default` cells), `parseFunctionTail`, and `renderWithTail` (the three-tier method itself), plus a small private `appendTailPart` join helper (a `: ReturnType` cell joins with no space against the preceding `)`, an `= expr` cell joins with one space -- distinguished by the cell's own leading character, since inline/params-broken-but-tail-fits/return-type-half-of-the-wrap-case all share this one join point). An explicit return type with no `=` (an ordinary block-bodied function with `): Int {` and no expression body) is explicitly outside STYLE_KOTLIN.md §9's own documented scope once tier-2 (breaking params) still doesn't fit -- there is nothing left to wrap, so tier 3 is a no-op for that shape and the combined line is used as-is; this was confirmed with a harness case reproducing exactly that shape. Two bugs caught during harness verification (not present in any prior committed code, purely artifacts of writing this method): (1) an unconditional `" "` was being inserted between the closing `)`/broken-params-last-line and the tail even when the tail started with `:` (`) : Int` instead of `): Int`), and a separate case where `= expr` alone (no return type) needed the opposite -- fixed by the `appendTailPart` join helper described above; (2) `lines.set(...)` on an `UnsupportedOperationException` when the params-only portion happened to fit on one line via `render()`'s own bypass check (which returns an immutable `Collections.singletonList`) even though the *combined* line (with tail appended) did not -- fixed by always re-wrapping `render()`'s result in a fresh `new ArrayList<>(...)` before further mutation. A third, more consequential bug was also caught and fixed as a **shared-class change**: `MiscRule.isTightToken` (used by the shared `renderTokens` join-spacing heuristic, called by both this file and `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule`) treated `*`/`&` as always-tight tokens, a C/C++ pointer/reference-declarator convention (`int* p`) — applied unconditionally, this collapsed ordinary Kotlin multiplication spacing in any expression rendered through `renderTokens` (`x * x` was rendering as `x* x`), which is exactly what §9's own tail-expression rendering surfaced (the style doc's own literal worked example, `x * x + y * y`, was failing before this fix). Fixed by gating the `*`/`&` tight-token treatement on `!lang.isKotlin` (Kotlin has no legitimate use of either symbol as a declarator sigil — its `*` is always multiplication or the spread operator, and it has no bare `&` at all), same "no-op for C/C++/Java, Kotlin-only behavior change" posture as RDD_KEY_100/109's prior shared-class changes. Full C/C++/Java suite 32/32 both before and after this change. All six harness cases (fits-inline, the style doc's exact worked example, params-break-but-tail-fits, empty-params-with-only-the-tail-needing-to-wrap, no-return-type/no-`=`, explicit-return-type/no-`=`) verified standalone, not end-to-end (not yet wired into `Formatter.formatOne`, same caveat as every other Step 3 item). |
| RDD_KEY_113 | Kotlin generic variance (`in`/`out`) — STYLE_KOTLIN.md §13; `TokenizerCore.GENERIC_SAFE_KEYWORDS` additive fix | The Step 1 scoping table flagged this as a small additive fix belonging with Step 0 in spirit: `reclassifyAngleBrackets`'s `<`/`>` generic-vs-comparison disambiguation walks the content between a candidate `<`/`>` pair and only accepts it as a generic pair if every token inside is on `GENERIC_SAFE_KEYWORDS`'s (or a few other) allow-lists; `"in"`/`"out"` (Kotlin's declaration-site variance modifiers, `Box<out T>`/`Comparable<in T>`) weren't on it, so `Box<out T>`'s `<`/`>` would have fallen through to plain `OP` tokens (misread as a comparison) rather than `ANGLE_BRACKET_OPEN`/`_CLOSE`. Fixed by adding `"in"`/`"out"` to the shared `GENERIC_SAFE_KEYWORDS` set. Pure no-op for C/C++/Java -- neither keyword exists in any of their `KEYWORDS_*` sets (confirmed by grep before making the change, not assumed), so the `case KEYWORD:` branch that consults this set can never even be reached for those tokens in those languages. Verified via a standalone harness (not committed): `class Box<out T>(...)`, `interface Comparable<in T>`, and `class Pair<in T, out U>` all now correctly tokenize their `<`/`>` as `ANGLE_BRACKET_OPEN`/`_CLOSE`; a plain comparison (`a < b`, `a < b && c > d`) is unaffected, still plain `OP` tokens. Full C/C++/Java suite 32/32 before and after (shared-class change, `TokenizerCore.java`). This is a Step 0/tokenizer-level fix, not a `KotlinSpecificRule` pass -- no rendering/spacing behavior needed beyond correct classification, since nothing else in the pipeline currently treats `in`/`out` specially once they're recognized as ordinary generic-parameter-list content. |
| RDD_KEY_114 | Kotlin function/secondary-constructor body Allman-brace conversion — STYLE_KOTLIN.md §3/§3.3 | Reclassified during the (a)-sweep from "(a), already covered" to "(c), needs new code": `BlockStructureRule.enforceKAndRBraceStyle` (shared) only ever *adds* K&R bracing, never converts an already-K&R function/constructor body brace to Allman -- that direction exists only as `JavaSpecificRule.enforceMethodDefinitionAllmanBraceStyle`/`CppSpecificRule.enforceFunctionDefinitionAllmanBraceStyle`, both per-language, neither with a Kotlin counterpart. New `KotlinSpecificRule.enforceFunctionDefinitionAllmanBraceStyle`, mirroring those two's overall shape (build a `gapToBrace` relocation map, single linear rebuild pass) but with a substantially different, more conservative candidate-detection signal than Java/C++ use. Java/C++ recognize a candidate by "IDENTIFIER before `(`, not preceded by `new`" -- Kotlin has no `new` keyword, and critically, Kotlin's trailing-lambda call syntax (`someCall(args) { ... }`, extremely common) is token-shape-identical to a function definition's own body brace (a `)` directly followed, possibly after a return type, by `{`), with no `new`-style negative signal available to rule ordinary calls out. Guessing past this would misfire constantly. Instead requires unambiguous positive evidence via a backward scan (`isFunctionOrConstructorCloseParen`): starting from the parameter list's name, skip an optional extension-receiver chain (`Type.`/`Type<Args>.`) and an optional `<T>` function type-parameter clause, and the scan must land exactly on the `fun` keyword -- or, for a secondary constructor, the token immediately before `(` must be the `constructor` keyword itself. Any other shape bails (not a candidate), same "give up rather than guess" posture as `KotlinSignatureRule.parseKotlinSignature`'s null return. This scan shape also incidentally excludes a Kotlin enum entry's own anonymous body (`RED(0xFF0000) { override fun toString() = ... }`) for free, with no separate `isEnumConstantBody`-style guard needed (unlike Java's version) -- an enum entry's name is never preceded by `fun`, so it can never satisfy the positive-evidence requirement. A second, non-obvious complication surfaced only via harness testing: a function's own `<T>` type-parameter clause immediately after `fun` (`fun <T> identity(...)`) is *not* reclassified by the tokenizer into `ANGLE_BRACKET_OPEN`/`_CLOSE` tokens the way a normal generic-usage `<T>` (e.g. `List<T>`) is -- confirmed via a token-dump harness that `fun <T> ...` tokenizes `<`/`>` as plain `OP`, since `reclassifyAngleBrackets` doesn't treat the position right after `fun` as a generic-usage-triggering context. The backward-scan helpers (`isAngleClose`/`isAngleOpen`/`skipAngleBracketsBackward`) were written to accept either shape (`ANGLE_BRACKET_CLOSE`/`_OPEN` or plain `OP("<")`/`OP(">")`) uniformly, rather than assuming reclassification always happened, since that's a Kotlin tokenizer quirk this code needs to tolerate rather than fix (fixing it would risk misclassifying a genuine generic-function-clause-adjacent comparison elsewhere; scoping the tolerance to just this backward-scan use site was the smaller, safer change). A third complication: return types. `): String {`/`): T {` shapes put an identifier (the return type) directly before the `{`, not a `)` -- Java/C++ have an analogous "throws clause between `)` and `{`" case handled by preserving the gap's own text and only redirecting the *final* newline-insertion point, and the same technique was mirrored here via a new `findSignatureCloseParenBeforeBrace` helper that scans backward from the last significant token before `{` over a depth-tracked `: ReturnType` span (tracking nested `(`/`[`/`<` from function-type parameters, e.g. `(Int) -> String`) looking for a depth-0 `:` immediately preceded by `)`; the render-time split point is always the last token before `{` (preserving whatever return-type text sits in between), while the found `)` is used purely for the `isFunctionOrConstructorCloseParen` validation check. One-liner bodies (the whole `{ ... }` span on one physical line) are left K&R untouched, same RDD_KEY_75/RDD_KEY_89 exception as Java/C++, implemented here as a simpler `isSingleLineBody` (no-`NEWLINE`-in-span) check with no call-line-breaking-prediction refinement, since no Kotlin equivalent of `MiscRule.enforceCallLineBreaking` exists yet -- if one is added later this method may need the same idempotency refinement Java/C++ already carry. Verified via a standalone harness (scratchpad only, not committed) covering eleven cases: plain function K&R→Allman, secondary constructor K&R→Allman, extension function (`fun String.reverseWords(): String {`) K&R→Allman, generic function (`fun <T> identity(x: T): T {`) K&R→Allman, generic extension function (`fun <T> List<T>.second(): T {`) K&R→Allman, an already-Allman body left untouched (idempotent), a one-liner body left K&R, a trailing-lambda call (`someCall(1, 2) { ... }`) correctly left untouched while its enclosing real function definition still converts (the critical false-positive check), an enum entry's anonymous body correctly left untouched, a control-flow `if` block correctly left untouched (not a candidate shape at all), and a plain function call with no following body correctly left untouched. All eleven matched expected output exactly after two rounds of harness-driven fixes (the return-type-gap handling and the plain-`OP`-angle-bracket tolerance, both described above -- neither was anticipated before running the harness). No shared-class change at all -- fully additive within the already-Kotlin-only `KotlinSpecificRule.java`. Full C/C++/Java suite 32/32 (expected zero-risk given no shared file touched). Not yet wired into `Formatter.formatOne` -- verified standalone only, same caveat as every other Step 3 item so far. |
| RDD_KEY_115 | Kotlin semicolon stripping — STYLE_KOTLIN.md §1; fixed a real bug in the pre-existing implementation | `KotlinSpecificRule.stripOptionalSemicolons` was already implemented and committed in an earlier session (`b0e778f`, predating this session's own RDD-log convention, hence no prior RDD_KEY), but the scoping table still listed §1 as plain "(c)" with no "**done**" marker, so it was re-examined rather than assumed complete -- and doing so surfaced a real, silently-broken-output bug: the old implementation only ever protected the enum entry-list's own mandatory `;` (§2); every *other* `;` in the file was stripped unconditionally, including a `;` deliberately separating two statements kept on one physical line (`val a = 1; val b = 2`) -- STYLE_KOTLIN.md §1's own explicit second "kept" case. Stripping that `;` without also splitting the two statements onto separate lines would have silently merged them into a single invalid/meaning-changing statement (`val a = 1 val b = 2`), not just a style nit. Rewrote the method (keeping the same public method name/intent, `List<Token> -> String` now for consistency with every other method in this file, all of which return rendered `String`, not a `List<Token>`) around a single positive-evidence rule: a `;` is only safe to strip when it is the last significant thing on its physical line (`isTrailingSemicolon` -- next non-gap token, skipping whitespace/comments, either starts a new line or there is none left in the file); anything else (including the multi-statement-same-line case) is left untouched by construction, no special-casing needed. Reused the existing `findEnumConstantListTerminators` (§2/RDD_KEY_111) directly for the enum-mandatory-`;` exclusion rather than re-deriving the old implementation's own separate `enum`/`class`/brace-tracking state machine, keeping "what counts as mandatory" defined in exactly one place. Also fixed a smaller, previously-unnoticed gap: the old implementation left a stray trailing space when a `;` had a preceding space (`foo() ;` -> `foo() ` instead of `foo()`) since it only ever dropped the `;` token itself; the new version also drops any contiguous `WHITESPACE` token(s) immediately preceding a stripped `;`. Verified via a standalone harness (scratchpad only, not committed) covering eight cases: plain flat declarations (stripped); a space-padded `;` (no stray trailing space left); the multi-statement-same-line case (now correctly kept, the bug this session's re-check caught); a trailing line comment after a `;` (still correctly stripped, comment doesn't count as "another statement"); an enum with members after its mandatory `;` (kept); an enum with a trailing `;` but no members after it (correctly stripped, optional per §1's own "only where required" framing, matching RDD_KEY_111's established precedent for that shape); a file with no semicolons at all (untouched); and a `;` at literal end-of-file with no trailing newline (stripped, no `NEWLINE`-token special case needed since `isTrailingSemicolon` already treats "nothing left in the file" as trailing). All eight matched expected output exactly. No shared-class change -- fully additive/replacement within the already-Kotlin-only `KotlinSpecificRule.java`. Full C/C++/Java suite 32/32. Not yet wired into `Formatter.formatOne` -- verified standalone only, same caveat as every other Step 3 item so far. |
| RDD_KEY_116 | Kotlin string template tokenizer risk — STYLE_KOTLIN.md §19; `TokenizerCore.emitString`/`skipKotlinString` Kotlin-only interpolation-aware fix | Step 1 had flagged this as a real correctness risk needing investigation before trusting any Kotlin fixture with string templates, deliberately left uninvestigated pending an actual failing case rather than guessed at: `emitString` (shared, written for C/Java string literals with no interpolation) does a naive scan-to-next-`"` -- for a Kotlin string containing a `${...}` interpolation whose expression itself contains a nested string literal (`"${foo("x")}"`), the nested `"` terminates the token early. Confirmed via a token-dump harness: this input tokenized as three tokens (`"${foo("` / `x` (bare `IDENTIFIER`) / `")}"`) instead of one correctly-bounded `STRING` token. This is a genuine correctness risk, not just cosmetic -- with the string's own quote-boundary lost, a later spacing pass operating on the token stream (any pass that inserts/collapses whitespace based on adjacent token types) could insert whitespace *inside* the string literal's actual text, silently changing the compiled program's runtime string value. Fixed by adding a Kotlin-only path in `emitString`: a new `skipKotlinString` scans for the closing `"` the same way as before but additionally recognizes `${` as opening an interpolation block, handed off to a new `skipKotlinInterpolationBlock` that depth-tracks `{`/`}` (to correctly skip a lambda literal passed inside the interpolation expression, e.g. `"${list.map { it * 2 }}"`) and recursively calls back into `skipKotlinString`/a new `skipKotlinChar` whenever it encounters a nested `"`/`'` inside the block -- so a nested string (with its own `${...}`, arbitrarily deep) or a char literal containing a `"` never desynchronizes the depth count or gets mistaken for a closing delimiter. Bare `$x` interpolation (no braces) needed no special handling at all -- the `$` character introduces no nesting risk by itself, so it already round-trips correctly as ordinary string content with zero code changes. Gated entirely behind `lang.isKotlin`; the non-Kotlin branch is the exact original scan loop, moved into an `else`, untouched. Verified via a standalone harness (scratchpad only, not committed) covering eleven cases, each checked for both correct single-`STRING`-token tokenization and a byte-for-byte token-text-rebuild-equals-original round-trip: bare `$x`; braced `${x}`; the original failing case `${foo("x")}` (nested string); a lambda literal inside the interpolation (`${list.map { it * 2 }}`); two adjacent interpolation blocks (`${a}${b}`); a doubly-nested string-inside-interpolation-inside-string (`"outer ${ "inner ${1+1}" } end"`); an unterminated string (bails at the newline exactly as the original did, no new infinite-loop or off-by-one risk); a plain string with zero interpolation; an escaped `\$` (not treated as interpolation); a char literal containing an escaped `\"` sitting immediately before an interpolation block containing its own char-literal-with-a-`"` (`x.equals('"')`) -- the specific case exercising `skipKotlinChar`; and, separately, a plain C string literal run through the *non*-Kotlin path to confirm that branch is byte-for-byte the original untouched logic. All eleven passed. Full C/C++/Java suite 32/32 before and after (shared-class change, `TokenizerCore.java`, but gated and additive only). Triple-quoted raw strings (`"""..."""`) are a related but explicitly separate and out-of-scope risk, noted but not fixed here -- neither STYLE_KOTLIN.md nor STYLE_KOTLIN2.md mentions them at all (unlike Java's `"""` text block, which is an explicitly documented, gated, and implemented feature via `isJavaTextBlockOpen`/`emitJavaTextBlock`), and they have different lexical rules entirely (no backslash-escape processing, terminated by 3+ `"`, can contain unescaped `"` freely) that would need their own dedicated design rather than an extension of this fix -- flagged as a new item in the scoping table rather than silently left for a future session to rediscover. |
| RDD_KEY_117 | Kotlin triple-quoted raw string tokenizer support — STATE_KOTLIN.md row 19.1, surfaced during RDD_KEY_116's §19 investigation and explicitly investigated on user request | Neither STYLE_KOTLIN.md nor STYLE_KOTLIN2.md mentions raw strings (`"""..."""`) at all, unlike Java's `"""` text block (an explicitly documented, gated, implemented feature via `isTextBlockOpener`/`emitTextBlock`, `lang.isJava`-only). Confirmed via harness that this was badly broken, not merely unhandled: `"""hello "world" end"""` mis-lexed as five tokens (`""` / `"hello "` / a bare `IDENTIFIER world` / `" end"` / `""`) instead of one, and a multi-line raw string mis-lexed a spurious `NEWLINE` token into the middle of what should be one opaque string -- exposing the content's real newlines to every indentation/scope pass in the pipeline, exactly the corruption risk an unrecognized text block would cause. Fixed with a Kotlin-only path: `isKotlinRawStringOpener` (`lang.isKotlin` + `"""` lookahead) dispatches to a new `emitKotlinRawString`/`skipKotlinRawString`, checked in the tokenizer's main dispatch before the existing plain-`"` and C/C++ raw-string-prefix branches. Two rules distinguish this scan from every other string helper in the file: (1) no backslash-escape processing at all -- `\` is a literal character inside a Kotlin raw string, by design, so a lone trailing `\` right before the closing `"""` must not swallow the first delimiter quote the way an escape pair would elsewhere; (2) termination is greedy on the first `"""` encountered, matching the real Kotlin compiler exactly -- content may freely contain runs of one or two unescaped `"` (e.g. `"""hello "world" end"""`), but the moment three land in a row that's the close, even if a fourth immediately follows (four trailing quotes closes at the first three, leaving one stray `"` behind as its own token -- matches real Kotlin semantics; a literal quote right at the very end needs `${'"'}` in real source, not something this tokenizer needs to solve). `${...}` interpolation is still recognized and depth-tracked via the existing `skipKotlinInterpolationBlock` (RDD_KEY_116) exactly as for plain strings, extended with one addition: `skipKotlinInterpolationBlock` now checks for a `"""` lookahead before falling back to `skipKotlinString`, so a nested raw string inside an interpolation expression (`"${"""nested"""}"`) is recognized and skipped correctly too, rather than being misread as an ordinary string with two stray quotes. Verified via a 14-case harness (each checked for a single correctly-bounded `STRING` token -- or the expected count for the deliberate 4-trailing-quotes edge case -- and a byte-for-byte token-text round-trip): plain single-line raw string; embedded single/double `"` runs as plain content; multi-line with an embedded literal newline; a literal backslash inside (confirming no escape processing); bare `${name}` interpolation; interpolation containing a nested ordinary string (`${if (x) "a" else "b"}`); interpolation containing a nested raw string (`${"""nested"""}`); four trailing quotes (closes at first three, one stray-quote token left over, matching real Kotlin); an unterminated raw string (bails at EOF, no crash); an ordinary non-raw Kotlin string (unaffected baseline); interpolation containing a nested ordinary string with its own nested quote (`${foo("x")}`, exercising both fixes together); plus three non-Kotlin sanity checks confirming Java's `"""` text block, a C++ raw string literal, and a plain C string are all still handled exactly as before (zero shared-behavior change). Full C/C++/Java suite 32/32 before and after. |
| RDD_KEY_118 | Kotlin import-ordering implementation — STATE_KOTLIN.md Step 3.5 "Implementation" item, per STYLE_KOTLIN.md §24 (spec already written) | Added `enforceKotlinImportOrdering` to `KotlinSpecificRule.java`, mirroring `JavaSpecificRule.enforceImportOrdering`'s overall structure (per-language mirroring, not shared-class reuse -- same precedent as RDD_KEY_106/111/114 and every other Kotlin-only method in this file) but adapted for two Kotlin-specific differences: (1) no `static` bucket -- `KOTLIN_IMPORT_GROUP_KEYS` is `{kotlin, java, com, org, other, local}` instead of Java's six-with-`static`, since Kotlin has no `import static` keyword (a companion-object-member or top-level-function import uses the exact same `import a.b.c` syntax as any other import, so "this is a static import" isn't lexically detectable); classification priority is local > kotlin > java/javax > org > com > other. (2) an import statement's own end is whichever comes first -- an optional `;`, or NEWLINE/EOF -- never a required `;`, since Kotlin imports are conventionally not `;`-terminated (STYLE_KOTLIN.md §1 strips the optional trailing `;` elsewhere in the pipeline); a tolerated trailing `;` is simply dropped on regeneration, never preserved. New `parseKotlinImportStatement` also recognizes an optional `as Alias` suffix (`as` lexes as `TokenType.KEYWORD` for Kotlin, confirmed via `TokenizerCore.KEYWORDS_KOTLIN`, not a plain identifier) -- aliased and wildcard imports sort/group by their pre-alias/pre-wildcard path per §24's own text, with the `as Alias` suffix re-emitted verbatim on regeneration. Reused two already-existing helpers verbatim by call-site convention: `hasCommentBetween(tokens, fromExclusive, toExclusive)` and `anyFrozen(tokens, fromInclusive, toExclusive)`. Added three new private helpers mirroring Java's exactly since they didn't exist yet in this file: `appendRange`, `joinVerbatim`, `isPathOp` (the last covers `TokenizerCore`'s combined `.*` multi-char OP token for wildcard imports, same discovery as `JavaSpecificRule.isPathOp`'s own doc comment). Added `findLocalPackagePrefix` (reads the first `package a.b.c` declaration's top `importDepth` components, same mechanism as Java's `findLocalPrefix`, adapted for Kotlin's optional-`;` termination) and `classifyKotlinImportGroup`/`matchesPrefix`. `groupOrder` permutation validation is a config-validation precondition (throws `IllegalArgumentException` on a non-permutation), not a per-file content-shape judgment call -- same posture as Java. Added `kotlin-import-order`/`kotlin-import-sort`/`kotlin-import-depth`/`kotlin-import-blank-lines` to `Config.java`'s known-keys list, fields, getters, and `fromRawMap` parsing, mirroring the existing `java-import-*` keys exactly (default group order `kotlin, java, com, org, other, local`, matching §24's documented default). Not yet wired into `Formatter.formatOne` -- verified via a standalone 10-case scratch harness only (default grouping/sorting/regrouping; wildcard import exercising the combined `.*` OP token; aliased import sorting/grouping by pre-alias path; optional trailing `;` tolerated and dropped; custom group order + custom blank-line count; `sortAlphabetically = false` preserving original relative order; zero-imports no-op; a comment between two imports blocking the entire pass back to a byte-for-byte no-op; no `package` declaration present (local bucket simply never populated); an invalid `groupOrder` throwing `IllegalArgumentException`) -- all 10 passed. Full C/C++/Java suite 32/32 before and after (Kotlin-only change, no shared-class touch). |
| RDD_KEY_119 | Kotlin primary-constructor classes/`enum class` losing their named-construct closing comment + blank lines — Step 4 known-bugs punch list item 1 (`test/kt_combined_inp.kt` `enum class Status(val code: Int) { ... }`) — **shared-class fix**, `TokenizerCore.java` | Confirmed via a minimal-reproduction harness (`class Big { fun a() {...} }` correctly got both `} // class Big` and its blank-line, but `class Big(val id: Int) { fun a() {...} }` — identical body, only a primary-constructor parameter list added — got neither) that this wasn't an enum-specific or closing-comment-min-lines-threshold issue at all (as STATE_KOTLIN.md's own starting map had guessed), but a tokenizer-level named-construct-name loss affecting any Kotlin `class`/`enum class` with a primary constructor. Root cause: `TokenizerCore`'s per-token dispatch has a branch — `t.type == PUNCT && "(".equals(t.text) && t.parenDepth == 1` — that clears `pendingNamedConstructName` on entering any outermost paren group, with the stated purpose (per its own comment) of preventing a function body's `{` from picking up a surrounding class/struct name. For C/C++/Java this is safe: by the time a member function's own `(` is reached inside a class body, `pendingNamedConstructName` has already been consumed (reset to null) by the class's own `{`, so the clear is a no-op there. But a Kotlin primary constructor's parameter list (`class Foo(val x: Int) {`) has the exact same keyword-IDENTIFIER-`(` token shape while the `(` sits *between* the just-armed construct name and its own body brace, not after a separate function's name — so the same clear wrongly wipes the name before `emitOpenBrace` ever gets to consume it, leaving that `{` nameless: no closing comment (`decideComment`'s `NAMED` case never fires, falls to `default: return null`) and no name-driven blank lines (`insertNamedConstructBlankLines`, which also keys off the same name). This is structurally the same category of problem Java's `record Name(...)` component list already solves — but `record` survives via its own separate `pendingRecordName` field (armed by `computeRecordHeaderName`, never touching `pendingNamedConstructName`), which Kotlin classes have no equivalent of. Fixed by gating the clear-on-`(` branch off for Kotlin (`&& !lang.isKotlin`) rather than adding a parallel `pendingKotlinConstructorName` field, since Kotlin's primary-constructor paren immediately follows the armed name with nothing in between that could otherwise need clearing (unlike the C/C++/Java shape the original clear defends against, which never arises for Kotlin's `NAMED_CONSTRUCT_KOTLIN` keyword set — `fun` isn't a named-construct keyword, so a member function's own parameter list was never at risk of picking up a stale class name in the first place). Verified via harness: `class Big(val id: Int) { ... }`, `enum class Status(val code: Int) { ... }` (the exact fixture shape), and a `constructor`-keyword secondary-constructor variant all now correctly get their closing comment + blank lines; a plain `class Big { ... }` with no primary constructor (already working before this fix) is unaffected. `make test` 32/32 before and after. |
| RDD_KEY_120 | Kotlin `for(n in numbers) { total += n }` not collapsing to `for(n in numbers) total += n` — Step 4 known-bugs punch list item 2 — **shared-class fix**, `BlockStructureRule.tryCollapse` | STATE_KOTLIN.md's own §8/RDD_KEY_112 note had already recorded that `SINGLE_EXPR_KEYWORDS` includes `for`, so the keyword set was never the problem — confirmed via harness that `tryCollapse` (the method that actually decides whether a braced body qualifies for the STYLE.md §10 single-statement omission) was the real blocker: it counts top-level `;` in the body and requires `semiCount == 1`, unconditionally, for any language. Kotlin has no mandatory statement-terminating `;` (STYLE_KOTLIN.md §1) — a single-statement Kotlin body like `{ total += n }` has `semiCount == 0` and was therefore always rejected (`return null`) regardless of how simple the body was, a total blind spot for Kotlin rather than a threshold/edge-case bug. Fixed by adding a Kotlin-only branch alongside the existing `semiCount == 1` path: when `semiCount == 0 && lang.isKotlin`, a new `isKotlinSingleStatementBody` helper re-derives "exactly one statement" from newline boundaries instead of `;` boundaries — walks the body's token span tracking `(`/`[`/`{` nesting depth, treats a depth-0 `NEWLINE` after some content has already been seen as a statement boundary, and rejects (returns false) if any further significant token follows that boundary (multi-statement body) or if any comment token appears anywhere in the body (same conservative "don't guess past a comment" posture as the existing `;`-based path's own comment checks, reused verbatim for the `semiCount == 1` case, not touched). A `NEWLINE` inside an unclosed paren/bracket/brace (e.g. a multi-line call argument as the lone statement) does not count as a boundary. Verified via the `test/kt_combined_inp.kt` fixture's own `for(n in numbers) { total += n }` (now collapses correctly) plus a multi-statement-body negative case (two statements on separate lines inside the braces, correctly left unbraced-uncollapsed) and a body containing a trailing comment (correctly left untouched, conservative bail). Also surfaced, but explicitly left unfixed as out-of-scope for this bug: a pre-existing, fully independent indentation bug in `ScopePipeline`'s Kotlin declaration-alignment splice logic (`addKotlinDeclReplacement`) that double-indents a `val`/`var` declaration when it is the very first statement inside a Kotlin function/class body (confirmed via bisection to predate this session's changes entirely, reproduces with zero interaction with either RDD_KEY_119 or this fix, e.g. `fun sumAll(...) { var total = 0 ... }` renders `var total` at 8 spaces instead of 4) — flagged as a new Open Question below rather than folded into this commit. `make test` 32/32 before and after (shared-class change, additive branch only, C/C++/Java's `;`-based path byte-for-byte untouched). |
| RDD_KEY_121 | Kotlin `val label = when (status) { ... }` mangled to one squished line (branches concatenated with no newlines, indentation lost) — Step 4 known-bugs punch list item 3 — **Kotlin-only fix**, `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule.parseKotlinDeclaration` | STATE_KOTLIN.md's own RDD_KEY_101 note had guessed the culprit was `KotlinSpecificRule.formatWhenExpressions` itself (Phase 4) interacting badly with an earlier blank-line-collapsing pass. Root-caused instead via temporary `System.err` debug prints added directly in `Formatter.formatOne` (env-var-gated, `JXM_DEBUG`) after each early pipeline phase, run against both a minimal isolated repro and the real fixture: the squishing was already fully present in the text coming *out of Phase 0's `ScopePipeline.process` call* — before `collapseSingleExpressionBlocks`, before `formatWhenExpressions` ever ran. Traced from there to `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule.groupPropertyDeclarations`/`parseKotlinDeclaration` (invoked from `ScopePipeline.applyKotlinDeclarationsPass`, STYLE_KOTLIN.md §6 property/local `val`/`var` alignment): `splitKotlinStatements` correctly keeps a multi-line `when(...) { ... }` initializer as one statement (its brace-depth tracking treats `{` like `(`/`[`, so depth-0-newline splitting never fires inside it), but `parseKotlinDeclaration` then builds `initTokens` from `significantOnly(stmt)` — which strips every `NEWLINE`/`WHITESPACE` token — losing the initializer's internal line-break structure entirely. That `KotlinDecl` is later rendered by `renderPropertyGroup`/`renderKotlinTokens` (the shared single-line-init renderer used for every declaration in an alignment group), which joins `initTokens` with normalized single-space gaps onto one physical line — correct for the short one-line inits every §6 worked example shows, but silently collapsing an arbitrary multi-line block-expression initializer (`when`, an `if`/`else` used as an expression, a multi-line lambda, etc.) onto one line, corrupting the input before any later phase (including `formatWhenExpressions`, whose branch-finding relies on the source's own newlines) ever sees it. Fixed by adding a `spansMultipleLines(stmt, afterToken)` check right after locating the declaration's `=` token in `parseKotlinDeclaration`: scans the *raw* (unfiltered) `stmt` token list, by object identity from the `=` token onward, for any `NEWLINE`; if found, `parseKotlinDeclaration` returns `null` for that statement (same "don't guess past an unrecognized shape" bailout already used for every other unparseable shape in this method), which `groupPropertyDeclarations` treats as a group-breaking non-declaration — the declaration is left out of the alignment group entirely and rendered verbatim by whatever later phase would otherwise touch it, its internal line structure intact for `formatWhenExpressions` to correctly re-flow. Verified via harness: the isolated `val label = when (status) { ... }` repro now renders with branches on separate lines, correct indentation, and the closing `} // when status` comment/arrow-alignment all applied correctly by `formatWhenExpressions` as designed; a same-shape *single-line* init (`val x = foo(1, 2)`, no `NEWLINE` in its span) is confirmed unaffected, still grouped/aligned normally. Against the real `test/kt_combined_inp.kt` fixture, the `when (status) { ... }` block's diff against `test/kt_combined_out.kt` is now fully resolved (zero lines of diff in that region). `make test` 32/32 before and after (Kotlin-only file, no shared-class touch — `DeclarationAlignmentRule`'s base-class C/C++/Java logic untouched). Temporary `JXM_DEBUG` debug prints used for root-causing were removed from `Formatter.java` before this commit; none remain in the shipped code. |
| RDD_KEY_122 | Kotlin property accessor body (`set(value) { field = value }`) closing `}` under-indented by one level — surfaced re-triaging the "double-indentation" item from the Step 4 punch list, after discovering that item was a false positive (see below) — **shared-class fix**, `ScopePipeline.findParentIndent` | While re-investigating the previously-reported "first-statement double-indentation" bug (originally surfaced during RDD_KEY_120), root-caused it as a pure test-harness artifact, **not a real formatter bug**: a stray `/tmp/kt_test/.jxmake-code-formatter` config file left over from earlier ad-hoc testing in this session had `indent-size=8` (and `closing-comment-min-lines=1`), and the formatter's config discovery walks up from the file being formatted, so every fixture copied into a subdirectory of `/tmp/kt_test` silently inherited `indent-size=8` instead of the real default of 4 -- `normalizeIndent` was correctly rounding a real 4-space indent up to the nearest multiple of the (wrongly-8) configured width, doubling it. Deleted the stray config, re-ran the isolated repro and the full `kt_combined_inp.kt` diff with a clean `/tmp` directory, and the double-indentation symptom is completely gone with default settings -- confirmed via debug prints (`addKotlinDeclReplacement`'s own `miscRule.indentWidth` logged as 8, not the expected 4) before concluding this. The originally-reported item 4 ("nested `for` loop getting a spurious `} // for` closing comment") also stopped reproducing once the stray config was removed, most likely the same `closing-comment-min-lines=1` half of that same config file lowering the threshold below its real default -- also concluded to be a test-harness artifact, not a formatter bug, no code change needed or made for either. With the artifact-free baseline established, a *different*, real, previously-masked bug was found in the same area: `var count: Int = 0` followed by a `set(value) { field = value }` accessor on the next line renders with its closing `}` indented one level too shallow (4 spaces instead of 8). Root cause: `ScopePipeline.findParentIndent`'s backward scan for a span's owning statement stops only at a top-level `;`, `}`, or `case`/`default` label colon -- all C/Java-shaped statement terminators. A Kotlin property declaration like `var count: Int = 0` is conventionally newline-terminated with no `;` (STYLE_KOTLIN.md §1), invisible to that scan, so searching backward from `set(value) {`'s own anchor walks straight past the newline separating it from the property declaration line above (no `;` to stop it) and lands on the property declaration's own first token (`var`) as the anchor instead of `set` -- `findParentIndent` then returns the shallower property-line indent instead of the accessor's own deeper one. Fixed by adding a narrowly-scoped Kotlin-only rule to the same backward scan: a depth-0 `NEWLINE` immediately followed (skipping gap tokens) by the `get`/`set` soft keyword is now also treated as a statement boundary. Deliberately scoped to specifically `get`/`set` rather than "any Kotlin newline-terminated statement" in general, since a broader rule would also misfire on legitimate multi-line fluent call chains (`foo()\n.bar()\n.baz { }`) where a leading `.`/`?.` on the continuation line means the newline is *not* a statement boundary -- no such shape found in any fixture, left unaddressed rather than guessed at. Verified via harness: `set(value) { field = value }` immediately after a `;`-less `var`/`val` line now gets the correct one-level-deeper indent on both its own line and its closing `}`; also checked with a `get()` accessor present above `set` (no change needed there, `get() = field` is a single-line expression body with no `{}` of its own). Against the real `test/kt_combined_inp.kt` fixture, the `set(value) { ... }` block's diff against `test/kt_combined_out.kt` is now fully resolved. `make test` 32/32 before and after (shared-class change, gated to `lang.isKotlin`, C/C++/Java's existing `;`/`}`/label-colon scan untouched). |
| RDD_KEY_123 | Kotlin trailing-lambda call missing a space before its `{` inside a declaration initializer (`x?.let{ it + 1 }` should be `x?.let { it + 1 }`) — Step 4 known-bugs punch list item 2 (part 1 of 2), against `test/kt_combined_inp.kt`'s `val safe = x?.let { it + 1 } ?: 0` | Root cause: `DeclarationAlignmentRule.needsSpaceBetween` (the shared spacing rule `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule.renderKotlinTokens`/`renderTokens` reuses for rendering a declaration's initializer tokens) has a C/C++/Java-only rule that suppresses the space before a `{` directly following an identifier -- meant for a brace-initializer (`int arr[] = {1,2,3}`, C++ uniform-init `Widget w{}`), never a legitimate Kotlin shape (Kotlin has no aggregate-initializer syntax at all; its only identifier-then-`{` construct is a trailing lambda call, e.g. `.let { ... }`, which STYLE_KOTLIN.md's own worked examples always show with a space before the `{`). Fixed by gating that branch with `&& !lang.isKotlin`. Verified via harness (`val safe = x?.let { it + 1 } ?: 0` now round-trips with the space intact) and against the real fixture (this specific spacing mismatch is now resolved in the `kt_combined_inp.kt` diff). `make test` 32/32 before and after (shared-class change, gated, C/C++/Java's brace-initializer tight-spacing untouched). **Not fixed, flagged as ambiguous rather than guessed at (see STATE_KOTLIN.md Open Questions):** the *other* half of this punch-list item -- `kt_combined_out.kt` shows `val safe   = ...` column-aligned with the very next line's `val (a, b) = Pair(1, 2)` (a destructuring declaration), which would require merging `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule`'s ordinary-declaration and destructuring-declaration alignment groups into one shared column grid. This directly contradicts RDD_KEY_107's own documented design decision ("own group stream, never merged with §6's") -- either the fixture's alignment is a typo/inconsistent with the already-implemented spec, or the spec decision itself needs revisiting; not something to guess at unilaterally. |
| RDD_KEY_124 | Kotlin braceless multi-line `if(...)`/`while(...)`/`for(...)` body (`if(i <= 5)\n    return@forEach`) never collapses to one line (`if(i <= 5) return@forEach`) — Step 4 known-bugs punch list item 3 (`findFirst`/`findFirstX`/`test()`), against `test/kt_combined_inp.kt` | Root cause: `BlockStructureRule.collapseSingleExpressionBlocks`'s main loop only ever handled two shapes for a recognized `ControlBlock` -- a braced body (`block.openBraceIndex >= 0`, tries `tryCollapse`) or nothing at all. A body that starts life *already brace-free* (`block.openBraceIndex == -1`) fell through with no action whatsoever -- a complete blind spot, not a threshold/edge-case bug, because C/C++/Java source can never legally produce a multi-line braceless body as *input* (their grammar requires `{}` for anything but a single already-one-line statement), so this shape simply never arose before Kotlin. Fixed by adding a new `lang.isKotlin`-gated `else if (block.openBraceIndex < 0)` branch alongside the existing braced-body branch, calling two new helper methods: `tryCollapseBraceless` (scans forward from the condition's `)`, depth-tracking `(`/`[`/`{` nesting, treating a depth-0 `NEWLINE`-after-content or a depth-0 `}` as the single statement's end; bails out -- returns `null`, no rewrite -- on any comment token, an empty body, or a body whose first token is itself a compound-body-introducing keyword per the existing `COMPOUND_BODY_KEYWORDS` set; otherwise renders `prefix + " " + body` via the pre-existing `renderInline` helper) and `isPartOfElseChainBraceless` (mirrors the existing `isPartOfElseChain` guard, checking only whether the keyword itself is immediately preceded by `else`, deliberately not attempting to detect or preserve a *following* `else` on the next line -- no such shape found in any fixture, left unaddressed rather than guessed at). Verified via harness against `findFirst`/`findFirstX`/`test()` in the real `kt_combined_inp.kt` fixture: all four `if(...)`/`return@X`-shaped braceless multi-line bodies now correctly collapse to one line, diff against `kt_combined_out.kt` for those lines is fully resolved. `make test` 32/32 before and after (Kotlin-gated branch, C/C++/Java's existing braced-body path byte-for-byte untouched). **Explicitly out of scope, not attempted:** a bare `else\n    stmt` (no `if`/condition of its own) is not handled by this fix at all -- `collapseSingleExpressionBlocks`'s loop is keyed off `if`/`while`/`for`, never triggers on a standalone `else` keyword -- so `kt_combined_out.kt`'s `else               it.toInt()` (single-line, padding-aligned with the preceding `if(...)  0` branch) remains unresolved; this looks like a related but distinct construct-plus-alignment gap, not guessed at here. |
| RDD_KEY_125 | Kotlin function with an explicit return type (`fun sumAll(...): Int { ... }`) never gets STYLE.md §9's blank-line-before-`return` — reproduces with or without the for-loop collapse from RDD_KEY_120/124, i.e. a pre-existing, fully independent gap surfaced while re-verifying the full fixture diff after RDD_KEY_124 — **shared-class fix**, `MiscRule.isFunctionBodyBrace` | Root cause: `isFunctionBodyBrace`'s C/Java-shaped check requires `)` to directly precede the function's `{` (skipping known qualifiers), with one more fallback for C++'s trailing `-> ReturnType {` shape (`findCloseParenBeforeTrailingReturnType`, scanning for `->`). Kotlin's own explicit-return-type shape (`fun foo(): ReturnType {`) matches neither -- it's a third, `:`-prefixed shape unrecognized by either existing check -- so `isFunctionBodyBrace` always returned `false` for any such Kotlin function, silently disabling `insertBlankLineBeforeReturn` for it entirely (a Kotlin function with an *inferred/omitted* return type has no `:` at all and was already unaffected, working correctly). First fix attempt added a Kotlin-only `findCloseParenBeforeKotlinReturnType` call (`:`-scanning analog of the `->`-scanning method) but placed it *after* the existing unconditional `->`-scan -- that scan, given Kotlin input with no `->` anywhere in the signature, runs off the front of the token stream and returns -1, clobbering `closeParen` to -1 before the new Kotlin branch's own `closeParen >= 0` guard ever gets a chance to see the original, still-valid value -- confirmed via a minimal isolated repro (`fun sumAll(...): Int { ... total; return total }`) still failing to reproduce the fix after the first attempt, before finding this ordering bug. Fixed by gating the pre-existing `->`-scan itself with `!lang.isKotlin` (Kotlin never produces `->` there, so skipping it outright for Kotlin is a pure no-op change for that language, and stops it from destroying `closeParen` before the new Kotlin check runs). Second bug found in the same pass: the new `findCloseParenBeforeKotlinReturnType` initially checked for the return-type colon with `isPunct(t, ":")`, but `:` is tokenized as `TokenType.OP`, not `PUNCT` (confirmed via grep across every other `:`-detecting call site in the codebase, all of which use `isOp(t, ":")`) -- `isPunct` never matched, so the method always fell through to its `NEWLINE`/`{`/`}` bail case regardless of input, silently never firing. Fixed by switching to `isOp(t, ":")`, matching the codebase's own established convention for this token. Verified via harness: `fun sumAll(vararg numbers: Int): Int { var total = 0; total += 5; return total }` now correctly gets a blank line inserted before `return total`; a same-shape function with an inferred return type (no `:` at all) confirmed unaffected (already worked, still works). Against the real `kt_combined_inp.kt` fixture, all four previously-missing blank-line-before-`return` diffs are now resolved. `make test` 32/32 before and after (Kotlin-only guard additions to a shared method, C/C++/Java's existing `)`-direct and `->`-trailing-return-type paths behaviorally untouched for their own languages). |
| RDD_KEY_126 | **REVISES RDD_KEY_107**: user request to merge Kotlin destructuring-declaration alignment (§12) into the same column-aligned group stream as an adjacent plain `val`/`var` declaration (§6), rather than keeping them in two entirely separate group streams as RDD_KEY_107 originally decided — precedent cited: this codebase's own C++ structured-bindings support already aligns `auto [b, c] = somePair;` in the same group as a preceding `int a = xxx;`, so `val safe = ...` / `val (a, b) = ...` should do the same | User's stated preference, in priority order: (1) full alignment — both the `val`/`var` keyword column and the name-start column align across the two lines (`val  safe  = ...` / `val (a, b) = ...`, treating the parenthesized name as one opaque name-cell); (2) fallback if (1) proved to be a large restructure — `=`-column-only alignment. Implemented (1), the fuller form, since it did not require a large restructure. Added a new `Row` model to `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule` that generalizes both existing per-line shapes into one: `nameCell` holds either a plain identifier's text (from a `KotlinDecl`) or a destructuring declaration's already-comma-normalized `"(a, b)"` text (from a `DestructuringDecl`) — since both are ultimately just strings occupying the same name column, unifying them into one `Row.nameCell` field means `renderAlignedGroup`'s `ColumnGrid`-based rendering needs no per-row-shape branching at all, it treats every row identically. Added `groupAlignableDeclarations` (replaces `groupPropertyDeclarations`/`groupDestructuringDeclarations` as the pass `ScopePipeline` actually calls, though both old methods are kept, marked `@deprecated`, since `parseKotlinDeclaration`/`parseDestructuringDeclaration` are still reused internally): for each statement, tries `parseKotlinDeclaration` first, then `parseDestructuringDeclaration` if that returns null (mutually exclusive grammars — `parseDestructuringDeclaration` requires a `(` immediately after `val`/`var` where `parseKotlinDeclaration` requires a plain identifier, so try-order between them is irrelevant to correctness), wrapping whichever succeeds in a `Row` via a small `toRow(KotlinDecl)`/`toRow(DestructuringDecl)` overload pair; a statement matching neither breaks the current group exactly as before. Added `renderAlignedGroup`, the merged replacement for `renderPropertyGroup`/`renderDestructuringGroup`, sharing one modifier-column model (`KotlinModifierPriority`) and one name column across both row shapes; a destructuring row's type column is always blank (§12 destructuring lists have no type-annotation column per RDD_KEY_107's own original finding) but participates in the shared `anyType`/`anyInit`-driven column-presence logic exactly like a §6 row with no explicit type would. `ScopePipeline.applyKotlinDeclarationsPass` simplified from two separate loops (one per group-stream type, each producing its own splice) into one loop over `groupAlignableDeclarations`'s merged groups, spliced via each `Row` group's `first.firstAnchor`/`last.lastAnchor` — `addKotlinDeclReplacement`'s own signature was untouched, it already took anchors + lines generically. One self-caught design correction during implementation: `Row` was initially made `private` with wrapper methods (`firstAnchorOf`/`lastAnchorOf`/`renderAlignableGroup(tokens, group)`) to avoid exposing it outside `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule`'s package — recognized this was unworkable since `ScopePipeline` (a different package) needs to hold and pass `List<Row>` directly between `groupAlignableDeclarations`'s return and `renderAlignedGroup`'s parameter; fixed by making `Row` `public` with `public` fields and removing the wrappers. Verified via `make all` (silent success), full regression suite (`make test` 32/32, zero regressions), and a real-fixture diff against `test/kt_combined_out.kt` (fresh copy in `/tmp/kt_test/merge1/`): the `val safe = x?.let { it + 1 } ?: 0` / `val (a, b) = Pair(1, 2)` line pair, previously flagged as an open/ambiguous mismatch under RDD_KEY_123, is now fully resolved in the diff — both the `val` keyword column and the name-start column align exactly as the user's preferred first form requested (`val safe   = x?.let { it + 1 } ?: 0` / `val (a, b) = Pair(1, 2)`, verified byte-for-byte via `sed`). |
| RDD_KEY_127 | Bare Kotlin `else\n    stmt` (no `if`/condition of its own) never collapses to one line — distinct gap from RDD_KEY_124, which only ever triggers on `if`/`while`/`for`'s own `(...)` condition and never on a standalone `else` keyword; reproduces in `test()`'s second `let` block in `test/kt_combined_inp.kt` (`if (it.isEmpty())\n    0\nelse\n    it.toInt()`) | Root cause: `collapseSingleExpressionBlocks`'s main loop only ever inspects `KEYWORD` tokens in `SINGLE_EXPR_KEYWORDS` (`if`/`while`/`for`); a bare `else` is never a member of that set and has no `(...)` condition to anchor a `ControlBlock` match on in the first place, so it fell through completely untouched, structurally identical to RDD_KEY_124's blind spot but for a keyword RDD_KEY_124 never covers. Fixed by extracting `tryCollapseBraceless`'s body-scanning/rendering core (depth-tracked forward scan for a depth-0 `NEWLINE`-after-content or depth-0 `}`, single-statement/no-comment/no-nested-compound-body checks, `renderInline` rendering) into a new shared `collapseBracelessBody(tokens, fromIndex, prefix, outBodyEnd)` helper that takes an arbitrary start index and a pre-rendered prefix string, rather than always deriving both from an `if`/`while`/`for`'s `ControlBlock`; `tryCollapseBraceless` itself now just renders its own `if(...)`/`while(...)`/`for(...)` prefix and delegates to the shared helper. Added a new `else if (t.type == TokenType.KEYWORD && "else".equals(t.text) && lang.isKotlin)` branch to the main loop: skips (leaves untouched) an `else if` (still an `if`, handled when the loop reaches that token on its own) and an already-braced `else { ... }` (unrelated pre-existing gap, out of scope — braced `else` was never collapsed by this pass for any language, C/Java included, not something this fix touches), otherwise calls `collapseBracelessBody(tokens, elseKwIndex + 1, "else", bodyEnd)` and splices in the result on success. **Evidence-over-reasoning scope limit, explicitly not attempted:** `kt_combined_out.kt`'s expected output additionally column-pads the collapsed `else`'s body to align with the *preceding* `if` branch's own collapsed body at the same character column (`if(...) 0` / `else               it.toInt()`) — this fix only produces the plain single-space-joined `else it.toInt()`, structurally collapsed but not column-padded. No STYLE_KOTLIN.md/STYLE_KOTLIN2.md worked example covers this specific if/else single-expression-body column-alignment shape (the only "else"-alignment example in either doc is `when`-expression arrow alignment, a different construct), and one fixture occurrence is not enough evidence to safely derive a general trigger condition (would it apply between any two adjacent single-line-collapsed branches regardless of intervening code shape? only for `if`/`else` specifically, not `when`? triggered by any padding difference, or only up to some threshold?) — flagged as an open question (see `STATE_KOTLIN.md`) rather than guessed at, consistent with RDD_KEY_123's own precedent (fixed the space, flagged the alignment). Verified via `make all` (silent success), full regression suite (`make test` 32/32, zero regressions), and a real-fixture diff against `test/kt_combined_out.kt` (`/tmp/kt_test/merge2/`): both the `if(...) 0` and `else it.toInt()` lines are now correctly collapsed to one line each (previously two separate multi-line diff hunks); the only remaining diff on that line is the still-open column-padding gap noted above. |
| RDD_KEY_128 | The column-padding half of RDD_KEY_127's own open question, now **confirmed wanted** by the user (`kt_combined_inp.kt`/`kt_combined_out.kt` enabled in the `Makefile`'s `INP_FILES`, exposing this as the sole remaining `make test` failure): a collapsed single-line `else` body should be space-padded to start at the same column as its immediately preceding single-line `if(...)` branch's own body (`if( it.isEmpty() ) 0` / `else               it.toInt()`) | First attempt (reverted, not committed): threaded a `padWidth` computed at `collapseSingleExpressionBlocks`' own collapse time (the `if` line's just-rendered prefix width) through to the `else` line's own collapse call. Root-caused via `System.err.println` debug prints added temporarily to `BlockStructureRule`/`Formatter.java` (removed before this fix's final commit) against the real `kt_combined_inp.kt` fixture: this was stale by exactly one column for `it.isEmpty()`'s *second* occurrence (the `result2` block) — at `collapseSingleExpressionBlocks`' own point in `Formatter.formatOne`'s pipeline (Phase 1, line ~81), that `if` condition was still `if (it.isEmpty())` (untight, 19 chars including the space after `if`); `MiscRule.enforceComplexityPadding` (Phase 0/Phase 1-again, STYLE.md §3.1's tight/loose paren logic) only tightens it to `if( it.isEmpty() )` (18 chars) in a *later* pass that runs after the collapse. The *first* occurrence (the `result1` block, whose `if` body is a `return@parse` rather than a plain expression) happened to already be tight at collapse time in this same file — coincidental, not reliable — so an isolated single-function repro (which didn't reproduce a second, non-tight occurrence) initially looked correct before the real fixture exposed the staleness. Reverted that whole approach (`git checkout` on `BlockStructureRule.java`, back to its clean RDD_KEY_127 state) rather than patching around the staleness in place, since the root problem — computing a final rendered width before every width-affecting pass has actually run — would recur for any future pass reordering. **Actual fix:** a brand-new, standalone, *last*-running pass, `KotlinSpecificRule.alignBracelessElseWithIf`, operating on the fully-formatted text (joins the final token stream verbatim, splits on `\n`) rather than tokens-mid-pipeline, called from `Formatter.formatOne` immediately after `enforceWhereClausePlacement` — the last of Phase 4's Kotlin-only calls, after every paren-tightening/spacing pass has already run. For each line, if it and the next line share identical leading-whitespace length, the first is an exact `<indent>if(...) <body>` shape (matching close paren found via local depth counting to handle a nested call in the condition, e.g. `if( System.currentTimeMillis() < 0 )`), and the second is `<indent>else <body>` (not `else if`, not `else {`), the `else` line is rebuilt with spaces after `else` so its body starts at the exact same column as the `if` line's body (floor of 1 space, matching every other single-space join in this codebase — never a negative pad). Trusted by construction that any two adjacent lines matching this exact shape are one `if`/`else` pair, never an unrelated coincidence, since Kotlin's grammar only ever places a bare `else` directly after its own `if`'s body. Verified: `make all` (silent success), and — since the coordinator had by this point enabled `kt_combined_inp.kt`/`kt_comments_inp.kt`'s sibling in the `Makefile`'s `INP_FILES` (`kt_comments_inp.kt` remains commented out, untouched, still has its own separate open bugs, out of scope) — `make test` now runs `kt_combined_inp.kt` for real, both the forward (`inp`→`out`) and idempotency (`out`→`out`) passes: **35/35, zero regressions**, including this exact line. |
| RDD_KEY_129 | User request: extend Kotlin's braceless-collapse + column-alignment behavior for `if`/`else if`/`else` chains (RDD_KEY_124/127/128) to C/C++/Java, but **only** as an all-or-nothing whole-chain transform — every branch in the chain must already be brace-eligible for a single-statement collapse, otherwise the entire chain (not just the non-qualifying branch) must be left untouched byte-for-byte, unlike Kotlin where per-branch membership alone suppresses that branch's own collapse | Implemented as a new whole-chain qualification scan, `chainAllBranchesCollapsible(tokens, kwIndex, n)`, run once from the chain's start (found via new `findChainStart`/`prevChainBranchIf`, which only ever hops backward through an explicit `else` token — deliberately never through a bare `}` alone, since an early buggy version that did so incorrectly absorbed an unrelated standalone `if(...) { ... }` statement immediately preceding a real chain into the chain-start search, partially collapsing branches that were never part of the same chain; root-caused via a minimal repro built by progressively narrowing a failing real-code case). `isPartOfElseChain` (the existing per-branch chain-membership guard) is restructured so its Kotlin behavior is unchanged (any membership suppresses that branch's own collapse) while its C/C++/Java behavior instead calls `chainAllBranchesCollapsible` and only suppresses when the *whole* chain fails to qualify — meaning collapse for C/C++/Java is now genuinely whole-chain, not per-branch. A comment sitting between two branches (`} /* note */ else {`) must also block the whole chain, not be silently skipped past, so two new whitespace/newline-only (not comment-skipping) traversal helpers were added, `skipWhitespaceOnly`/`skipWhitespaceOnlyBackward`, used at every inter-branch gap check in the new scan instead of the pre-existing `skipNonSignificant` (which does skip comments, wrong here) — verified against `test/real_code_regressions_5_inp.cpp`'s `if(result>0){...} /* Call otherwise */ else {...}`, which now correctly stays fully braced. A bare terminal `else { ... }` had no reachable collapse path at all before this change (the main loop's keyword dispatch only ever handled `if`/`while`/`for`, which have a `(...)` condition to anchor a `ControlBlock` match on — `else` has none), so a new sibling branch was added to `collapseSingleExpressionBlocks`'s main loop, gated by `!lang.isKotlin`, that finds its own chain start and re-checks `chainAllBranchesCollapsible` before collapsing. `alignBracelessElseIfChain` (previously Kotlin-only, in `KotlinSpecificRule.java`) was moved into the shared `BlockStructureRule.java` as a `public` method and is now invoked unconditionally for all languages from `Formatter.java`'s Phase 4, immediately after every earlier paren-tightening/spacing pass has settled — same ordering lesson as RDD_KEY_128 (an earlier attempt at collapse-time alignment would have measured a stale line width, for the same reason RDD_KEY_128's first attempt did). Verified against multiple real fixtures including edge cases: comment-only bodies (`test/cpp_core_inp.cpp`'s 4-branch chain, two of whose branches are comment-only — the whole chain correctly stays untouched, since `isSingleStatementBody` rejects a comment-only body), a comment between branches (`real_code_regressions_5`), and an unrelated standalone `if` immediately preceding a real chain (the `findChainStart` bugfix repro). Deliberately out of scope for this key: a chain where at least one branch is *already* brace-free going in (mixed braced/braceless input) is treated as non-qualifying by `chainAllBranchesCollapsible` and the whole chain is left untouched — no fixture exercises "chains built from an originally-braceless branch" for C/C++/Java, since (unlike Kotlin) that language family's grammar cannot produce a braceless multi-line body as raw input in the first place, so there was no such shape to generalize from. `make test` full suite green (forward + idempotency) after this key and RDD_KEY_130 landed together. |
| RDD_KEY_130 | Two follow-up defects found immediately after RDD_KEY_129 shipped, both against the same feature: (1) an idempotency failure — reformatting an already-formatted file with a collapsed+aligned `if`/`else` pair grew the alignment's leading spaces on every successive pass (`else           [[unlikely]] --x;` → `else                [[unlikely]] --x;` on a second run of the same input, for `cpp_modern.cpp`'s `[[likely]]`/`[[unlikely]]`-attributed pair); (2) a K&R `} else` (same physical source line as the closing brace) caused an entire multi-branch chain to collapse onto one single giant line, when the user's actual intent — confirmed directly — was Allman-style, one branch per line, column-aligned (`if(cond)       doA();` / `else if(other) doB();` / `else           doC();`), matching Kotlin's own collapsed-chain shape, not a single joined line | (1) Root-caused via a two-pass debug repro (`/tmp/dbg.cpp` formatted, its own output fed back in as input, tail compared) that isolated the growth to `alignBracelessElseIfChain`'s render loop in `BlockStructureRule.java`: when re-extracting an `else` line's "body" text (the part after the fixed `<indent>else` prefix) to re-pad it, the code stripped only exactly one leading space (`body = body.substring(1)` if the first char was a space) — correct for a freshly-collapsed line (which the original Kotlin-only version of this pass always received, since it ran once, on Kotlin's own single-space-joined collapse output) but wrong once the same pass could also see an *already column-aligned* line from a prior run, whose body has many leading spaces baked in as content, not just structural indent; only one of those pre-existing spaces was stripped, the rest were kept as part of "body" and then a fresh padding block was prepended on top, compounding every re-run. Fixed by stripping *all* leading spaces (`while` loop instead of a single `if`) before re-padding, making the extraction idempotent regardless of how much padding the line already carries. (2) Root-caused as a missing line-break, not an alignment bug: `collapseSingleExpressionBlocks`'s collapse of an `if`/`else if` branch (`tryCollapse`) only ever emits the collapsed text itself and advances past the closing `}` — whatever whitespace token originally separated that `}` from a following `else` (a single space, for K&R same-line input) is then passed through completely unchanged by the main loop's default token-copy path, reproducing the K&R same-line join instead of ever inserting a line break. Fixed by adding `mostRecentLineIndent(tokens, index)` (walks backward from a token index to the nearest `NEWLINE`, reads the `WHITESPACE` token immediately after it as that physical line's indent — for a K&R `} else if (...)`  line this resolves to the closing brace's own indent, which always matches the opening `if`'s indent in practice since the `}` was itself originally aligned with its `if`) and `appendChainNewlineBeforeElse(tokens, from, n, out, indent)` (peeks past whitespace-only gap tokens for a following `else`; if found, emits `\n` + `indent` into the output buffer instead of letting the original same-line whitespace pass through, and returns the index positioned at `else` so the main loop's existing per-branch dispatch picks it up fresh on the next iteration). Called from immediately after every successful `if`/`else if` collapse in the main loop, using the *pre-collapse* `if` keyword's own token index for the indent lookup (not the post-collapse position, which no longer has a well-defined "line" of its own until the newline is inserted). Not needed after a bare terminal `else { ... }` collapse, since that branch is always chain-terminal by grammar — no further `else` can follow it.
