| RDD_KEY_191 | `STYLE_DATA_FORMATS.md` §5/§6 (YAML/TOML formatting rules, drafted at user request via `AskUserQuestion` for the ambiguous YAML points): YAML mapping colons column-align matching JSON/CSS (§1.1/§3.1's group/group-break shape and space-before-`:` convention); sequence items indent one level deeper than their parent mapping key; flow-style collections (`{a: 1}`, `[1, 2]`) preserved as written by default, but converted to block style if they'd exceed the configured `line-length` as flow (one-directional -- block is never converted to flow regardless of length); `indent-size` reuses the existing global default (4), no YAML-specific override in the style rule itself, but local test fixtures should exercise YAML's own 2-space community convention via an in-file `#% JXM_CFMT_CFG indent-size=2` directive rather than changing the tool-wide default; `indent-style` is explicitly documented as ignored/inapplicable for YAML, since the YAML spec forbids tab characters for structural indentation -- output is always space-indented regardless of the configured value. TOML was drafted using standard, high-confidence conventions without needing a user question (its spec is far more rigid than YAML's): `=` alignment reuses STYLE.md §5/§6's assignment-alignment shape directly (no forced space-before-`=`, unlike JSON/YAML's `:`); `[section]`/`[[array-of-table]]` headers are treated as headers (not declarations, same as CSS at-rules) and get no added indentation for the keys under them, since TOML expresses nesting via dotted header names rather than indentation, matching real-world tooling (`taplo`, `cargo fmt`-adjacent formatters); inline tables (`{ key = "value" }`) are always single-line per the TOML v1.0 grammar itself (a spec constraint, not a style choice), unlike arrays which do have a tight/loose choice (reusing JSON's §1.2 signal). Both sections added to `STYLE_DATA_FORMATS.md` after §4 (HTML5); `STATE_DATA_FORMATS.md`'s Scope/Config sections updated to match, including a Config-section callout of the `indent-style`-ignored-for-YAML exception. No code changed -- documentation only, YAML/TOML remain scaffold-only. |
| RDD_KEY_192 | YAML/TOML real-logic implementation (`STATE_DATA_FORMATS.md` checklist): confirmed the line-based recursive-descent parser design for YAML (no `TokenizerCore`/`Token` reuse -- indentation-significant grammar, not brace-delimited) and the flat non-recursive single-pass line-scanner design for TOML (nesting is via dotted table-header names, never indentation) were both correct and necessary, per RDD_KEY_189/191's family exclusion. Neither rule class reuses `TokenizerCore.markFrozenSpans` (`//%`/`/*%`-oriented, token-stream-coupled) -- both implement independent `#%`-prefixed `JXM_CFMT_DIS`/`ENA` frozen-span capture as raw verbatim line lists. Neither reuses `FormatterSimpleBraced.capitalizeCommentStart` (`//`/`/*`-oriented, would never find a letter to capitalize starting from a `#`) -- both implement their own `normComment` starting the scan at index 1. `YamlSpecificRule.java`/`TomlSpecificRule.java` moved from `Lang.SCAFFOLD_ONLY_LANGUAGES` to `Lang.SUPPORTED_LANGUAGES` (`FormatterCore.forLanguage` gained `isYaml`/`isToml` branches; `FormatterYaml.java`/`FormatterToml.java` created as thin dispatch siblings), same precedent as RDD_KEY_190's JSON/CSS migration. All 8 fixtures (`yaml_core`/`yaml_comments`/`toml_core`/`toml_comments`, `_inp`/`_out` each) pass `make test` (fixture-diff + idempotency). Two implementation bugs found+fixed via fixture testing: (1) YAML's `parseKeyItem` used a strict `peek().indent > ln.indent` child-block trigger, silently failing (and cascading into a full-document silent truncation via the parent block's shape-mismatch-break logic) for a sequence child at the *same* indent as its parent key (`fruits:` / `- apple`, both indent 0 -- valid, common YAML) -- fixed by allowing `>=` specifically when the next line is a sequence item. (2) YAML's key extraction (`code.substring(0, colon)`, both in `parseKeyItem` and sequence-of-mapping's `firstKey`) didn't `.trim()`, so re-parsing the formatter's own aligned output (`name : widget`) captured a trailing-space-contaminated key, silently widening colon-alignment-group width on the second pass (an idempotency bug) -- fixed by adding `.trim()`. (3) TOML's flat scanner assumed one physical line per `key = value`, but the formatter's own §6.3 loose-array output is intentionally multi-line, breaking idempotency -- fixed via a quote-aware `bracketBalance` helper that detects unbalanced brackets/braces and consumes+concatenates continuation lines before calling `parseValue`. Also found and fixed one fixture-authoring error (not a code bug): `yaml_core_out.yaml`'s nested `endpoints` flow array was hand-drafted expecting block conversion, but per §5.4's recursive-per-depth rule it correctly stays flow (83 chars fits under the 100-char default `line-length` at its post-conversion depth) -- only the outer `config` mapping (121 chars) needed to convert; fixture corrected to match the implementation. |
| RDD_KEY_193 | XML real-logic implementation (`STATE_DATA_FORMATS.md` checklist): confirmed a from-scratch character-cursor recursive-descent parser is the right design for XML (no natural line boundary in tag/attribute grammar, unlike YAML's indentation-significant one; unrelated to brace-delimited grammars, so no `TokenizerCore` reuse). `#%`-equivalent `<!--% JXM_CFMT_DIS -->`/`ENA` frozen-span detection and comment-start-case normalization are implemented independently (same per-format-family approach as YAML/TOML, RDD_KEY_192), not via `TokenizerCore.markFrozenSpans`. `InFileConfig`'s `JXM_CFMT_CFG` directive regex/preamble pattern extended to recognize `<!--% JXM_CFMT_CFG ... -->`. `Lang.SUPPORTED_LANGUAGES`/`isSupported` gained `xml`; `SCAFFOLD_ONLY_LANGUAGES`/`isScaffoldOnly` dropped it (HTML5 stays scaffold-only, sharing `XmlSpecificRule` internally per RDD_KEY_188 once its own void-element/script-style-dispatch additions land). Unlike YAML/TOML, XML's rule constructor takes `indentStyle` (§2.1: XML uses the existing global `indent-size`/`indent-style` unmodified, no ignored-setting exception). One implementation bug found+fixed: the wrap-on-overflow check was only reached for elements with non-empty children, so an empty (childless) overflowing tag never wrapped -- fixed by checking fits/overflow before branching on children-empty-or-not. Wrap shape (closing `>` attached to the last attribute line, not on its own line) resolved by judgment call, matching common real-world XML formatter convention, since §2.2 doesn't spell out the exact shape. All 4 xml fixtures (`xml_combined`/`xml_comments`, `_inp`/`_out`) pass `make test` (202/202 total, zero regressions). (`xml_combined` originally shipped under the name/content `xml_core` -- a mismatch against `FUTURE_TEST_FIXTURES.md`'s drafted `xml_combined` fixture, corrected later; both re-verified against the real JAR at that point.) Known simplifications (not exercised by current fixtures): no content/text reflow, mixed content handled as separate lines rather than staying inline, `indent-style=auto` not detected, and §2.4's CDATA-inside-`<script>`/`<style>` unwrap-and-dispatch exception is not implemented (blocked on JS/TS/CSS dispatch, still scaffold/partial elsewhere). |
| RDD_KEY_194 | HTML5 real-logic implementation (`STATE_DATA_FORMATS.md` checklist): extended `XmlSpecificRule.java` (shared with XML per RDD_KEY_188, gated on `lang.isHtml5`) rather than creating a new class. §4.1 void elements (`area`/`base`/`br`/`col`/`embed`/`hr`/`img`/`input`/`link`/`meta`/`param`/`source`/`track`/`wbr`) parsed as self-closing leaves regardless of trailing `/`, rendered with bare `>` not `/>`; bare boolean attributes (no `=value`) accepted in HTML5 mode. §4.3 `<pre>` captured as a new `RAW` node type (byte-for-byte, no reindentation). `<script>`/`<style>` captured as raw text via new `finishRawTextElement` (never tag-parsed -- embedded JS/CSS isn't XML grammar). §4.2 dispatch: `<style>` splices to the already-real `CssSpecificRule.format`, reindented one level deeper via new `reindent` helper, spliced back before `</style>` -- fully real, since CSS support already landed (RDD_KEY_190). `<script>`: non-JS `type="..."` (e.g. `application/json`) stays opaque; JS-type content has no formatter to dispatch to yet since `STATE_JS_TS.md`'s job is still scaffold-only, so `renderScriptOrStyle` throws a clear `XmlParseException` rather than silently passing real JS through untouched -- a deliberate refinement of the file's earlier "HTML-before-JS/TS contingency" note (which had proposed silent opaque passthrough instead of an explicit throw). Escape hatch: new `isFrozenScriptContent` detects a `//% JXM_CFMT_DIS`/`//% JXM_CFMT_ENA` line pair anywhere in the raw content (not just the first line, to accommodate the CDATA-wrapped idiom whose literal first line is `<![CDATA[`) -- both local HTML fixtures currently rely on this to freeze their real JS `<script>` bodies; see the cross-job follow-up bullet added to `STATE_JS_TS.md`'s checklist for what happens when JS/TS support lands (remove the wrapping, wire a real dispatch call mirroring `<style>`'s CSS splice, re-verify both fixtures). One general (non-HTML5-specific) bug found+fixed: self-closing/void elements never checked line length or wrapped attributes on overflow (a previously-documented gap, RDD_KEY_193) -- fixed by adding the same fits-check + one-attribute-per-line wrap branch already used for non-self-closing tags, to the self-closing render path. `Lang.SUPPORTED_LANGUAGES`/`isSupported` gained `html5`; `SCAFFOLD_ONLY_LANGUAGES`/`isScaffoldOnly` dropped it; `FormatterCore.forLanguage` routes `isHtml5` to `FormatterXml` alongside `isXml`. Two fixture-authoring mismatches (not code bugs) corrected to match verified-real, precedent-backed behavior: mixed text+element content splits onto separate lines rather than staying inline (matches RDD_KEY_193's documented XML simplification), and a trailing same-line comment gets a preceding space (matches `xml_comments_out.xml`'s existing precedent); a third fixture under-coverage issue (a `<div>` attribute value too short to genuinely overflow the line-length limit, contradicting `test/README.txt`'s documented intent) was fixed by lengthening the value. `make test`: 212/212 PASS, 0 FAIL (up from 202, +2 HTML5 fixture pairs registered live in the Makefile, zero regressions). |
| RDD_KEY_195 | JS/TS §15 import-path local-vs-third-party classification (STATE_JS_TS.md checklist): STYLE_JS_TS.md's own text flags this as unresolved -- "local" detection has two disjuncts, relative-path-prefixed OR "resolves within the project's configured source root", and the second disjunct has no corresponding config key or resolution logic anywhere in this codebase. **Decision (asked and resolved by the user):** drop the source-root disjunct entirely. Only `./`-prefixed and `../`-prefixed import specifiers are classified as "local". Any bare specifier that is not a recognized Node built-in (via a known built-in module list and/or the `node:` prefix) and is not relative-path-prefixed is classified as "third-party" -- including genuinely first-party absolute imports resolved via a bundler/tsconfig `baseUrl`/`paths` mechanism (e.g. `"components/Widget"` pointing at the project's own source tree), which will be misclassified as third-party under this rule. This is a known, accepted simplification -- no new config key (e.g. a source-root path) is added. Documented as a Known Limitations entry in `README.md`. |
| RDD_KEY_196 | JS/TS closing-comment conventions for methods and object-shaped `type` aliases (STATE_JS_TS.md checklist, Checkpoint-21 bug #2): **Decision (asked and resolved by the user):** (1) a class method's closing comment omits all modifiers (`async`/`static`/`get`/`set`/etc.) and uses the bare member name only -- `// load`, not `// async load` -- since a method has no introducing keyword of its own (unlike `class`/`enum`/`for`/`switch`/standalone `function`, which do have one and keep it, e.g. `// class Widget`, `// function foo`); this matches existing C/C++/Java/Kotlin precedent of keyword+name with no modifiers. (2) An object-shaped `type X = { ... }` alias (§14) is a named construct in the same sense as `interface`/`class`/`enum` per STYLE.md §7's universal rule, so it always gets a closing comment regardless of body length -- `}; // type X`, placed after the trailing semicolon the alias requires. Non-object-shaped aliases with no brace body (e.g. `type Status = "active" | "inactive";`, §13) are unaffected. `STYLE_JS_TS.md` §14's `type Point` example updated to show `}; // type Point` instead of bare `};`. |
| RDD_KEY_197 | JS/TS §15 import-ordering pass, comment-near-import handling (STATE_JS_TS.md checklist, Checkpoint-21 bug #9): **Decision (asked and resolved by the user):** a trailing same-line comment to the right of an `import` statement (e.g. `import x from "y"; // note`) does NOT block reordering/grouping -- it stays attached to its import line and travels with it. A standalone comment on its own line between two import declarations DOES block reordering across it, but instead of bailing the entire pass (current behavior), it should act as a group boundary the same way a blank line already does elsewhere in this codebase for declaration-alignment groups (STYLE_JS_TS.md line ~294-295, "blank line or comment breaks the group") -- imports before the standalone comment and imports after it are grouped/sorted as separate segments, not reordered across each other, with the comment preserved in its original position. Not yet implemented as of this decision -- `enforceImportOrdering`'s current `hasCommentBetween`/bail-the-whole-pass design in `JsTsSpecificRule.java` needs updating to (a) exclude trailing comments from the bail check and re-emit them attached to their import's span, and (b) segment the import list at standalone comments instead of bailing, running the existing per-segment grouping/sorting logic independently per segment. |
| RDD_KEY_198 | HTML5 `<ruby>` implied-end-tag support (`STATE_DATA_FORMATS.md` Open Questions -> resolved): **Decision (asked and resolved by the user):** rather than implementing HTML5's full tree-construction implied-end-tag spec (a large, per-element-family feature covering `<li>`/`<dt>`/`<dd>`/`<p>`/`<thead>`/`<tbody>`/`<tfoot>`/`<tr>`/`<td>`/`<th>`/`<option>`/`<optgroup>`/`<colgroup>`/`<caption>` in addition to the ruby family), add a small, extensible `XmlSpecificRule.OPAQUE_IMPLIED_END_TAG_ELEMENTS` name set -- starting with just `ruby` -- reusing the same opaque-verbatim-span pattern already used for `<script>`/`<style>`/`<pre>` (`finishRawTextElement`/`finishRawElement`). New `parseElement` interception (before attribute parsing begins) routes any HTML5 element whose lowercased tag name is in the set to a new `parseOpaqueImpliedEndTagElement`, which scans forward from the element's own opening `<` to its own MATCHING `</tag>` close (tracking nested same-name open/close depth via a new boundary-aware `indexOfTagBoundary` helper, and a quote-aware `findTagEnd` helper to skip `>` inside quoted attribute values), capturing the ENTIRE span -- opening tag, all children including `<rb>`/`<rt>`/`<rp>`/`<rtc>` or any further nesting, and the closing tag -- as one new `NodeType.OPAQUE` node, rendered with a single leading indent and the raw span appended byte-for-byte untouched (no reformatting/reindentation/attribute normalization anywhere inside, including of the `<ruby ...>` opening tag itself). This is deliberately the simple "opaque scan-to-matching-close" mechanism only -- no per-element implied-closing-trigger logic was built; extending to additional elements later is adding a name to the set, nothing more. Fixture `test/real_code_regressions_104_{inp,out}.html` isolates the ruby case (both a whitespace-formatted `<ruby>` with `<rp>`/`<rt>` pairs, and the real dogfood repro's dense `<rb>`x3/`<rt>`x3/`<rtc>`x1 no-whitespace form). `make test`: 153/153 forward + idempotency, zero regressions. Confirmed via the `alexandersandberg/html5-elements-tester` dogfood spot-check that the specific `<ruby>` `XmlParseException` this was found from no longer occurs -- forward pass now proceeds past that line; a separate, unrelated pre-existing bug (`parseAttr` requires a quoted attribute value, but the file also has an unquoted `size=5` on a `<select>`, line 718) still blocks that file's full end-to-end dogfood completion, out of scope for this decision (see `STATE_DATA_FORMATS.md`'s HTML5 checklist entry). |
| RDD_KEY_199 | HTML5 unquoted attribute value support (`STATE_DATA_FORMATS.md` HTML5 checklist follow-up to RDD_KEY_198): `XmlSpecificRule.parseAttr` previously required a quoted (`"`/`'`) attribute value unconditionally, throwing `XmlParseException("expected quoted value for attribute '...'")` on any bare value -- confirmed as the blocker that stopped the `alexandersandberg/html5-elements-tester` dogfood forward pass past RDD_KEY_198's `<ruby>` fix, at line 718's `<select ... size=5>`. Per the HTML5 spec grammar, an unquoted attribute value is legal wherever it contains none of: whitespace, `"`, `'`, `=`, `<`, `>`, backtick. Fix: on the `lang.isHtml5` branch only (plain XML unchanged -- still requires quotes, no such grammar exists in the XML spec), when no opening quote follows `=`, scan forward per that grammar and accept the run as the value; empty run (bare `=` with nothing valid after it) is still a hard `XmlParseException`. Output preserves the value unquoted, exactly as written -- no forced normalization to double-quoted was applied, since `STYLE_DATA_FORMATS.md` says nothing about attribute-value quote-style normalization and the codebase's existing behavior for the already-quoted branch is to preserve whichever quote character (`"` or `'`) was originally written verbatim (the substring capture already includes the original quote chars, no rewrite step) -- the same "preserve as written" posture used elsewhere (JSON5 per-key quote style §1, TOML string quote style §6.4). Extending to force double-quotes was judged a new, unrequested normalization decision, not a bug fix, so it was not done. Fixture `test/real_code_regressions_106_{inp,out}.html` isolates the case (`<select size=5>`, multiple `<option value=...>`, an `<input>` mixing unquoted values with a bare boolean attribute). `make test`: 155/155 forward + idempotency, zero regressions. Re-running the `alexandersandberg/html5-elements-tester` dogfood forward pass confirms it proceeds past line 718, but hits a new, distinct, unrelated pre-existing blocker further along at line 759's `<datalist><option value="Option 1"> ... </datalist>` (bare `<option>` tags with no closing `</option>` at all, relying on HTML5's implied-end-tag rule for `<option>`/next-sibling-`<option>`/parent-close, the same open-ended implied-end-tag family named but not built out in RDD_KEY_198's decision) -- out of scope for this fix, not addressed, see `STATE_DATA_FORMATS.md`'s HTML5 checklist entry. |
| RDD_KEY_200 | HTML5 `<option>` implied-closing-trigger support (`STATE_DATA_FORMATS.md` HTML5 checklist follow-up to RDD_KEY_199): **Decision (asked and resolved by the user):** rather than either treating `<option>` fully opaque like `ruby`/`OPAQUE_IMPLIED_END_TAG_ELEMENTS` (rejected -- `<option>` is far more common and usually explicitly closed, so opacity would silently stop reformatting its content in the common case too) or building full HTML5 tree-construction support for every optional-end-tag element family at once (rejected earlier, RDD_KEY_198, as too large), build a small, general, reusable "implied-closing trigger" table -- `XmlSpecificRule.IMPLIED_CLOSE_TRIGGERS`, `Map<String, Set<String>>` from element name to its set of sibling start-tag names that implicitly close it -- and register only `option` today (`{"option", "optgroup"}`). Unlike `ruby`'s opaque-verbatim-span capture, an element with a registered trigger set is still parsed as a REAL node (attributes/children/normal rendering) -- only the "when do I stop consuming children" decision changes. Mechanism: `parseNodes` gained an overload taking an optional trigger set; its loop now also breaks (without consuming) as soon as upcoming input is a start tag (not a closing tag) whose name is in the set, via new `startsWithTriggerTag`. `parseElement` looks up `IMPLIED_CLOSE_TRIGGERS.get(lowerTag)` (HTML5-gated) and passes it through; after children parsing, if an explicit `</tag>` follows, it's consumed exactly as before (regression-safe for the common explicitly-closed case); otherwise, if a trigger set was registered, the element is treated as implicitly closed with no explicit tag consumed (covers both the sibling-trigger case and the pre-existing "parent's own closing tag also stops children" behavior `stopAtCloseTag` already provided, reused rather than reinvented for the parent-close half of the rule); otherwise (no trigger set registered) the existing hard `XmlParseException` is unchanged. Fixture `test/real_code_regressions_108_{inp,out}.html` covers both an explicitly-closed `<option>` (regression guard) and a `<datalist>` with bare `<option value="...">`/`<optgroup>`-wrapped bare `<option>` relying on the implied-close rule (sibling-trigger and parent-close cases both). `make test` green, zero regressions. Re-ran the `alexandersandberg/html5-elements-tester` dogfood forward pass: it now completes the full 42KB file end-to-end (forward pass, round2 reformat, `diff -rq` idempotency, `html_sc.js` syntax-check, and `html_content_diff.py` content-preservation all clean) -- this was the file's third and last known blocker; the dogfood run for this candidate is now DONE. |
| RDD_KEY_201 | `alignCommentSeparators` false-positive narrowing attempt, reverted (STATE_C_CPP_JAVA.md "Known Gaps -- Open", jenkinsci/jenkins item 25 follow-up): tried narrowing `MiscRuleCore.parseSeparatorComment`'s qualifying-character set from RDD_KEY_50's original "any single non-alphanumeric character flanked by a literal space" to a fixed allowlist (`—:–|~`, matching STYLE.md §15's own worked-example separator characters), to fix the false-positive found on ordinary English prose mentioning a single punctuation character (e.g. `// The @ can be used...`). **Reverted**: this backfired, dropping `make test` from 162/162 to 157/162 by newly corrupting 5 pre-existing Kotlin fixtures (`real_code_regressions_{21,24,25,26,30}`) whose multi-line doc-comments discuss `:` in prose. Root cause of the backfire: RDD_KEY_50's original broad rule's "a comment line with 2+ qualifying candidates is disqualified entirely" check was incidentally protecting these blocks, since under the broad definition each prose line already had several OTHER non-alphanumeric-flanked-by-space characters (unrelated to `:`) that tripped the 2+-candidate disqualifier. Narrowing the charset removed those other candidates from consideration, leaving only the lone `:` per line, so these blocks newly qualified as a genuine separator pair and got corrupted. Conclusion: character-set narrowing alone does not fix this class of false positive -- it only relocates which punctuation triggers it, and can also make previously-safe files newly unsafe by disabling the multi-candidate disqualifier they depended on. Fully reverted via `git checkout` on `MiscRuleCore.java`; confirmed `make test` back to 162/162 forward + idempotency. The underlying design question (how to actually distinguish a genuine aligned-table separator comment from incidental prose punctuation) remains OPEN -- candidate directions not yet tried: requiring 3+ consecutive qualifying lines instead of 2, or requiring the label/rest text to look structurally like code rather than natural-language prose. Do not re-attempt plain character-set narrowing as the fix without also addressing the multi-candidate-disqualifier interaction documented here. |
| RDD_KEY_202 | `alignCommentSeparators` false-positive on ordinary English prose -- FIXED (third attempt, direction (2) of two prior structural directions considered; see RDD_KEY_201 for the two earlier reverted attempts: fixed-character-set narrowing, then a 3+-consecutive-line threshold, each of which broke a different pre-existing legitimate case). Added `MiscRuleCore.looksCodeLike(String)`, a structural code-likeness check applied to each separator-alignment candidate line's parsed `label` and `rest` (`SepMatch`) before it's allowed to extend or start a run: a fragment must (a) split into at most 4 whitespace-separated words, (b) be at most 24 characters, and (c) contain no whole word (case-insensitive, single-letter words exempted so identifier-like suffixes such as the `A`/`BB` in `Comment A`/`Comment BB` are never mistaken for the article "a") matching a small common-English-stopword list (a/an/the/is/are/was/were/be/been/being/to/of/in/on/if/that/this/these/those/can/used/use/with/for/and/or/but/not/it/as/by/from/one/which/quoted/correctly). A line failing either check on label or rest is treated exactly like a non-qualifying line (breaks the run) rather than merged into or corrupting a group. This resolves the jenkins `IdStrategy.java`-style false positive (`// The @ can be used in local-part if quoted correctly` / `// => the last @ is the one used to separate the domain and local-part`, both now left untouched and idempotent) while leaving the pre-existing legitimate `hpp_core_inp.hpp` 2-line case (`// Comment A : 10` / `// Comment BB : 20`) correctly padded -- the exact case the two prior reverted attempts each broke in a different way. Verified: `make test` 172/172 forward + idempotency (up from clean 171/171 baseline). New fixture `real_code_regressions_123` combines the jenkins-style prose repro with a genuine 2-line separator-alignment pair (`// Count : 1` / `// GrandTotal : 22`) in one file. Hand-stress-tested beyond the fixture (not committed): a solo short prose comment under the word/length caps but hitting no stopword correctly stays unaligned only when paired with a differing/absent neighbor (isolated single-line case is a no-op regardless, per the pre-existing 2+-line minimum-group-size rule); a legitimate pair with a longer-but-still-code-like label (`GrandTotal`) still aligns/pads correctly. No local jenkins/microsoft-STL checkout was reachable this session for further real-world spot-checking beyond the unit suite and these constructed fixtures. See `STATE_C_CPP_JAVA.md`'s "Known Gaps -- Fixed". |
| RDD_KEY_203 | `GetterSetterRuleCurly.parseOneLinerMember`'s breakable-width pre-check gated only on `isDefinition` -- FIXED (second, independent shape of "Declaration-alignment column-padding non-idempotency" left open after RDD_KEY_202's session; see `STATE_C_CPP_JAVA.md`'s "Known Gaps -- Fixed"). Repro: `microsoft/STL`'s `filesystem.hpp` `recursive_directory_iterator` class -- a zero-arg default ctor, a long copy-ctor declaration whose own too-long non-empty parameter list is later wrapped across multiple physical lines by `enforceCallLineBreaking` (RDD_KEY_86), a move-ctor declaration, and a destructor, all `= default;` pure-specifier one-liners. On a fresh format the long copy-ctor is still one raw physical line so it wrongly joins the group and its full un-wrapped width sets the `=` column; on a reformat of that fresh output the now-wrapped copy-ctor no longer parses as a one-liner and is excluded, narrowing the group and shrinking the `=` column -- non-idempotent. Root cause: `parseOneLinerMember` already has a documented pre-check (added for exactly this class of bug, per that method's own class-level doc comment) that excludes a member from its group when its predicted rendered width exceeds `lineLengthLimit`, keeping first-pass grouping decisions consistent with what a later reformat would produce -- but it was gated `isDefinition && hasBreakableCall(...)`, covering only definitions with a breakable call in their body; a non-definition member (plain declaration or pure-specifier `= 0`/`= delete`/`= default`) has no body, but its own `(params)` list is exactly the shape `enforceCallLineBreaking` may wrap, and had the identical divergence risk left unchecked. Fixed by adding `hasBreakableParams = !isDefinition && paramsFrom < paramsTo` alongside the existing `hasBreakableCall` check. Verified via a minimal constructed fixture (`test/real_code_regressions_124`, no local `microsoft/STL` checkout was reachable this session to spot-check the real file directly): round1 == round2, and the surviving group (move-ctor + destructor) aligns its `=` column at the same character index in both rounds. `make test`: 172/172 forward + 172/172 idempotency, zero regressions. |
| RDD_KEY_204 | HTML5 `<p>` not auto-closed by a following block-level sibling -- FIXED. Found during the `apache/ant` `manual/` HTML5 dogfood run: a `<p>` with no explicit `</p>` before a following `<h3>` (a common, valid HTML5 idiom relying on the spec's "close a p element" implied-end-tag rule) made `parseElement`'s `parseNodes(true, null)` call keep consuming every subsequent sibling as a child of that `<p>` (since `<h3>` is a start tag, not a closing tag, so the `stopAtCloseTag` check never triggers), until the first unrelated real closing tag anywhere downstream got misattributed as this `<p>`'s own close -- producing a spurious duplicate `</p>` right before `</body>` at EOF and silently reparenting a large subtree under the wrong element. Fixed the same way as `option`/`head` (RDD_KEY_200): registered `p` in the existing, general, reusable `XmlSpecificRule.IMPLIED_CLOSE_TRIGGERS` map with the HTML5 spec's fixed, stable "close a p element" trigger-tag list (`address`, `article`, `aside`, `blockquote`, `details`, `div`, `dl`, `fieldset`, `figcaption`, `figure`, `footer`, `form`, `h1`-`h6`, `header`, `hgroup`, `hr`, `main`, `menu`, `nav`, `ol`, `p`, `pre`, `section`, `table`, `ul`) -- no new parsing logic, same mechanism already built for `option`/`head`. This reverses that map's own prior doc-comment caveat ("do NOT add `li`/`td`/`tr`/`p`/etc. speculatively"), which was about *speculative* additions with no real-world evidence; this addition is driven by real, confirmed dogfood input (`apache/ant manual/antexternal.html` and ~30 other files in the same corpus), the same "populate only once real dogfood input needs it" criterion the map's doc comment already states. Fixture `test/real_code_regressions_125_{inp,out}.html`. `make test`: 173/173 forward + idempotency, zero regressions. See `STATE_DATA_FORMATS.md`'s `apache/ant manual/` dogfood entry. |
| RDD_KEY_205 | HTML5 same-line trailing comment after a `<td>`'s (or any element's) sole text child silently dropped -- FIXED. Found during the `apache/ant` `manual/` HTML5 dogfood run, alongside RDD_KEY_204: `<td>The directory to invoke JVM in.<!--(ignored if fork is disabled)--></td>` lost its comment entirely on format. Root cause was two separate gaps: (1) `attachTrailingCommentIfAny` (the existing mechanism from the `WordPress/wordpress-develop` session's `real_code_regressions_103` fix) correctly attaches the comment as the preceding `TEXT` node's own `trailingComment` field, but `renderNode`'s `case TEXT:` branch never read it, only `n.raw` -- fixed by routing it through the existing `appendWithTrailing` helper like every other node type already does. (2) Independently, `renderElement`'s "sole content child" inline fast path (`<tag>text</tag>` rendered as one line when the tag's only child is a TEXT/CDATA node) reads `onlyChild.raw` directly and calls `appendWithTrailing` with the *parent* element's own `n.trailingComment`, never the child TEXT node's `onlyChild.trailingComment` -- this path bypasses `renderNode(onlyChild, ...)` entirely, so fix (1) alone did not cover it; fixed by splicing `onlyChild.trailingComment` into the inline line before the closing tag. Combined into fixture `test/real_code_regressions_125` alongside RDD_KEY_204's repro. `make test`: 174/174 forward + idempotency, zero regressions. See `STATE_DATA_FORMATS.md`'s `apache/ant manual/` dogfood entry. |
| RDD_KEY_206 | `JetBrains/kotlin` dogfood cluster C6i (1 file) -- FIXED: multiple one-line interface member declarations fused without separators, no lambda/braces involved. Root cause: `ScopePipelineCurly.applySignaturePass`'s Kotlin `: ReturnType` tail detection (the mechanism behind RDD_KEY_147/RDD_KEY_163) finds the *last* top-level `)` in a named-scope span via `findLastTopLevelCloseParen(tokens, span.start, span.openBraceIdx)` and, once it confirms a `:` immediately follows, only bailed on a *blank-line* run (RDD_KEY_147's guard) between that `)` and the span's own `{` -- not on an ordinary single NEWLINE. A headerless one-liner interface member with no body (`fun clear(): Unit`) immediately followed, on the very next line with no blank line between, by an unrelated named construct's own opening brace (a nested `interface MutableEntry<K, V> : ... { ... }`) had its `)`/`:` wrongly matched as belonging to that later brace: the whole span from `clear()`'s `)` through the interface's own name got swallowed into `KotlinSignatureRule.parseFunctionTail`'s tail range and flattened onto one line with no separators, silently fusing the two unrelated statements together. Fixed by a new `hasTopLevelNewline(tokens, afterParen, span.openBraceIdx)` bail, gated to fire only when no depth-0 `=` token is present in that same range (`ScopePipelineCurly.hasTopLevelOperator`, new helper) -- a genuine block-bodied `: ReturnType {` tail is always on one unbroken physical line, but the pre-existing legitimate expression-bodied-function-with-trailing-lambda-body shape (fixture `_33`, RDD_KEY_153's `TypeSpecHolder.kt` `addTypes`, `: ReturnType =` legitimately continuing onto a later physical line before its own trailing-lambda body's `{`) always has a depth-0 `=` in the same range and must keep working -- the `=`-guard distinguishes the two shapes. Reproduced from the real dogfood file `libraries/stdlib/js-ir-minimal-for-test/js-src/collectionsWithoutExportLogic.kt`'s `MutableMap` interface; minimized to fixture `test/real_code_regressions_155`. `make test`: 203/203 -> 204/204 forward + idempotency, zero regressions (including fixture `_33` itself, re-verified passing). | RDD_KEY_207 | `JetBrains/kotlin` dogfood cluster C6b (12 files) -- FIXED: Kotlin 2.4's multi-dollar string interpolation prefix (`$$"..."`, `$$$"""..."""`) got a spurious space before the string it prefixes (`$$"$key1"` rendered as `$$ "$key1"`). Root cause: `TokenizerCore.isIdentifierChar`/`isIdentifierStart` already treat `$` as an identifier char (needed for bare `$x` interpolation, RDD_KEY_116), so a `$$`/`$$$` run lexes as a plain IDENTIFIER token immediately followed by the STRING token it prefixes -- no existing tight-token entry recognized this specific IDENTIFIER-then-STRING join as needing to stay tight, so the generic default (space) fired. Same class of bug as C2's `@`-tight carve-out and C6c -- a missing entry in the shared tight-spacing table, not a tokenizer defect (confirmed the tokenizer itself already emits correct tokens; the gap is purely the downstream spacing decision). Fixed by a new `isDollarRun(text)` helper (exact `"$$"`/`"$$$"` match only, not a general `$+` regex -- a single bare `$` has no such meaning) plus a `lang.isKotlin && prev.type == IDENTIFIER && isDollarRun(prev.text) && cur.type == STRING` carve-out added to both documented duplicates, `MiscRuleCore.needsSpaceBetween` and `DeclarationAlignmentRuleCore.needsSpaceBetween` (mirroring C2's/C6d's precedent of fixing both copies). `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule.renderTokens`'s own override already calls the 4-arg `needsSpaceBetween(prev, t, tokens, i)` overload, so no third copy was needed there (unlike C6d, which needed a tokens-list lookahead only reachable through that overload -- this fix needs no lookahead beyond `prev`/`cur` themselves, so the 2-arg overload already used elsewhere is sufficient). Fixture `test/real_code_regressions_158`. `make test`: 206/206 -> 207/207 forward + idempotency, zero regressions. See `STATE_KOTLIN.md`'s Dogfood: JetBrains/kotlin section, cluster C6b. |

| RDD_KEY_210 | `JetBrains/kotlin` dogfood C6k Shape C6k-4 (`ClientUtils.kt`) -- FIXED: `!!` not spaced before a following keyword, e.g. `assert(port!! in COMPILE_DAEMON_PORTS_RANGE_START..<COMPILE_DAEMON_PORTS_RANGE_END)` had the source's own `port!! in` space stripped to `port!!in`, a parse error. Fixed by a new `isPostfixNullOpContinuation(t)` helper (`.`/`[`/`(`/`?.`/`!!`) narrowing `!!`'s right-side tightness to only fire before those, replacing the old symmetric `isTightNullOp(lastSignificant)` check with `isOp(lastSignificant, "!!") && isPostfixNullOpContinuation(t)`; `?.`'s own right side is left unconditionally tight (`dotTightOnRight = isOp(lastSignificant, "?.")`) since a member name always follows it directly, no ambiguity. As a side effect this also fixes an untested-but-latent related shape, `x!! ?: y` (elvis directly after `!!`), which the old formula also wrongly tightened; the narrower formula now correctly falls through to the pass's existing elvis-spacing branch for that case too. Diagnosed via temporary debug prints after an initial fix attempt using `isPunct(t, "?")` silently never matched -- confirmed via debug dump that Kotlin's `?` nullability-suffix token tokenizes as `TokenType.OP`, not `PUNCT` (consistent with `isOp(t, "?")` already used elsewhere in the codebase for the same purpose); corrected to `isOp`, removed debug prints once verified. | RDD_KEY_212 | `JetBrains/kotlin` dogfood C6k Shape C6k-1 (`Number2String.kt`, `TypeExpansionUtils.kt`, `TypeCommonizerTest.kt`) -- FIXED: two independent root causes both under the "multi-statement fusion" label. (a) `BlockStructureRule.isSingleStatementBody` routed Kotlin bodies through the C/C++/Java `semiCount == 1` branch whenever exactly one `;` was present anywhere, regardless of how many newline-delimited statements preceded it -- `if (tmp <= delta) { _K += kappa; grisuRound(tmp, delta); return len }` (two newline-separated statements, one trailing `;`-terminated statement, exactly one `;` total) was misdetected as single-statement and its enclosing braces/newlines collapsed away, deleting the earlier statements' structure. Kotlin's optional-semicolon grammar (STYLE_KOTLIN.md S1) makes "exactly one `;`" meaningless as a single-statement signal, unlike C/C++/Java where every statement mandatorily ends in one. Fixed by always routing Kotlin through the existing `isKotlinSingleStatementBody` helper regardless of `;` count, and widening that helper's boundary detection to also treat a depth-0 `;` PUNCT as a statement boundary (not just NEWLINE), so same-line `;`-separated statements (e.g. `real_code_regressions_26_inp.kt`'s `{ redirected = i; break }`) still correctly collapse to single-statement as before. (b) `KotlinSignatureRule.parseKotlinSignature` called `significantWithComments` immediately on entry, discarding NEWLINE tokens before any downstream pass could observe that a parameter's trailing-lambda default value held multiple newline-separated statements (e.g. `expandedConeType: (FirTypeAlias) -> ConeClassLikeType? = { alias -> alias.lazyResolveToPhase(...); alias.expandedConeType }` spanning two lines inside the lambda); `renderWithTail`'s inline-fits shortcut then flattened the whole signature onto one line, fusing the lambda body's statements together with no separator. An initial fix attempt added a bail inside `parseKotlinParam` (mirroring the existing `containsLineComment` bail there) but never fired -- debug prints confirmed the per-param token slice reaching that method already had its NEWLINE tokens stripped by the upstream `significantWithComments` call, so the multi-line shape was undetectable at that point. Fixed by moving the bail to the very top of `parseKotlinSignature`, operating on the raw pre-strip `sigTokens` via a new `containsMultilineNestedBrace(List<Token>)` helper (checks for a `{...}` block containing a depth-greater-than-0 NEWLINE) that returns `null` (leave the signature untouched) as soon as one is found -- mirroring `BlockStructureRule`'s identically-named, independently-kept helper (deliberate non-promotion to a shared base class, consistent with this codebase's established per-class-hierarchy duplication precedent). Fixture `test/real_code_regressions_163` (combines both sub-causes in one file). `make test`: 211/211 -> 212/212 forward + idempotency, zero regressions. Verified against all 5 real `JetBrains/kotlin` corpus file instances (`Number2String.kt` x3 under `libraries/stdlib/wasm/{internal,wasi,js}/`, `compiler/fir/providers/.../TypeExpansionUtils.kt`, `native/commonizer/.../TypeCommonizerTest.kt`) via `kotlin_syntax_check`, all clean. See `STATE_KOTLIN.md`'s Dogfood: JetBrains/kotlin section, cluster C6k. |
| RDD_KEY_213 | `JetBrains/kotlin` dogfood C6k Shape C6k-2 (`KtVisitorTest.kt`, `BenchmarksReport.kt`) -- FIXED: a Kotlin raw (triple-quoted) string whose content happens to end in its own literal `"` immediately before the closing `"""` produces a run of 4+ contiguous quote characters at the string's end in the source, e.g. `"""...s3""""".trimMargin()`. `TokenizerCurly.skipKotlinRawString` (RDD_KEY_117) terminated the raw string greedily at the *first* `"""` found within such a run, on the documented belief that this matched real Kotlin semantics -- it does not. Verified via `kotlin_syntax_check` that real `kotlinc` accepts both `"""abc"""".trimMargin()` (4 trailing quotes) and `"""abc""""".trimMargin()` (5 trailing quotes) with zero syntax errors, which is only possible if the closing delimiter is always the *last* three quotes of a trailing quote run, with everything before it (including any extra quotes) treated as string content -- the opposite of RDD_KEY_117's "greedy on the first `\"\"\"`" assumption. Terminating at the first three quotes of the run left one or more stray `"` characters as their own separate token(s) immediately before the following `.trimMargin()`/etc. call, and a later spacing pass then inserted a space before that stray quote token -- turning the source's `""""` into `""" "`, a real content corruption (not merely misplaced whitespace) that also broke the string's own boundaries. Fixed by extending `skipKotlinRawString` to scan through the *entire* contiguous quote run once three or more are found (`while (source.charAt(q) == '"') q++`), so the returned closing position always lands after the whole run regardless of its exact length, matching real `kotlinc` behavior for any trailing-quote-run length. Fixture `test/real_code_regressions_164` (covers both the reported 4-quote case from the real corpus and a synthetic 5-quote case as a non-regression). `make test`: 212/212 -> 213/213 forward + idempotency, zero regressions. Verified against both real `JetBrains/kotlin` corpus files (`compiler/psi/psi-impl/tests/.../KtVisitorTest.kt`, `kotlin-native/performance/benchmarksReports/.../BenchmarksReport.kt`) via `kotlin_syntax_check`, both clean. See `STATE_KOTLIN.md`'s Dogfood: JetBrains/kotlin section, cluster C6k. |
| RDD_KEY_214 | `JetBrains/kotlin` dogfood, Category 2 (idempotency-only) cluster D2, largest bucket (182 of 334 files matched a strict "closing-brace-line-only, same trimmed content" diff shape; 328/334 of the full known idempotency-flap list resolved by this one fix) — **FIXED**: `ScopePipelineCurly.processScope`'s `effectiveSpanIndent` selection already had an `isChainedCatchFinally` carve-out (RDD_KEY_158) making a chained `catch`/`finally` span inherit the immediately-preceding span's own already-resolved `effectiveSpanIndent` rather than re-deriving `braceIndent`/`spanIndent` from its own physical text — but that carve-out was scoped only to the `catch`/`finally` keywords, not to an ordinary Kotlin fluent-chain continuation (`}.apply {`, `}.also {`, `}?.let {`, etc). For such a chain, this span's own `braceIndent` (the physical-line indent of wherever its `{` happens to land) is not a stable fixed point across passes: it depends on where the IMMEDIATELY PRECEDING span's own closing `}` physically lands, which can legitimately reflow round-to-round for reasons unrelated to this span at all (confirmed via `JetBrains/kotlin`'s `declarationBuilders.kt`: `fun IrClass.addFunction(...): IrSimpleFunction = addFunction { ... }.apply { ... }` — round1 read `}.apply {`'s own physical-line indent as 4 spaces, round2 re-read the same join as 0 spaces once the preceding `addFunction { ... }` span's own close text had reflowed between passes, an unrelated but adjacent volatility). Debug-traced via a temporary env-gated print in `ScopePipelineCurly.processScope` dumping `isNamedScope`/`spanIndent`/`braceIndent`/`effectiveSpanIndent`/`chainKwIdx` for every span (removed before commit) against the real corpus file, confirming `chainKwIdx` for the `.apply {` span was a plain `.` OP token immediately following the preceding span's own close brace with zero gap, and that the preceding span's own `prevEffectiveSpanIndent` was already stable (0) across both rounds even though its own physical closing text was not. **Fix**: new `isChainedFluentCall` boolean, structurally identical to `isChainedCatchFinally`'s own condition (Kotlin-gated, `prevEffectiveSpanIndent != null`, `prevCloseBraceIdx >= 0`, `nextSignificantIndex(current, prevCloseBraceIdx) == chainKwIdx`) but checking `current.get(chainKwIdx)` is an `OP` token `"."`/`"?."` instead of a `catch`/`finally` keyword; both conditions now feed the same `effectiveSpanIndent = prevEffectiveSpanIndent` branch. Verified via a fresh reformat of the full previously-known 334-file idempotency-flap list (`/tmp/kt_retriage_round1` → refresh → re-diff): 328/334 now round-trip clean (up from 0 known-fixed before this session), the strict-diff-shape D2 bucket itself (182/334, files whose only diff is a same-trimmed-content closing-brace line) went 182/182 resolved. 6 files remain open, confirmed by inspection NOT the same root cause (2 are the D2-adjacent-but-distinct `where`-clause-continuation-indent shape — `TestStepBuilder.kt`/`common.kt` — likely another `effectiveSpanIndent` gap specific to a `where`-clause-wrapped **function** header, since `isNamedScope` per `NAMED_CONSTRUCT_KOTLIN` only covers `class`/`object`/`interface`/`enum`/`init`, never `fun`, unlike RDD_KEY_159's class/interface/object coverage; 2 are the unrelated D3 wrap-decision-flap family — `GenerateReleaseNotes.kt`/`TypeBridging.kt`; 2 are an unexplained residual brace-indent drift — `KaBaseSymbolRelationProvider.kt`/`TopLevelPhases.kt` — not yet root-caused, left open). Fixture `real_code_regressions_165`. `make test`: 213/213 → 214/214 forward + idempotency, zero regressions (Kotlin-gated in effect since the new predicate's `lang.isKotlin` check mirrors `isChainedCatchFinally`'s own gate, though the field itself is not explicitly re-gated since `braceIndent` is already `null` for non-Kotlin languages upstream, making the new branch structurally unreachable there — confirmed no C/C++/Java fixture regressed). |
| RDD_KEY_215 | `JetBrains/kotlin` dogfood D2a residual (RDD_KEY_214's own deferred 6-file list), where-clause-shaped share -- **FIXED**: two independent root causes, both surfacing only on a class/`fun` chain 3+ levels deep where each level carries its own wrapped generic `where` clause (`TestStepBuilder.kt`, `common.kt`, `KaBaseSymbolRelationProvider.kt`, `TopLevelPhases.kt`). (a) `ScopePipelineCurly.processScope`'s `effectiveSpanIndent` selection had RDD_KEY_159's `isNamedScope` carve-out (force `spanIndent` over a volatile `braceIndent`) scoped only to `NAMED_CONSTRUCT_KOTLIN` (`class`/`object`/`interface`/`enum`/`init`) -- a `fun` with its own wrapped generic `where` clause fell through to the plain `braceIndent.length() > spanIndent.length()` comparison, reading the now-wrapped `where` continuation line's own (deeper) indent and pushing the whole body one level deeper each pass, non-idempotent. Fixed by a new `headerHasTopLevelWhereClause(tokens, span)` helper (scans backward from `span.openBraceIdx` to the true statement start over paren/bracket/angle-bracket depth, mirroring `findParentIndent`'s own backward scan, then forward for a depth-0 `where` KEYWORD token) and a new `hasWhereClauseHeader` condition added alongside `isNamedScope` in the `effectiveSpanIndent` `if`-chain. This alone fully resolved `common.kt` and `KaBaseSymbolRelationProvider.kt`, and resolved `TopLevelPhases.kt`'s where-clause-related lines (leaving one unrelated line, see RDD_KEY_216). (b) `TestStepBuilder.kt` was NOT fixed by (a) alone -- its bug lives in the separate `KotlinSpecificRule.enforceWhereClausePlacement`/`signatureLineIndent` whole-file pass (distinct from `ScopePipelineCurly`), which wraps a `where` clause onto its own indented continuation line(s) when the collapsed one-line rendering would exceed `line-length`. `signatureLineIndent` (RDD_KEY_164) deliberately anchors a where-clause's base indent on the physical line of the boundary token (`;`/`}`/`{`) immediately preceding the statement, intended to read "the enclosing scope's own indent" -- correct when that boundary sits alone on a simple unwrapped line (verified against the existing, still-passing fixture `real_code_regressions_47`), but non-idempotent when the boundary token is itself the tail end of an ANCESTOR construct's own already-wrapped multi-line `where` clause: round1 reads that boundary line's indent from the original (arbitrary, human-authored) source text, while round2 reads it from whatever round1 itself freshly wrote for the ancestor's own where-clause tail line -- these are not guaranteed to match. Root-caused via a temporary `JXM_DEBUG_WHERE`-env-gated debug print inside `signatureLineIndent` (removed before commit) against a minimal 3-level-nested-where-clause reproduction (`InputArtifact`/`FacadeStepBuilder`/`NonGroupingStage`, closely modeled on the real `TestStepBuilder.kt`'s exact shape including a multi-line generic supertype list before the innermost `where`), confirming `baseIndent` computed as 14 in round1 but 20 in round2 for the same where-clause occurrence. Fixed by replacing the boundary-line-anchoring scan (`scanFrom = stmtStart - 1`) with a computation derived from the TRUE statement's own (stable) header-line indent -- via the same `stmtStart`+`nextSignificantIndex` scan already present in the method, but scanning from `stmtStart` itself rather than one token before it -- minus one `indentUnit`'s length (`String.substring`), since indentation always increases by exactly one `indentUnit` per nesting level, an invariant the formatter maintains; verified this formula reproduces fixture `_47`'s existing expected output exactly (statement indent 4 minus indentUnit 4 = baseIndent 0). Fixture `real_code_regressions_166` (the minimal 3-level nested-where-clause repro, cleaned up with the standard copyright header). `make test`: 214/214 -> 215/215 forward + idempotency, zero regressions. Verified all 4 where-clause-shaped files (`TestStepBuilder.kt`, `common.kt`, `KaBaseSymbolRelationProvider.kt`, `TopLevelPhases.kt`) round-trip clean for their where-clause-related lines. See `STATE_KOTLIN.md`'s Dogfood: JetBrains/kotlin section, cluster D2a. |
| RDD_KEY_216 | `JetBrains/kotlin` dogfood D2a residual (RDD_KEY_214's own deferred 6-file list), `TopLevelPhases.kt`'s remaining line after RDD_KEY_215's where-clause fix -- **FIXED**: `isReferencedByNativeRuntime`'s body (`declarations.any { ... } || declarations.any { ... }`) had its second `.any { ... }`'s closing `}` drift indent between rounds (8sp round1, 4sp round2). Root cause: `ScopePipelineCurly.isChainedFluentCall` (RDD_KEY_214) only recognized a trailing-lambda call chained onto the immediately preceding span's own `}` via a direct `.`/`?.` member-access token sitting right after it -- a chain instead joined by a boolean infix operator (`} || declarations.any {`, the token immediately after `}` is `||`, not `.`) fell through to the plain `braceIndent.length() > spanIndent.length()` comparison, reading the second `.any {`'s own physical-line indent, which is not a stable fixed point since it depends on how the FIRST `.any { ... }`'s own closing `}` happens to render that particular pass -- structurally the same volatility RDD_KEY_214 already fixed for the `.`/`?.` case, just gated too narrowly. Fixed by widening the chain-continuation-operator check to a new `isChainedBooleanOp` (chainKwIdx token is `||` or `&&`), OR'd into the existing `isChainedFluentCall` condition alongside the `.`/`?.` check, both feeding the same `effectiveSpanIndent = prevEffectiveSpanIndent` branch. | RDD_KEY_217 | Step 3 GRU sample_default.txt / trained weights: exception to RDD_EXT_19's never-commit posture, user-directed | User explicitly directed committing `tools/gru/sample_default.txt` (the classifier-auto-labeled corpus from `make gru-acquire-corpus`) and `code-formatter-ai-assist-weights.json` (the weights trained on it), reversing RDD_EXT_19's general "real extracted/labeled corpora and derived trained-weights artifacts are never committed" posture for these two specific files.
