RDD_EXT_19 itself is NOT retracted or reworded -- it still governs every other real corpus/weights artifact this job produces (Pool A/Pool B candidate files, ad hoc training runs, cross-validation working files, etc.); this is a narrow, named exception for exactly `tools/gru/sample_default.txt` and its trained `code-formatter-ai-assist-weights.json`, the two files this session's `gru-acquire-corpus`/`gru-train` Makefile targets are designed to regenerate and keep committed going forward. |
| RDD_KEY_218 | `JetBrains/kotlin` dogfood, Category 2 (idempotency-only) cluster D4 (minor adjacent-closing-brace spacing flap, `) }` vs `)}`), sample hit `JsArgumentsImpl.kt` -- **FIXED**: `BlockStructureRule.collapseBracelessBody` (the Kotlin-only sibling of `tryCollapse` used for a body that already arrived brace-free and multi-line -- the shape produced once a prior format pass had already stripped an enclosing `if`'s own braces, e.g. reformatting round1's own output) scans forward from the body's start tracking `(`/`[`/`{` nesting depth until it finds either a depth-0 NEWLINE (the statement's own end) or a depth-going-negative `}` (this body was the enclosing scope's last statement); in the latter case it correctly sets `bodyEnd` to that `}` token's own index (excluding the brace itself from the rendered body, so the surrounding caller's normal per-token loop re-emits it verbatim afterward) -- but the WHITESPACE/NEWLINE token immediately preceding that `}` was still included in `contents` (`tokens.subList(bodyStart, bodyEnd)`), and `renderInline` (used to flatten `contents` onto one line) never emits *trailing* whitespace at all (by construction: `pendingSpace` is only flushed when a further real token follows it), so a source-preserved single space between the collapsed body's last real token and the following `}` was silently dropped once this path ran on an already-braceless input -- e.g. `) }` collapsing to `)}`. `tryCollapse`'s sibling braced-body path (the fresh, still-braced first-pass shape) never has this loss: it deletes the `{`/`}` pair being collapsed away, then lets the caller's own untouched loop re-append whatever whitespace token originally followed that now-deleted `}` (a *different* piece of source text than the one lost here), so round1 (still-braced input) renders correctly while round2 (reformatting round1's own now-braceless output) drops the space -- a genuine, reproducible round1-vs-round2 idempotency flap, not a fresh-format bug. Reproduced directly (bypassing the full ~16k-file corpus per this session's scoping instructions) against the real corpus file `compiler/build-tools/kotlin-build-tools-impl/gen/org/jetbrains/kotlin/buildtools/internal/arguments/JsArgumentsImpl.kt` and a minimal standalone repro modeled on its exact shape (a `try { if (...) { longCall(...) } } catch (...) { ... }` where the call's argument list is long enough to force `enforceCallLineBreaking` to wrap it): confirmed round1 renders `    ) }` (closing paren, space, enclosing `}`) while round2 (reformatting round1's own output, where the `if`'s braces are already gone) renders `    )}` (space silently dropped) before the fix, and confirmed both rounds now produce identical `    ) }` after it. Fixed by checking, right after computing `bodyEnd`, whether `bodyEnd` actually points at a `}` (as opposed to running off the end of `tokens`) and whether the token immediately before it is WHITESPACE/NEWLINE; if so, appending one trailing space onto the returned `prefix + " " + body` string instead of leaving it bare. Fixture `real_code_regressions_168` (the minimal `try`/braceless-`if`/wrapped-call repro above). Also required updating the pre-existing `real_code_regressions_33_out.kt` fixture (from `square/kotlinpoet` dogfooding, RDD_KEY_153) -- its own line 18 (`)} as T`) had, by coincidence, already encoded this exact same bug's buggy output as its recorded "expected" result (that fixture's own input never contains an explicitly-braced `if`, so it never exercised `tryCollapse`'s correct braced path at all -- only `collapseBracelessBody`'s buggy one, on every single format, forward pass included, not just idempotency) -- corrected to `) } as T` to match the fix; the file was re-confirmed idempotent (round1 byte-identical to round2) both before and after this line's correction. `make test`: 216/216 forward + idempotency both before and after (before: passing only because the stale `_33_out.kt` reference itself baked in the bug; after: passing because both the fix and the corrected reference now agree), zero regressions. See `STATE_KOTLIN.md`'s Dogfood: JetBrains/kotlin section, cluster D4. |
| RDD_KEY_219 | `JetBrains/kotlin` dogfood, Category 2 (idempotency-only) cluster D1 (declaration/accessor column-alignment padding flap), sample hits near `KlibMetadataVersionWriteExtension` usage and `DummyJavaFileCodeStyleFacadeFactory` -- **PARTIALLY FIXED**: two independent root causes, both in the group-width recompute instability family (RDD_KEY_139/140/162 -- a shared column-alignment pass computes padding based on a group of declarations/one-liners, one member's padded line overflows `lineLengthLimit`, a LATER pipeline phase wraps that member onto multiple lines, and re-parsing that now-multi-line output on the next pass treats it as a hard group boundary, producing a narrower round2 padding decision than round1's). (1) `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule.renderAlignedGroup` (RDD_KEY_162's own budget-aware exclusion mechanism, which excludes a row from the shared column grid if its group-aligned width overflows `lineLengthLimit` AND its initializer is brace-bodied) rendered all surviving (non-excluded) rows as ONE flat shared-width grid even when an excluded row sat in the MIDDLE of the group -- round1 aligned the rows on either side of the gap together, while round2 (once the excluded row becomes genuinely multi-line via `enforceCallLineBreaking` and hard-breaks parsing at that point per this class's own documented `spansMultipleLines` behavior) naturally splits into independently-narrower groups, an idempotency flap. Root-caused via a temporary debug print (removed before commit) tracing `nameCell`/`hasBraceBodiedInit`/exclusion decisions across a targeted round1 vs round2 re-run of `KlibMetadataVersionWriteExtension`-adjacent real-corpus code (never the full ~16k-file corpus, per this session's explicit scoping instructions). Fixed by splitting surviving (non-excluded) rows into maximal CONTIGUOUS runs (with respect to position in the original pre-exclusion group) and rendering each run independently via the existing `renderAlignedGroupRaw`, instead of one flat `safeRows` grid spanning the whole group. (2) The analogous bug in the SHARED (Java/C++/Kotlin) `ScopePipelineCurly.applyGetterSetterPass` driver (`groupOneLiners` -> `excludeOutliers` -> `render`) for Kotlin's own one-liner expression-bodied function/accessor grouping (Section 8/9): when `excludeOutliers`'s width-ratio outlier test (`OUTLIER_RATIO = 3`) removes a member from the middle of a group, the remaining members were rendered as one flat group via `getterSetterRule.render`, the same non-contiguous-run bug as (1). Fixed with a new Kotlin-gated (`lang.isKotlin`) private helper `renderKotlinFilteredRuns`, which splits `filtered` into maximal contiguous runs with respect to `group`'s original member positions and renders each independently, leaving C/C++/Java's existing flat-group `getterSetterRule.render(tokens, filtered)` call completely untouched (zero regression risk to those languages, since no equivalent later phase re-wraps a one-liner accessor's own signature into genuine multi-line form for them the way Kotlin's signature/param-list wrapping does). Reproduced directly against real-corpus-derived code (`DummyJavaFileCodeStyleFacadeFactory`'s `withLanguage` override, whose 111-char raw/solo line length already exceeds `lineLengthLimit` before any group padding, hitting the pre-existing raw-length parse-time pre-check from RDD_KEY_139/140, then re-exposing this contiguous-run bug once its siblings' shared column grid is recomputed around it). Fixture `real_code_regressions_169`. `make test`: 217/217 forward + idempotency both before and after (the fixes were verified via targeted single/few-file re-runs, not by re-running the fix against a stale full-suite count), zero regressions. **Explicitly NOT fixed in this session** (left open, still within D1): a THIRD, distinct sub-shape of the same family, where the offending member's own SOLO/raw width already fits under `lineLengthLimit` (so it is never excluded via the raw-length parse-time pre-check that catches cases (1) and (2) above), but the GROUP's own shared-column padding alone -- once every member's field name/return-type column is stretched to the widest member's own width -- pushes that specific member's padded line over the limit; unlike cases (1)/(2), the member is never excluded from the grid at all (parse-time pre-check never triggers, group-time budget check in `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule` only fires post-padding for the declaration-alignment path, and `KotlinGetterSetterRule`'s one-liner grouping has no equivalent budget-aware exclusion mechanism at all, unlike `renderAlignedGroup`'s own RDD_KEY_162 gate), so a later phase still wraps it as multi-line, still producing an idempotency flap this session's fix does not address. Because D1's ~100-file estimate almost certainly contains an unknown mix of all three sub-shapes, this closure should be treated as PARTIAL, not full -- analogous to how RDD_KEY_214's own D2 fix left a small residual for RDD_KEY_215/216 to pick up. See `STATE_KOTLIN.md`'s Dogfood: JetBrains/kotlin section, cluster D1. |
| RDD_KEY_220 | `JetBrains/kotlin` dogfood, Category 2 (idempotency-only) cluster D1, third sub-shape left open by RDD_KEY_219 -- **FIXED**: group-padding-induced overflow in `KotlinGetterSetterRule`'s one-liner function/accessor grouping, where a member's own solo/raw width fits under `lineLengthLimit` (so neither `parseKotlinOneLinerMember`'s own raw-length pre-check nor `GetterSetterRuleCurly.excludeOutliers`'s body-width-ratio test ever excludes it) but the group's shared-column padding (stretching every row's columns to the group's widest member) alone pushes its rendered width over the limit once actually laid out in the grid. Pre-fix this was silently absorbed by a later, unrelated pass -- `MiscRule.enforceCallLineBreaking` -- wrapping the offending member's call arguments across multiple lines even though the member would have fit on a single line if simply excluded from the shared grid and rendered solo/unpadded instead, an ugly and unnecessary multi-line rewrite purely as a side effect of neighboring-row padding. Root-caused and verified with a minimal hand-reduced 2-member repro (`getAlpha(): VeryLongReturnTypeXX = someShortCall(argOne)` paired with `getBeta(): Int = someFunctionCall(argumentOne, argumentTwo, argumentThreeXYZ)` inside `class Foo { ... }`): confirmed via `git stash` (temporarily reverting to pre-fix `HEAD`) that the unfixed code wraps `getBeta`'s call arguments onto their own indented lines, while `getBeta`'s own raw one-line form is under `lineLengthLimit` when rendered standalone. Fixed by porting RDD_KEY_162's fixed-point budget-exclusion loop (`renderAlignedGroup`'s indent/`lineLengthLimit`-aware repeated-exclusion approach, re-run against the shrinking remaining set until a fixed point, bounded by group size) into `KotlinGetterSetterRule`, gated on `GetterSetterRuleCore.hasBreakableCall` (only a call-bodied member can ever become genuinely multi-line via a later wrap phase, so a member with no breakable call is never excluded purely for padding reasons) -- combined with RDD_KEY_219's own contiguous-run rendering for whichever rows survive exclusion. Implementation: added a new `depth`-aware 3-arg `GetterSetterRuleCurly.render(tokens, group, depth)` (default: ignores `depth`, delegates to the existing 2-arg `render` -- a no-op for C/C++/Java, which have no equivalent later-phase re-wrap risk), `@Override`-d in `KotlinGetterSetterRule` with the real budget-aware fixed-point loop, dispatching between the existing function-shape and accessor-shape raw grid-rendering logic (moved unchanged into new private `renderRaw`/`renderAccessorGroupRaw` helpers) via `isAccessorMember(group.get(0))`; `ScopePipelineCurly.applyGetterSetterPass`/`renderKotlinFilteredRuns` updated to thread `depth` (already available at the call site) through to the new 3-arg override for the Kotlin branch, leaving the C/C++/Java branch's existing 2-arg call untouched. Verified idempotent (round1 == round2, byte-identical) on the minimal repro, and that the fix is load-bearing by re-testing the same repro against pre-fix `HEAD` via `git stash` (bug reproduces: call-argument wrap) versus the fixed working tree (bug resolved: both members render as independent one-liners, no padding applied since only one member survives the fixed-point exclusion in this 2-member repro, no wrap). Fixture `real_code_regressions_170`. `make test`: 218/218 -> 219/219 forward + idempotency, zero regressions. This closes out D1 fully -- all three sub-shapes (RDD_KEY_219's two plus this one) are now fixed. See `STATE_KOTLIN.md`'s Dogfood: JetBrains/kotlin section, cluster D1. |
| RDD_KEY_221 | `JetBrains/kotlin` dogfood, Category 2 (idempotency-only) cluster D3 (multi-line-call/condition wrap-decision flap, ~33 known files incl. `GenerateReleaseNotes.kt`/`TypeBridging.kt`) -- **INVESTIGATED, root cause confirmed, fix NOT landed**: `MiscRuleCurly.renderCallCandidate`'s no-newline-branch fits-check (`final String wholeLineRest = collapseToOneLine(tokens, lineStartIndex(tokens, nameIdx), effectiveLineEndIndex(tokens, closeIdx) - 1);`) measures a call/condition candidate's fit against the length of its entire ENCLOSING PHYSICAL SOURCE LINE (via `lineStartIndex(tokens, nameIdx)`, which walks back to the start of whatever physical line `nameIdx` sits on) rather than a position tied to the candidate's own stable content -- so a small nested candidate (e.g. `startsWith('@')` inside a longer `val text = ...` line) gets measured against the whole enclosing line's length, flapping between wrapped/unwrapped across formatting rounds as that enclosing line's own length changes between round1 (original source) and round2 (round1's own reformatted output). Confirmed reproducer: `GenerateReleaseNotes.kt`/`TypeBridging.kt` from the `/tmp/jb_kotlin_kt/kotlin-master` checkout. Candidate fix tried: anchor the measurement at `nameIdx` itself (the candidate's own start) instead of `lineStartIndex(tokens, nameIdx)` -- same "stable position tied to the candidate" principle as `effectiveCallBaseIndent`'s existing Kotlin merge-detection carve-out in the same file. This candidate fix REGRESSED 28 existing fixtures at `make test` (C++: `real_code_regressions_1`; Java: `_3`/`_10`/`_55`/`_56`/`_128`; C/C++ headers: `_12`/`_16`/`_76`/`_119`/`_121`; TS: `_81`/`_85`/`_93`/`_96`/`_141`/`_142`; Kotlin: `_18`/`_22`/`_27`/`_33`/`_44`/`_45`/`_46`/`_64`/`_148`/`_156`/`_169`) -- dropping the enclosing line's leading prefix text (`return `, `if (`, `val x = `, an outer statement's own lead-in, etc.) from the measurement under-counts real rendered line length whenever such a prefix is present, so lines that should wrap under the line-length limit no longer do, across all four curly-family languages, not just Kotlin. Per this session's explicit instruction not to force through or hack around a wide regression surface, the fix was reverted (change discarded via `git checkout --`, confirmed `make test` back to 219/219 forward + idempotency, zero diff from before this investigation started) and NOT committed. Documented instead as a `README.md` "Known Limitations" bullet and left open in `STATE_KOTLIN.md`'s D3 tracking -- a real fix needs a more careful design distinguishing "prefix belongs to an unrelated outer construct, safe to exclude" from "prefix is part of this same rendered line and must count toward the limit," plus a full regression review across all four languages' fixtures, which is a larger, riskier undertaking than this investigation's scope. |
| RDD_KEY_222 | `MiscRuleCore.computeLineCommentGroups`'s §15 consecutive-`//`-comment grouping (RDD_KEY_89) capitalized every group member's first letter independently, per-line -- so a genuine multi-line `//` comment (e.g. `// this function must be...` / `// because the design requires it.`) got its SECOND (and later) lines wrongly capitalized too, unlike the equivalent `/* */` block-comment path (`stripSoleTrailingPeriodAcrossLines` + single `capitalizeFirstLetter` call on content line 0 only), which was already correct. FIXED by capitalizing only `contents.get(0)` before populating `result`, matching the block-comment path exactly. Applies to every Curly-family language (`Lang.isCurly` = C/C++/Java/Kotlin/JS/TS, all sharing `FormatterCurly`/`MiscRuleCore`); Python3/data-formats/XML/HTML5 have no §15 comment-normalization pass at all, so no change needed there. This surfaced a second, previously-latent bug in the SAME grouping logic: `nextCommentChainLinkIfAdjacent` chained a trailing end-of-line comment (one following real code on its own physical line, e.g. `const x = 1; // greeting label`) onto a following line's standalone `//` comment, treating them as one logical prose block for capitalization purposes even though they are logically unrelated (one annotates a statement, the other starts a fresh comment). Confirmed via `test/js_comments_inp.js` (`// greeting label` / `// nullish fallback to zero`) and `test/ts_comments_inp.ts` (`// unique identifier` / `// display label`) regressing after the first fix landed. FIXED with a new `isStandaloneCommentLine` helper (true iff the comment is alone on its line, only `WHITESPACE` back to a `NEWLINE`/start-of-tokens); `nextCommentChainLinkIfAdjacent` now returns -1 immediately if the token at `idx` is not standalone, so a trailing comment can still be capitalized/period-stripped on its own (as a size-1 group) but never extends the chain onto the next line. Verified via three additional scratchpad repros (standalone single-line `//` still capitalizes normally; a genuine 2+-line group's line 2+ stays uncapitalized; a trailing-comment-then-standalone-comment pair is no longer wrongly merged) before touching any fixture. 26 pre-existing `*_out` fixtures (Java/C/C++/Kotlin/TS across `real_code_regressions_*`, `c_cpp_decl_gaps`, `java_format_toggle`, `java_preprocessor_method`, `js_comments`, `ts_comments`) had hand-authored the old buggy per-line capitalization as "expected" and were updated to match; `make test`: 219/219 forward + idempotency after both fixes, zero unexpected diffs (every changed fixture line reviewed individually against the expected "continuation line loses wrong capitalization, first line/trailing comment unchanged" pattern). |
| RDD_KEY_223 | `apache/ant` `manual/running.html` HTML5 dogfood (STATE_DATA_FORMATS.md's Open Questions item 2, full scoping pass done 2026-07-31) -- **FIXED**: `XmlSpecificRule.parseNodes`'s `stopAtCloseTag`-branch unconditionally broke out of the children-parsing loop on ANY closing tag, even a genuine orphan (a closing tag matching no open element anywhere on the path to the document root, e.g. a stray `</p>` with no open `<p>` at all -- a different shape from RDD_KEY_204, which only covers an *open* `<p>` implicitly closed by a following sibling *start* tag). Control returned to the enclosing `parseElement` for `<body>`, whose own closing-tag check failed and fell into the existing HTML5 tolerant-close fallback (added for WPT's `charset/after-bogus.html` mismatched-tag case) -- WITHOUT consuming the orphan `</p>` token -- so `<body>` tolerant-closed right there, then the same still-unconsumed `</p>` cascaded the identical tolerant-close onto `<html>` one level up. Confirmed via a minimal fixture and via `grep` on the real formatted `running.html`: the actual serialized output closed `</body></html>` at the orphan `</p>` and dumped the rest of the real document (hundreds of lines, through genuine end-of-`<body>`) as raw top-level siblings after `</html>`, outside the document root entirely -- a materially bigger structural corruption than the "1 `<p>` lost" `html_content_diff.py`'s `parse5`-based spec-tolerant reparse originally reported (its own recovery re-parents post-`</html>` content back inside `<body>` almost transparently, masking the true magnitude; noted for whoever next touches that script). FIXED by adding `XmlSpecificRule.openTagStack` (a `Deque<String>` of currently-open lowercased tag names, pushed in `parseElement` right before parsing an element's children and popped via an enclosing `try`/`finally` covering every return path -- matched close, implied-close-trigger, and tolerant-close fallback alike) and a new `peekCloseTagNameLower` helper; `parseNodes`'s `stopAtCloseTag` branch now breaks (cascade-close, unchanged) only if the closing tag's name is found anywhere in `openTagStack` (current element or a real ancestor) when `lang.isHtml5`, and otherwise discards the stray tag in place and continues the same children-parsing loop (mirroring the existing top-level-only stray-tag discard) instead of returning control to the caller -- gated on `lang.isHtml5` so strict XML/XHTML keeps its original unconditional-break/throw behavior unchanged. Deliberately additive, not a replacement: every existing "mismatched tag cascades to close ancestors" fixture (the `charset/after-bogus.html` mechanism) is unaffected since a real ancestor name is still found in the stack; only the previously-unhandled "matches nothing at all" case changes, from "wrongly tolerant-close every ancestor up to the document root without consuming the tag" to "discard the orphan tag and keep parsing in place." Per-spec note (documented, not implemented): the true HTML5 "p end tag" algorithm synthesizes an empty `<p></p>` at an orphan `</p>` rather than pure discard; pure discard-only was chosen to match this formatter's existing "preserve as written, don't fabricate tags" posture (RDD_KEY_185) -- as a result, `html_content_diff.py` against `running.html` still reports the same pre-existing "1 `<p>` lost" (`<body>` child count 82 vs 81) as a known, accepted, unchanged gap; what the fix actually resolves is the much larger structural corruption (content serialized outside `</html>`), confirmed fixed by direct inspection of the reformatted file (`</body></html>` now correctly at the true end of the document, nothing after). New fixtures `real_code_regressions_173` (minimal orphan-`</p>` repro) and `real_code_regressions_174` (regression guard: an unclosed `<bogus>` tag proving the mismatched-tag-cascades-to-ancestor path via a `</div>` closing tag still works unchanged). Full `apache/ant` `manual/` 226-file corpus re-run: 226/226 forward + idempotency, 226/226 `html_syntax_check.sh` clean, content-diff shows only the expected accepted `running.html` gap plus 4 pre-existing unrelated comment-capitalization mismatches (`Tasks/antlr.html`/`imageio.html`/`attrib.html`/`image.html`, confirmed identical against a pre-fix baseline build, not a regression from this change). `make test`: 221/221 -> 223/223 forward + idempotency, zero regressions. |
| RDD_KEY_224 | `apache/ant` `manual/` HTML5 dogfood re-verification of `real_code_regressions_125`'s 4 "benign comment-capitalization-only" diffs (`Tasks/antlr.html`, `Tasks/imageio.html`, `Tasks/attrib.html`, `Tasks/image.html`) -- re-checked directly with `html_content_diff.sh`, found 2 of the 4 (`antlr.html`, `attrib.html`, 3 total mismatches across both) were NOT benign prose capitalization but genuine commented-out HTML markup corruption: `<!--tr> <td>fork</td>...</tr-->` (and a second `<tr>` block in `attrib.html`) and `<!--p>By default...</p-->` had their leading tag-fragment token (`tr>`/`p>`) capitalized to `Tr>`/`P>` by `normalize-comment-start-case`, since the comment's content is a markup fragment (author's `<!--`/`<` comment boundary landed mid-tag) not an English sentence. **FIXED**: new `XmlSpecificRule.isMarkupFragmentDirective(text)` -- true iff `text`'s leading run of lowercase letters is immediately followed by `>` (no interior whitespace) AND that run is a member of a new closed `MARKUP_FRAGMENT_TAG_NAMES` set of real HTML5 tag names (not "any lowercase word immediately followed by `>`", to avoid false positives on a coincidental short word) -- wired into `normComment` alongside the existing `isSingleWordDirective` check (same `||` skip-capitalization condition), same file/pattern as the two directly analogous precedents (CSS's `isSingleTokenDirective` from `real_code_regressions_69`, HTML5's own `isSingleWordDirective` from the WordPress magic-comment case) but a distinct shape from both -- not a single directive-shaped token, not a single word, specifically a leading bare tag-name-open fragment. Evidence gathering before committing to the rule: reproduced all 4 original diffs directly via `html_content_diff.sh` against fresh copies of the 4 files from the existing `/tmp/ant` checkout; grepped `apache/ant manual/` plus the still-extant `WordPress/wordpress-develop` and `alexandersandberg/html5-elements-tester` checkouts for `<!--[a-z]+>` and found exactly 2 `<!--tr>` + 1 `<!--p>` occurrences corpus-wide, zero unrelated/false-positive-shaped hits -- confirming the tag-name-set restriction costs nothing in practice. The other 2 of the original 4 (`Tasks/imageio.html`/`Tasks/image.html`, both flagging the identical string `attributes inherited from MatchingTask` -> `Attributes inherited from MatchingTask`) were separately confirmed via grep to be a genuine, unrelated doc-authoring convention (the exact same sentence-shaped comment reused verbatim across both files, sitting between `</tr>`/`<tr>` as a section-boundary annotation, with no unclosed/fragment tag anywhere in it) -- correctly falls through the new check (no `>` immediately after its first word) and is left as ordinary, expected capitalization, not a bug. New fixture `test/real_code_regressions_175_{inp,out}.html` (both markup-fragment shapes, `tr>`/`p>`, in one file). `make test`: 223/223 -> 224/224 forward + idempotency, zero regressions. Full `apache/ant manual/` 226-file corpus re-run: 226/226 forward + idempotency, 226/226 `html_syntax_check.sh` clean; content-diff mismatches dropped from 4 to 3 -- `antlr.html`/`attrib.html` now clean, `imageio.html`/`image.html` unchanged (confirmed expected/non-bug per above), `running.html` unchanged (RDD_KEY_223's accepted residual "1 `<p>` lost" gap, confirmed identical child-count mismatch as before this change). |
| RDD_KEY_225 | `jenkinsci/jenkins` `hudson/PluginManager.java` real-code repro (`STATE_C_CPP_JAVA.md`'s "Known Gaps -- Open" entry: `doPluginsSearch`'s `sitePlugins` stream-chain declaration produced a ~1992-character single physical line, differently on every round, no line ever staying under `lineLengthLimit`) -- root cause was NOT in `MiscRuleCurly.enforceCallLineBreaking` (already correctly bails via `topLevelArgs.size() <= 1 -> return null` for a call whose sole argument is a lambda, ruled out by direct code reading) but in `ScopePipelineCurly.applyDeclarationsPass` -> ENTIRE initializer (via `significantOnly`-filtered `initTokens`, which never contains a NEWLINE token regardless of the original source's line count) onto one physical line with no line-length check and no multi-line-render path at all -- so by the time `enforceCallLineBreaking` runs afterward, the original multi-line structure of an embedded multi-statement `.map(x -> { ... })`-style lambda body is already destroyed, and nothing downstream can safely re-wrap an arbitrary already-flattened lambda body back into statements. **FIXED** (narrow, targeted -- explicitly NOT the larger "general scope-depth reindentation" architectural rewrite tracked separately in `STATE_COMMON.md`'s TODOs): new bail-out in `DeclarationAlignmentRuleCurly.parseDeclaration`, analogous to its two existing pre-flight "can't safely collapse, don't try" aggregate-init bails (trailing-`}` non-flat, `//`-commented, over-length-flat), that locates the initializer's token span by identity in the ORIGINAL unfiltered `stmt` list (a new `rawSliceBetweenUnfiltered` helper -- needed because the pre-existing `rawSliceBetween` helper's javadoc claims "raw ... comments and all" but its actual implementation, confirmed by reading it, ALSO strips WHITESPACE/NEWLINE tokens, same as `significantOnly`) and a new `containsMultilineBraceBody` helper (simple `List<Boolean>` per-open-brace-level stack, marking every currently-open level as "saw a NEWLINE" and returning true if any level's flag was set when its matching `}` closed) -- if any brace pair inside the initializer originally spanned more than one physical source line at any nesting depth, `parseDeclaration` returns `null` and the whole statement is left completely untouched, preserving its original (already well-formatted, since it came from real multi-line source) formatting so `enforceCallLineBreaking` and everything else downstream can still operate on it normally. Debugged per `STATE_COMMON.md`'s evidence-over-reasoning methodology with 3 rounds of temporary `System.err.println` debug markers (all removed once confirmed) -- the first two fix attempts silently no-op'd because of the two NEWLINE-stripping pitfalls above (`significantOnly`-derived `initTokens`, then the also-stripping `rawSliceBetween`) before landing on the working `rawSliceBetweenUnfiltered`-based approach. Verified against the durable repro (`~/Projects/JxMake/0_excluded_directory/formatter_repro_fixtures/jenkinsci_jenkins_PluginManager.java`): round1 == round2 == round3 (byte-identical, fully idempotent); the `sitePlugins` statement's own lines are no longer flattened, though a handful of the statement's OTHER pre-existing lines (from its `.filter(plugin -> {...})` lambda body, e.g. 148/135/132/131 chars) remain over `lineLengthLimit` because they were already over-length in the ORIGINAL unformatted GitHub source itself (confirmed by grepping the original repro file directly) and the fix's "leave completely untouched" bail-out, by design, cannot and must not attempt to re-wrap an arbitrary already-long line inside a bailed-out multi-line lambda body -- that re-wrap capability is exactly the larger architectural rewrite this fix was explicitly told to avoid. This surfaced that 3 pre-existing, previously-PASSing fixtures (`real_code_regressions_57`/`129`/`130`) had themselves hand-encoded this exact bug class as "expected" output (flattened lambda bodies producing 199-, 134-, and 296-character lines respectively, all over the 100-char default `lineLengthLimit`), unnoticed because those fixtures were originally authored to validate unrelated, narrower bugs (`57`: RDD_KEY_57's `isCStyleCastClose` control-flow exclusion; `129`/`130`: `openrewrite/rewrite` dogfood cluster fixes) -- per `test/README.txt`'s own stated convention ("The *_out files are the ground truth. If a formatter fix intentionally changes the output for a rule, update the corresponding *_out file in the same commit") and the directly analogous RDD_KEY_222 precedent, all 3 `_out` files were updated to match the new, correctly-multi-line (and for 129/130, now fully under-limit) output, each re-verified idempotent before updating. New fixture `test/real_code_regressions_176_{inp,out}.java` (minimal 2-stage `.filter(...).map(...)` stream chain, each stage a multi-statement brace-bodied lambda, faithful to the real `PluginManager.java` shape). `make test`: 224/224 -> 225/225 forward + idempotency, zero regressions. |
| RDD_KEY_226 | `JetBrains/kotlin` dogfood cluster D3 -- **design from the 2026-07-31 scoping session (RDD_KEY_221's `statementStartIndex`/depth-0-`;`/`{`/`}` backward-scan anchor, Kotlin-gated at `MiscRuleCurly.renderCallCandidate`'s no-newline fits-check) IMPLEMENTED, VALIDATED, and REVERTED -- the design's own documented "Known open risk" materialized and is a real, confirmed blocker.** The dedicated synthetic multi-arm `when {}` stress fixture (short arm immediately followed by a long arm, no `;` between them, plus the grounded `EqualityAndComparisonCallsTransformer.kt` `when`-arm shape) passed clean, round1==round2 -- but the full `make test` suite regressed 16 Kotlin fixtures (`kt_combined_inp.kt`, `kt_comments_inp.kt`, `real_code_regressions_{20,22,27,32,33,39,44,45,46,62,156,165,169,170}_inp.kt`), zero regressions in C/C++/Java/TS confirming the `lang.isKotlin` gate itself worked as intended. Root cause of the regression: Kotlin statements are ordinarily NEWLINE-separated, not `;`-separated (`;` is optional and usually absent after `stripOptionalSemicolons`, RDD_KEY_115) -- so `statementStartIndex`'s backward scan, which only stops at a depth-0 `;`/`{`/`}`, walks straight past the current statement's own true start and merges an entire UNRELATED PRECEDING SIBLING STATEMENT's text into the measurement whenever consecutive statements in the same scope have no `;` between them (the common case). Concrete minimal repro, `real_code_regressions_20_inp.kt`: `val display = (if (warning != null) "$warning\n\n" else "") + "Done"` immediately followed by `showMessage(context, display)` inside the same function body -- `showMessage(...)`'s own fits-check backward-scans past its own line, past the NEWLINE, all the way back through the entire preceding `val display = ...` statement (no `;`, no `{`/`}` in between) to the function body's opening `{`, inflating the measured "line" to include both statements' text and pushing it over `lineLengthLimit`, producing a false-positive wrap (`showMessage(\n    context, display\n)`) where the correct one-line render was previously produced correctly. This is exactly the design's own predicted failure mode (`STATE_KOTLIN.md`'s 2026-07-31 session, "Known open risk" subsection) -- confirmed, not merely theorized. Per this session's explicit bail-out instruction, the source change was reverted in full (`git checkout --` on `MiscRuleCurly.java` only, no other file touched), `make test` reconfirmed clean at 225/225 forward + idempotency (the green baseline had moved from 219/219 as of RDD_KEY_220's note to 225/225 by RDD_KEY_225 in the interim; both before and after this session's revert are 225/225, zero net change). No fixture added (nothing to register -- the fix was never landed). D3 remains open; a real fix needs actual Kotlin statement-boundary tracking beyond bracket-depth-plus-`;`/`{`/`}` (i.e. also recognizing a depth-0 NEWLINE that ends a statement, distinguished from a depth-0 NEWLINE that's merely mid-wrap inside the current candidate's own already-broken rendering) -- closer to `STATE_COMMON.md`'s "General scope-depth reindentation" architectural TODO's territory than a self-contained fix, per that section's own anticipation. |
| RDD_KEY_227 | `STATE_CURLY_GDR.md` `JXM_CFMT_GDR 0`/`1` directive semantics (not yet implemented -- design decision only, precedes any code). Three sub-questions resolved by the user: (1) **Flat toggle, not nesting** -- a single `1` always re-enables regardless of how many `0`s preceded it; a redundant `0` while already disabled is a harmless no-op. Simpler than a depth counter and matches `JXM_CFMT_CFG`'s existing simplicity precedent. (2) **Unmatched trailing `0` at EOF is neither a hard error nor an implicit restore -- it's a moot case, not a real one.** The user's own reasoning: there is nothing left to format past EOF for the current file, so "re-enabling" has no observable effect either way; the next file (if any) starts fresh from its own default config / env var / CLI option / in-file config, entirely unaffected by a prior file's unclosed directive. Implementation implication: no EOF-specific error path or restore logic is needed at all for this case -- simply let the disabled state fall out of scope when the file ends, same as any other end-of-input condition. (3) **Silent no-op when `curly-general-scope-reindent` is off** -- `JXM_CFMT_GDR 0`/`1` parses without error even when the feature it toggles is globally disabled; this lets a file be prepared for GDR ahead of a project-wide flag flip without penalty. |
| RDD_KEY_228 | `STATE_CURLY_GDR.md` scope expansion, requested directly by the user: **JS/TS are also curly-brace-family, and must be reindentable by the GDR pre-pass** -- the original Scoping section's "curly-brace-family (C/C++/Java/Kotlin)" text (written before this session) is superseded; JS/TS are now in-scope, HTML/data-formats remain explicitly out of scope (structurally indent-based, not brace-depth-based). Implementation: `GdrPipelineGate.isCurlyFamily` gained `"js"`/`"ts"`; `GdrTokenizer` gained `scanTemplateLiteral` (a new backtick-delimited `` ` `` template-literal scanner, symmetric to the existing `scanQuoted`/raw-string handling but allowing embedded newlines since a template literal legitimately spans multiple physical lines) so that `${...}` interpolation content doesn't get misread as real brace/paren/bracket depth -- confirmed via a direct smoke test with a template literal containing an interpolated string. Known, documented, NOT-yet-fixed gap: a JS/TS regex literal (e.g. `/[{]/`) is still tokenized as ordinary `TEXT`, not recognized as a string-like unit, so any bracket-family character inside a regex literal will still be miscounted for depth -- deferred as a follow-up, not blocking this session's scope-expansion work since it did not appear in the corpora exercised so far. Separately, wired the same `GdrPipelineGate.apply` call into `XmlSpecificRule.renderScriptOrStyle`'s existing JS-dispatch call site for embedded HTML `<script>` content (the exact gap that same method's own pre-existing javadoc on `dedent` already named: "this formatter preserves original relative indentation rather than re-deriving it from brace depth (STATE_COMMON.md's 'General scope-depth reindentation' gap)") -- confirmed via `test/html_js_flush_left_inp.html` (with `curly-general-scope-reindent=on` set via the top-of-file `<!--% JXM_CFMT_CFG ... -->` in-file directive) that a flushed-left embedded `<script>` body is now correctly reindented relative to its enclosing function, whereas before this fix it stayed flush left regardless of the enclosing HTML's own (already-correct) indentation. Separately, while testing this fixture, found and fixed a real, independent HTML-formatter bug (not GDR-specific): `XmlSpecificRule.parseCommentOrFrozen`/its COMMENT-node render path (`<!-- ` + text + ` -->`) unconditionally inserted a space immediately after `<!--` for EVERY ordinary HTML comment, including a `%`-prefixed marker/directive comment such as `<!--% JXM_CFMT_CFG ... -->` -- turning it into `<!-- % JXM_CFMT_CFG ... -->` on format, which permanently breaks the marker's required exact `<!--%`-with-no-space prefix on any subsequent parse (both `InFileConfig.java`'s own regex and this same file's DIS/ENA exact-literal-string checks require it). Only the DIS/ENA markers were previously protected (handled as a fully separate `FROZEN` node type, verbatim byte-for-byte); a bare `JXM_CFMT_CFG` directive expressed in HTML-comment form had no equivalent protection and fell through to ordinary comment rendering. FIXED with a new `Node.commentVerbatim` boolean: `parseCommentOrFrozen` sets it (and stores the untrimmed, un-normalized inner text) whenever the trimmed inner content starts with `%`, and the COMMENT render case checks it to render with zero added inner spacing (`<!--` + text + `-->`) instead of the normal `<!-- ` + text + ` -->`. Confirmed via `--standalone --diff`: the marker line no longer appears as a spurious remove/re-add in the diff, and `--standalone --check` reports idempotent. `make test`: 228/228 forward + idempotency, zero regressions (both `test/java_flush_left_*`/`test/html_js_flush_left_*` fixtures re-verified/regenerated against the real CLI, `curly_general_scope_reindent_*` and every other existing fixture unaffected). |
| RDD_KEY_229 | `STATE_CURLY_GDR.md` real-code test (checklist item, previously never executed against any corpus) run against the 3 `angular/angular` TS dogfood cluster-5 "accepted gap, no action planned" files (`user_metric_spec.ts`, `emit.ts`, `i18n_parse.ts` -- see `STATE_JS_TS.md`), `curly-general-scope-reindent=on` -- **1 of 3 fixed as-is (`emit.ts`); 2 of 3 (`user_metric_spec.ts`, `i18n_parse.ts`) exposed a real, confirmed GDR ordering bug, NOT FIXED, decision made to document only, no code change this session (user: "too dangerous").** Root cause: GDR's pre-pass computes each line's structural-depth indent target BEFORE the normal pipeline's brace-placement pass runs; for source using one-true-brace-style joins (`} else if (...) {`, `} catch (...) {` etc. on the closing line), the pre-pass measures depth for that ONE physical line as it exists pre-split, but brace-placement later splits it into separate `}` / `else if (...) {` lines to match this formatter's Allman style -- the newly split-out line never gets its own GDR-computed target, producing wrong/non-idempotent indentation (confirmed via direct repro: `user_metric_spec.ts`'s `} else if (...) {` -> garbled mixed-depth output). **A second ordering was tried and also confirmed broken, not landed:** running GDR as a POST-pass (after the normal pipeline instead of before) does fix the else-split case (`user_metric_spec.ts` becomes idempotent, confirmed via a direct `GdrRewriter.rewrite` harness call on already-pipelined output) but introduces a NEW non-idempotency for `emit.ts`/`i18n_parse.ts`: GDR's post-pass indentation change alters a line's column width, which flips the pipeline's own line-wrap fits-check decision (`MiscRuleCurly.renderCallCandidate`) on the next round, since that round's wrap decision is now measured against the previous round's GDR-adjusted indentation rather than the original -- a genuine circular dependency between GDR's structural-depth indent and the pipeline's width-based wrap decisions, confirmed by direct round1/round2 diffs showing call-wrap flapping that did not occur under the original pre-pass ordering. Two remediation options were scoped (iterate pipeline+GDR to a bounded fixpoint; or feed GDR's precomputed indent into the wrap fits-check instead of the line's raw current indentation) but explicitly NOT attempted -- user judged both too risky for this session, matching `STATE_CURLY_GDR.md`'s own Background-section warning that pass-ordering interactions are this job's single riskiest unsolved dimension. **No source code changed as a result of this investigation.** `README.md`'s "Known Limitations" GDR bullet updated to describe the joined-brace-style gap in user-facing terms (does not reference this key or any `STATE*.md` file). `STATE_JS_TS.md`'s cluster 5 entry and `STATE_CURLY_GDR.md`'s checklist updated to record the finding and point future work at this key. |
| RDD_KEY_230 | `STATE_HTML5_TCG.md` insertion-mode state design (tc gap job, item 2, design session 2026-08-02) | **No generic insertion-mode enum/frame stack** -- rejected in favor of three independent, narrow pieces of state each threaded like the existing `openTagStack` precedent (instance field; `try`/`finally` push-pop where stack-shaped, plain scan-helper where not). Gap 3 (implicit `<body>`): single `boolean bodyInserted` flag, no stack. Gap 1 (foster-parenting): detection via `isInTableInsertionMode()`, a top-down `openTagStack` scan returning true if `table` is found before `td`/`th`/`caption` (those three switch to spec modes that behave like "in body" and do not foster-parent) -- same shape as `peekCloseTagNameLower`; relocation via **Option B** (side-channel field checked by the immediate caller in `parseNodes`), not Option A (changing `parseElement`'s return type to carry a list) -- Option A's blast radius (every `parseElement` call site needing a list-or-node check) judged disproportionate for a WPT-pathological-only construct, whereas Option B matches existing side-channel precedents (`openTagStack`, `attachTrailingCommentIfAny`'s `pos` save/restore); shape is a named wrapper class, not a raw nested generic -- `private static final class FosterBuffer { final List<Node> nodes = new ArrayList<>(); }` backing `private final Deque<FosterBuffer> fosterBufferStack`, pushed/popped in `parseElement` alongside `openTagStack` on `<table>` open/close, `Deque`-of-buffers (not one flat list) so a fostered node inside a nested `<table>` doesn't clobber an outer buffer, `fosterBufferStack.isEmpty()` asserted at the end of `format()` as a leak guard. Gap 2 (form pointer): single `Node currentFormElementPointer` field set/cleared around `<template>` boundaries via the same `try`/`finally` pattern as `openTagStack` -- reassess whether nesting needs a `Deque` instead when this level is actually implemented, not assumed up front. **New config key `html5-tc-gap-level`** (int, default `0` = current behavior/no gaps active, cumulative levels `1`-`4` strictly ordered simplest-to-most-complex: `1`=gap 3 implicit `<body>`, `2`=+gap 1 foster-parenting, `3`=+gap 2 form pointer, `4`=+gap 4 adoption agency), gated by this config value alone with no additional `lang.isHtml5 &&` check on new code paths (the config only takes effect where `lang.isHtml5` already applies elsewhere in the pipeline). `README.md` and `STATE_COMMON.md` to be updated with the new config key's documentation once level 1 lands (`STATE_HTML5_TCG.md` checklist item 2a) -- not done yet, no config exists in code as of this key landing (design-only decision, no implementation). |
| RDD_KEY_231 | User-improved `test/java_combined_inp.java` fixture (`evaluateAt`/`evaluateAt2`) surfaced 2 bugs, confirmed to also affect C/C++/JS/TS declaration-initializer rendering (Kotlin already immune, see below): (a) `DeclarationAlignmentRuleCore.needsSpaceBetween`'s strictly pairwise (prev, cur) signature has no notion of unary-vs-binary `+`/`-`, so both `renderTokens` (used directly by JS/TS's `JsTsDeclarationAlignmentRule` for init-token rendering) and `renderInitTokens` (used by C/C++/Java) unconditionally inserted a space after a leading unary sign (`int aaa = +1;` -> `= + 1`) and, once the fix's first cut only recognized IDENTIFIER/NUMBER/`)`/`]` as "this must be binary" evidence, also broke a genuine binary `+` following a STRING/CHAR operand (`"ch" + i` -> `"ch" +i`). `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule` already had an identical, Kotlin-scoped fix (`isUnaryMinusOperand` + a `renderTokens` override) from an earlier `square/okio` dogfood session, whose own javadoc explicitly flagged that C/C++/Java's `renderInitTokens` path was "untouched" -- this is that flagged gap, now closed. FIXED by promoting `isUnaryMinusOperand` (STRING/CHAR added to the binary-operand-evidence set) from `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule` up to `DeclarationAlignmentRuleCore` (`protected`), consulted by both `renderTokens` and `renderInitTokens`; `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule`'s now-redundant override + private duplicate removed, it inherits the base behavior unchanged. (b) Independent second bug, only visible once (a) was fixed and the fixture's `evaluateAt2` (declarations mixing a `final`-modified sibling with unmodified siblings in one alignment group) rendered correctly enough to expose it: `ScopePipelineCurly.applyDeclarationsPass`'s idempotency-strip heuristic (comment at the site already named the risk) blindly stripped `freshPad` trailing spaces off the raw leading gap whenever `trailingSpaces >= freshPad`, with no way to distinguish a genuine re-format (previous pass's own self-padding on top of an already-valid indent) from a first-time format whose true indent simply happens to be numerically >= the modifier-column pad width (here, an 8-space method-body indent vs. a 6-wide `"final "` column) -- silently ate a real indent level, then `normalizeIndent` rounded the resulting non-multiple remainder back up to the wrong level (8 -> stripped to 2 -> rounded to 4). FIXED by only accepting the strip when its result is already `indentWidth`-aligned (a genuine re-format's stripped remainder is always already a multiple, having been written by a previous pass; a false-positive first-time-format overlap generally is not) -- `evaluateAt2`'s `int`/`final int` group now indents at the correct 8/14-column split. `make test`: 228/228 forward + idempotency, zero regressions. |
| RDD_KEY_232 | HTML5/XML multi-line `<!-- -->` comment verbatim preservation (resolves the "HTML5 multi-line `<!-- -->` comments collapse to a tight single/pulled-up form" open question in `STATE_DATA_FORMATS.md`, added 2026-08-03). **Detection:** a comment is "multi-line" iff its raw interior (the text between `<!--` and `-->`, checked BEFORE any `.trim()`) contains a newline; single-line comments (no interior newline, regardless of internal spacing/dashes) are unaffected and still go through the existing normal path (trim, `normalize-comment-start-case`, `isSingleWordDirective`, etc.). **Scope:** only the comment's interior content freezes byte-for-byte verbatim -- the comment node's own placement/indentation (where `<!--` starts), and reindentation of sibling nodes immediately before/after, are both unaffected, since only content extraction/rendering changed, not the node-tree/indentation logic; comment-boundary detection (`s.indexOf("-->", pos + 4)`) is unchanged. **Implementation:** `XmlSpecificRule.parseCommentOrFrozen` now captures the raw (pre-trim) interior string; if it contains `\n` (and is not already a `%`-prefixed directive comment, which keeps its own pre-existing verbatim handling), it reuses the EXISTING `Node.commentVerbatim` boolean + verbatim `commentText` field (originally added for `%`-prefixed marker comments) rather than inventing a parallel mechanism -- the existing `COMMENT` render case already emits `<!--` + text + `-->` with zero added spacing when `commentVerbatim` is set, which is exactly the behavior a frozen multi-line comment also needs. No `JXM_CFMT_DIS`-specific semantics leak in since `commentVerbatim` on the `COMMENT` node type is a pure render-spacing flag, unrelated to the separate `FROZEN` node type DIS/ENA spans use. Shared `XmlSpecificRule.java` code path covers both XML and HTML5 (RDD_KEY_188 one-class-two-languages scoping). New fixture `test/html_multiline_comment_verbatim_{inp,out}.html` (copyright-block case, sibling `<p>` before/after the frozen comment, and a single-line comment discriminator, all reindented/normalized correctly per normal rules while only the frozen comment's interior stays byte-for-byte). Every pre-existing fixture whose expected `_out` file had pinned the old collapsed-form bug in a copyright-header comment (`html_comments`, all `html_tc_gap_level*`, `real_code_regressions_74/91/92/112/125/173/174/175`, `xml_comments`) had its `_out` file updated to the new, correct verbatim output -- each diff verified to be exactly that one change before updating, nothing else affected. `make test`: 237/237 forward + idempotency, zero unintended regressions. |
