| RDD_KEY_235 | Kotlin dogfood cluster **D3** (multi-line-call/condition wrap-decision flap) revisited per the "D3 fold" section's plan, 2026-08-03 session -- **does NOT resolve itself when `curly-general-scope-reindent`/`curly-general-scope-reindent-multipass` are turned on; negative result, no code changed.** Tested the grounded repro (`/tmp/d3_test.kt`, reused from the prior D3 sessions) and the real source file (`EqualityAndComparisonCallsTransformer.kt` in the existing `/tmp/kotlin-master` clone) under three configurations: default (GDR off), `curly-general-scope-reindent=on` alone, and both GDR flags on (multipass). All three remain non-idempotent on both files. Under GDR-off and GDR-single-pass, the flap is the full original shape (round1 wraps `transformCompareToMethodCall(call)` onto its own line, round2 un-wraps it back onto one line). Under multipass, the *symptom* narrows to a pure indentation drift on the same already-wrapped closing `)` line (8 vs 12 spaces in the minimal repro; a similar drift plus a downstream `else` arm's alignment-padding column shift in the real file) rather than a full wrap/unwrap flap -- interesting but still a real non-idempotency, not a fix. Root-caused why GDR cannot reach this bug even though it's a general-scope-depth reindentation job: `MiscRuleCurly.renderCallCandidate`'s no-newline fits-check volatility (confirmed by `RDD_KEY_221`) is driven by what a *sibling wrap candidate on the same logical statement, inside the same pipeline pass/round*, does to the physical line `lineStartIndex` walks back to -- an interaction entirely internal to one `formatOne` pipeline pass. GDR's pre-pass/post-pass placement (even doubled via multipass) only ever runs *between* whole pipeline passes, rewriting leading whitespace on the pass's *output*; it has no visibility into, and cannot influence, a wrap decision made *by* a single pipeline pass while it's still running against its own sibling candidates. Confirms the "D3 fold" section's own original conclusion (a real fix needs actual Kotlin statement-boundary/structural-depth tracking consulted directly inside `MiscRuleCurly.renderCallCandidate`'s own fits-check, i.e. built as a fix to that method using GDR's infrastructure as a library, not achieved by simply toggling GDR on ahead of/around the existing pipeline) still holds; this session's `curly-general-scope-reindent(-multipass)=on` test was worth running (per the coordinator's explicit request) since it was a cheap, well-motivated hypothesis given the just-landed multipass work, but the hypothesis did not pan out. A real fix (consulting `GdrBraceDepthCounter`/`GdrLineParenBracketDepth`-style structural-depth tracking directly from inside `renderCallCandidate`'s fits-check, replacing its volatile `lineStartIndex` anchor with a stable structurally-derived one) remains a distinct, not-yet-attempted implementation task, flagged for a future session to attempt only after an explicit go-ahead (per `STATE_COMMON.md`'s ambiguity-handling protocol -- this is a real design/implementation decision, not a bug fix with an obvious correct patch, and two prior narrow-patch attempts, `RDD_KEY_221`/`RDD_KEY_226`, already regressed other fixtures). No source code changed this session. |
| RDD_KEY_236 | Orphan `</p>` residual gap (`STATE_DATA_FORMATS.md` item 2, `apache/ant manual/running.html`, `RDD_KEY_223`'s accepted discard-vs-synthesize gap) -- user directive, 2026-08-03, reversing RDD_KEY_223's "discard-only" choice for this one tag name. **Superseded: `</p>` now synthesizes an empty `<p></p>` instead of being discarded, matching the real HTML5 "p end tag" tree-construction algorithm** (spec-mandated for parser-state/insertion-mode interoperability across conforming implementations, independent of any visual effect -- though an empty `<p>` does typically render visible vertical whitespace via the UA-stylesheet default `<p>` margin, unlike e.g. an empty `<span>`). New `XmlSpecificRule.synthesizeEmptyElement(String)` builds a synthetic empty `ELEMENT` `Node` (`type=ELEMENT`, `tagName` set, `children=new ArrayList<>()`, no attrs, not self-closing); called from both orphan-close-tag discard sites in `parseNodes` (the `stopAtCloseTag` branch guarded by `openTagStack.contains(...)`, and the separate document-root-level stray-closing-tag branch) exactly when the discarded tag name equals `"p"` -- every other orphan tag name's discard-only behavior (RDD_KEY_223's original fix) is unchanged. Verified: `test/real_code_regressions_173_out.html` updated to include the synthesized `<p></p>`; `make test` 238/238 forward + idempotency; full `apache/ant manual/` 226-file corpus re-run (`--preserve-tree --root`) 226/226 forward + idempotency + `html_syntax_check.sh` clean, `diff -rq` round1 vs round2 empty; direct `<p` occurrence count on the reformatted `running.html` confirms exactly one new synthesis (60 vs. original 59), closing the "`<body>` child count 82 vs 81" gap `RDD_KEY_223` had left as an accepted residual. No other fixture/corpus regressed. |
| RDD_KEY_237 | STATE_PYTHON3.md's "Open Questions" indent-conversion granularity (Python analog of `MiscRuleCore#convertIndentation`), resolved 2026-08-04 against real evidence rather than the prior hypothesis. Real-code check across existing `/tmp/black`, `/tmp/django`, `/tmp/cpython` checkouts (`grep -rlP '^\t'`): zero tab-indented files anywhere in `django/django`'s or `python/cpython`'s actual code; exactly 3 in `psf/black`, and all 3 turned out to be entirely inside already-opaque triple-quoted docstrings (`tests/data/cases/docstring.py`/`docstring_tabs.py`/`whitespace.py`), never real block indentation. **Conclusion: real-world disciplined Python code essentially never has in-code (non-string-literal) indentation drift within a single file** -- the prior "per-block, rescale if clean multiple of the presumed unit" plan (guessing block boundaries from raw per-line width) was solving a hypothetical that doesn't occur in practice, and was strictly less safe than an alternative available in this codebase: `TokenizerIndent#synthesizeIndentation` already computes a fully authoritative INDENT/DEDENT depth stack for every real statement line (CPython-equivalent algorithm; the file having tokenized at all proves that depth is internally consistent). **Decision: granularity is neither whole-file-uniform nor per-block-heuristic -- it is per real statement line, reconstructed directly from the tokenizer's own already-resolved depth**, never re-derived by guessing from raw width. New `MiscRuleIndent#convertIndentation` (mirrors `MiscRuleCore#convertIndentation`'s name/shape/signature) walks the token stream with a running `depth` counter incremented/decremented by each INDENT/DEDENT marker actually encountered, rendering each real statement line's leading whitespace as `depth` indent units in the target style; a blank/comment-only line (no INDENT/DEDENT of its own, ambiguous true depth -- e.g. a comment deliberately dedented early to visually group with a following shallower block, confirmed via `test/py_comments_inp.py`) is never depth-rewritten, only width-converted in place via the inherited `MiscRuleCore#renderIndent`/`expandedIndentWidth` (safe regardless of true depth, since it changes no width, only character style); an interior continuation-line of a multi-physical-line statement (bracket/backslash continuation, `t.parenDepth > 0` or backslash-final) is left completely untouched, mirroring `TokenizerIndent#synthesizeIndentation`'s own `insideBrackets`/backslash check for what counts as a new logical line. Wired into `FormatterIndent#formatOne` as a final phase (re-tokenize the fully-formatted text, then convert), exactly mirroring `FormatterCurly`'s own "Phase 6: final whitespace normalization, last" placement of the C-family original; no new config key needed -- reuses the existing shared `indent-style` key (already resolved from `auto` to a concrete `spaces`/`tabs` choice upstream in `Main.formatStandalone`/`ServerMode` before either family's formatter ever runs, same precedent the C-family original already relies on). Two real bugs found and fixed during implementation, both via the same `psf/black`/`django/django`/`python/cpython` real-code idempotency methodology used throughout this job: (1) a comment-only line adjacent to `match`/`case` initially got wrongly rewritten to the carried-over (deeper) depth instead of its own written (shallower) width -- repro `test/py_comments_inp.py`, fixed by exempting blank/comment-only lines from depth-based rewriting entirely (see above); (2) a synthesized end-of-file DEDENT-run token's `text` field (a literal width number for internal use, per `TokenizerIndent#synthesizeIndentation`'s own javadoc) was appended verbatim as if it were real source text whenever it landed outside the `atLineStart` branch (a file with no trailing newline on its last line) -- corrupted `psf/black`'s `tests/data/cases/comments3.py`/`annotations.py` with a stray trailing digit, growing further each idempotency round; fixed by explicitly skipping INDENT/DEDENT token text in the general (non-`atLineStart`) append branch. Final validation: `make test` 244/244 forward + idempotency (new fixture `real_code_regressions_178_{inp,out}.py`, tab-indented `match`/`case`/comment source modeled on the `py_comments` pattern, converted to the default `indent-style = spaces`); full-corpus re-run after both fixes -- `psf/black` (338 files), `django/django` (2927 files), `python/cpython` (`Lib/`, ~1500 files): zero crashes, zero non-idempotency, zero new `py_compile` errors (only pre-existing, formatter-unrelated failures reproduced identically on the unformatted originals) across all three. |
| RDD_KEY_238 | Self-hosting dogfood bug: `DeclarationAlignmentRuleCore.isTightToken`/`MiscRuleCore.isTightToken` treat any run of `&` characters (`Token.isRepOp(t, '&')`, matching Kotlin/JS/TS's earlier-fixed `&&` cases too) as a tight join with no leading space, gated only `!lang.isKotlin && !lang.isJs && !lang.isTs` — Java was never excluded. Java has no pointer/reference declarator syntax at all (unlike C/C++, where `&`/`&&` are legitimately tight against a preceding type as an rvalue-reference declarator sigil); Java's `&`/`&&` are always bitwise-AND/logical-AND (or, for a bare single `&`, an intersection-type bound like `<T extends A & B>`, conventionally spaced) — so the un-gated rule wrongly collapsed a Java logical-AND's leading space wherever an expression rendered through either shared join point, found via the formatter's own self-hosting dogfood (`XmlSpecificRule.java`'s `shouldFosterParent`-adjacent `final boolean fostered = ... >= LEVEL_TABLE_FOSTER&& ! fosterBufferStack.isEmpty()&& isInTableInsertionMode()&& shouldFosterParent(...)`). `||` was checked and confirmed NOT affected — `Token.isRepOp` is never called with `'|'` anywhere in the codebase, and no other tight-join rule mentions `|`/`||`, so `||`'s spacing was never derived from this rule. FIXED by adding `!lang.isJava` around the `Token.isRepOp(t, '&')` half of both conditions only (the `Token.isRepOp(t, '*')` half is untouched — no observed bug there; Java's `*` at this join point is only ever multiplication or an import wildcard, neither of which reaches a declaration-initializer/expression tight-join check). `make test`: 244/244 forward + idempotency, unchanged from baseline (no fixture registered — user-agreed simplified validation scope for this task explicitly excluded adding a new fixture pair). Full self-format dogfood-and-adopt re-run over `src/` and `tools/*` (`java_content_diff.sh`/`python_content_diff.sh` clean on every `tools/*` file) confirms the bug's real project-wide scope: 168 real (non-comment) `&&`-missing-space occurrences fixed across `src/`, 0 in `tools/*` (already clean), 0 `||` occurrences found anywhere (confirming the `||` negative). A separate, unrelated, out-of-scope limitation was surfaced during this validation and left untouched: 4 `&&`-missing-space occurrences remain in `src/` (`TomlSpecificRule.java`/`CssSpecificRule.java`/`YamlSpecificRule.java`/`JsonSpecificRule.java`, all a `groupStart = (! atEnd&& ...) ? i : -1;` ternary-in-plain-assignment shape) — confirmed via an isolated repro that the formatter's general statement-rewrite passes never re-derive operator spacing for a plain (non-declaration) assignment statement's inner expression at all, leaving whatever spacing the original source already had untouched; this is a pre-existing, much broader gap (general expression-statement spacing normalization, not specific to `&`/`&&`) outside this bug's scope, not a regression and not something this fix could address. |
| RDD_KEY_239 | `RDD_KEY_238`'s follow-up gap (STATE_C_CPP_JAVA.md "Known Gaps -- Open"): general (non-declaration) expression-statement operator spacing was never re-derived at all -- a plain assignment statement's RHS (e.g. `groupStart = ( ! atEnd&& items.get(i).isKeyed() ) ? i : - 1;`, `TomlSpecificRule.java`/`CssSpecificRule.java`/`YamlSpecificRule.java`/`JsonSpecificRule.java`) passed through byte-for-byte via `MiscRuleCore.render`'s `joinVerbatim`, so a pre-existing operator-spacing defect in the original source was never corrected no matter how many times the file was reformatted. Confirmed via git archaeology (`git show 78d177a~1`) that the hand-written source was correctly spaced (`!atEnd && ... : -1;`) and a prior self-format-adoption commit corrupted it -- a real live bug, not a hand-authored artifact. Three sub-issues, all confirmed real: (1) missing space before `&&` (`atEnd&&`), (2) extra space after unary `!` (`! atEnd`), (3) `- 1` instead of `-1` for unary minus. FIXED: (a) added an unconditional `if( isOp(prev, "!") ) return false;` tight-join rule to both `MiscRuleCore.needsSpaceBetween` and `DeclarationAlignmentRuleCore.needsSpaceBetween` (the `!`-tight-join gap affected declaration-initializer rendering too, not just plain assignments -- confirmed via repro); (b) new `MiscRuleCore.renderExpressionTokens` (ported from `DeclarationAlignmentRuleCore.renderInitTokens`'s binary-vs-unary `*`/`&` lookahead, C-style-cast-close detection via new `isCStyleCastClose`/`CONTROL_FLOW_KEYWORDS`, and unary-minus/plus detection) now used by `MiscRuleCore.render(List<Assignment>)`'s single-line RHS cell instead of `joinVerbatim`, deliberately scoped to the single-line grid-row path only -- `renderMultiLine` and the `valueSpansMultipleLines` fallback branch untouched, so no re-wrapping/re-ordering of multi-line RHS expressions, only inter-token spacing on the single-line path. Two real regressions found and fixed during validation, both via `make test`, neither hypothetical: (i) plain `renderTokens` (tried first, before `renderExpressionTokens` existed) regressed C pointer dereference `g_config = *cfg;` into `g_config = * cfg;` (no unary-`*`/`&` lookahead at all) -- root reason `renderExpressionTokens` was written as a dedicated joiner instead of reusing `renderTokens`; (ii) once `renderExpressionTokens` existed, `isCStyleCastClose`'s tight-cast-close carve-out was unconditionally applied to every language, but Java/Kotlin/JS/TS keep a space after a cast (`(ExpressionStmt) lambdaExpr...`) unlike C/C++'s tight `(Type)expr` -- regressed `real_code_regressions_55` (javaparser dogfood fixture); fixed by gating that carve-out with `(lang.isC || lang.isCpp)`, matching the sibling pointer-dereference carve-out's own existing gate. A third, self-hosting-dogfood-only regression (not caught by `make test`, only by the required src/ dogfood re-run) was also found and fixed: `renderExpressionTokens`'s binary-vs-unary `*`/`&` disambiguation only recognized "next token is IDENTIFIER/NUMBER" as evidence of a binary operator, missing `dh[k] * (hTilde[k] - hPrev[k])`-shaped expressions (`GruClassifier.java`) where `next` is `(` -- regressed `dh[k] * (...)` into `dh[k]* (...)`; fixed by broadening the check to `prevIsValue` (IDENTIFIER/NUMBER/closing `)`/`]`) combined with `next` being IDENTIFIER/NUMBER/`(`/null. A fourth pre-existing gap (unrelated to `!`/`&&`/unary-minus, discovered only because `renderExpressionTokens` started re-deriving spacing where `joinVerbatim` previously left it alone) was also found and fixed: `needsSpaceBetween` had no case for `(` directly after a closing `]` (array-subscript/array-`new`-size-clause call/constructor-invocation paren, e.g. C++'s `new float[n]()`), defaulting to spaced -- regressed `cpp_core`'s `new float[frames * channels]();` into `...] ();`; fixed by adding `if( isPunct(cur, "(") && isPunct(prev, "]") ) return false;`. Additionally, per an explicit coordinator request raised mid-investigation, a duplicated `isUnaryMinusOperand(List<Token>, int)` implementation (byte-for-byte identical, one copy each in `DeclarationAlignmentRuleCore` and `MiscRuleCore`, same "small per-rule-class token helper duplicated across the class-refactor hierarchy" pattern `RDD_KEY_238` itself was written up against) was centralized as `Token.isUnaryMinusOperand` in `TokenizerCore.java` (pure function of `(tokens, index)`, no `lang`/instance-state dependency, matching the existing `Token.isPunct`/`isOp`/`isRepOp`/`isKeyword`/`isComment`/`isGapToken` centralization precedent) -- all 3 call sites (one in `DeclarationAlignmentRuleCore.renderTokens`/`renderInitTokens`, two in `MiscRuleCore.renderTokens`/`renderExpressionTokens`) now call `Token.isUnaryMinusOperand` directly (inlined, no wrapper method kept in either rule class). `make test`: 244/244 forward + idempotency throughout, zero regressions (baseline unchanged from `RDD_KEY_238`, no new fixture registered -- user-agreed simplified validation scope for this task explicitly excluded adding a new fixture pair). Full self-format dogfood-and-adopt re-run over `src/` (round1-only, no round2-JAR re-test step, per user-agreed simplified scope) found 21 files with a diff exactly attributable to this fix (`ScopePipelineCurly.java`, `Main.java`, `DeclarationAlignmentRuleCurly.java`, `TomlSpecificRule.java`, `XmlSpecificRule.java`, `KotlinGetterSetterRule.java`, `KotlinSignatureRule.java`, `CppSpecificRule.java`, `CssSpecificRule.java`, `MiscRuleCurly.java`, `KotlinDeclarationAlignmentRule.java`, `YamlSpecificRule.java`, `JsonSpecificRule.java`, `BlockStructureRule.java`, `JsTsDeclarationAlignmentRule.java`, `KotlinSpecificRule.java`, `MiscRuleCore.java`, `GetterSetterRuleCurly.java`, `DeclarationAlignmentRuleCore.java`, `JsTsSpecificRule.java`, `TokenizerIndent.java`), each spot-checked, all `!`/`&&`/unary-minus-shaped, `java_content_diff.sh`-clean, adopted back; a re-run after adoption confirmed zero remaining diffs (fully idempotent). `tools/*` (13 `.java` files) checked separately and found already clean -- 0 files affected, no adoption needed there. |
| RDD_KEY_240 | Adversarial stress-testing task (2026-08-05, not real-code dogfooding -- synthetic constructions specifically targeting `curly-general-scope-reindent-multipass`'s bounded 4-stage loop, per `STATE_CURLY_GDR.md`'s "second-order oscillation" open risk) **found a genuine, minimal, reproducible counterexample to full idempotency.** Mechanism confirmed first: `GdrPipelineGate.applyAndFormat` is an unconditional, hardcoded 4-call sequence (GDR, formatOne, GDR, formatOne) -- NOT a "stop when stable, else iterate up to N" convergence loop; there is no stability check, no iteration count beyond exactly 4 calls, and no warning/error emitted if the sequence hasn't actually converged by the 4th call. Adversarial constructions tried: deeply nested (10/20/30/50-level) one-true-brace `} else if (...) {` chains, dense combinations of one-true-brace joins + chained fluent calls + multi-line lambda wraps within a single nested structure, deeply nested switch-in-if, wrapped ternary chains, randomized/garbage leading-whitespace input, and language coverage across C/C++/Java/Kotlin/JS/TS, all flush-left or randomly-indented (no clean original indentation to anchor a relative-delta reindenter) with `curly-general-scope-reindent=on; curly-general-scope-reindent-multipass=on` via in-file config, each round1/round2/round3/round4/round5-reformatted. Most constructions (deep one-true-brace nesting/dense-combo/switch-nest/ternary-chain/garbage-indent in Java, and the C++ deep-template/raw-string case) stayed fully idempotent across all 5 rounds -- no bug. **But a MINIMAL TS/JS repro was found and does NOT need deep nesting at all:** a single-level `if (...) { arr.filter(x => x > 0).map(x => x*2).forEach(x => {...}); } else if (...) { ... }` (one level of `if`/`else if`, no other nesting) with `curly-general-scope-reindent-multipass=on` reproduces at depth 1: round1's format of the raw input produces `.map(...)`'s wrapped continuation line indented at one level (e.g. col 12), but reformatting round1's own output (round2) re-indents that same continuation line one level deeper (e.g. col 16) -- round1 != round2, a genuine idempotency failure from a SINGLE top-level formatter invocation despite the internal 4-stage GDR+pipeline sequence already having run to completion within that invocation. Critically, round2 == round3 == round4 == round5 (confirmed) -- the output DOES stabilize, but only after an extra full re-application of the entire formatter (effectively 8 internal stages across two invocations), not within the bounded-4-stage single invocation the multipass design assumes suffices. This directly falsifies `STATE_CURLY_GDR.md`'s "Open design proposal" section's own claim under "Whether this achieves true idempotency" ("a *second* full 4-stage application should be a no-op end to end") -- it is NOT a no-op for this shape. Root cause (consistent with `RDD_KEY_229`'s already-diagnosed circular dependency): stage 3 (GDR-2)'s reindentation of the wrapped `.map(...)` continuation changes that line's column width, which is enough to flip stage 4 (pipeline-2)'s own wrap/indent-continuation decision for the SAME wrapped call one more time beyond what stage 4 can settle in a single pass -- i.e., a real second-order oscillation, exactly the risk `STATE_CURLY_GDR.md` flagged as unproven and went looking for. Confirmed language scope: reproduces in plain JS and TS (`arr.filter().map().forEach()` arrow-chain shape); does NOT reproduce in the equivalent Java shape (`list.stream().filter().map().forEach()` with lambdas) at the same shallow depth -- Java's wrap/continuation-indent rendering path apparently isn't as sensitive to this specific width perturbation. Kotlin reproduces too, but needed much deeper nesting (30 levels of nested trailing-lambda `if`/`else if` chains) before the same column-drift-by-one-level pattern appeared, rather than at depth 1 like JS/TS. **No source code changed** -- per `STATE_COMMON.md`'s ambiguity-handling protocol and this task's explicit constraint, a fix to the bounded-loop architecture itself is out of scope for a stress-testing task; this is a confirmed, documented finding only. `make test`: 244/244 forward + idempotency, unaffected (all adversarial work was `/tmp`/scratchpad-only, `curly-general-scope-reindent`/`-multipass` remain `off` by default, unchanged in `Config.java`). |
| RDD_KEY_241 | Fix for `RDD_KEY_240`'s confirmed second-order-oscillation counterexample: `GdrPipelineGate.applyAndFormat`'s hardcoded, unconditional 4-call sequence (GDR, formatOne, GDR, formatOne) -- which never compared any two stages' output and simply assumed 4 calls always sufficed -- replaced with an actual convergence loop. New behavior when both `curly-general-scope-reindent` and `curly-general-scope-reindent-multipass` are `on`: run the GDR-pass + `formatOne` cycle repeatedly, comparing each new cycle's `formatOne` output against the immediately preceding cycle's `formatOne` output (`String.equals`, byte-identical check), stopping as soon as two consecutive cycles match (a true fixed point) rather than stopping unconditionally after exactly 2 cycles (4 stages). A new `MAX_MULTIPASS_CYCLES = 20` safety cap (chosen as roughly 5x the ~4 cycles the confirmed `RDD_KEY_240` counterexample needed to reach its true fixed point, well above every other real/synthetic case tested across this job's history) throws `IllegalStateException` with a diagnostic message (including the file path and a pointer to this key) if reached without convergence, rather than silently returning a possibly-still-oscillating result. The base single-pre-pass-then-pipeline path (multipass off, or the base `curly-general-scope-reindent` flag off) is completely unchanged -- still exactly one GDR call plus one `formatOne` call, byte-for-byte identical to prior behavior; only the `on`+`on` multipass path's internal loop structure changed. **Validation:** `make test` 244/244 forward + idempotency throughout (unaffected -- change is scratchpad/`/tmp`-exercised only, no fixture added, matching how `RDD_KEY_229`/`RDD_KEY_240` findings were documented). Re-ran the exact minimal `RDD_KEY_240` TS/JS repro (`if (...) { arr.filter().map().forEach(); } else if (...) { ... }` with both flags on): previously round1 != round2 (non-idempotent); with the new convergence loop, round1 == round2 (byte-identical, confirmed via `--standalone --in-place` + `diff`) -- the counterexample is fixed. In practice the fix case converges after 2 extra internal cycles beyond the old fixed bound (i.e. what RDD_KEY_240 called "round2" of the old design is now folded into a single `applyAndFormat` invocation's own loop). Real-code corpora already validated for the base multipass mechanism (`javaparser-core-generators`, `javaparser-core` main module, `angular/angular` TS cluster-5, `tool/JSONEncoderLite.java`, `serge-sans-paille/frozen`) were not re-run file-by-file this session since the loop is a strict generalization (converges in >=2 cycles, same as before, for any input the old fixed-2-cycle design already handled -- those cases still terminate at cycle 2 exactly as before) and `make test`'s full suite covers the wiring; the change is additive safety (extra iterations only kick in when the old fixed bound wasn't already sufficient). `curly-general-scope-reindent`/`curly-general-scope-reindent-multipass` remain `off` by default, unchanged in `Config.java`. Full implementation: `GdrPipelineGate.applyAndFormat`, `src/com/jxmake/formatter/gdr/GdrPipelineGate.java`. |
| RDD_KEY_242 | Fixed a pre-existing `GdrRewriter.spaces` `NegativeArraySizeException` crash (side-finding from `RDD_KEY_240`/`RDD_KEY_241`'s session, undocumented until now, confirmed pre-existing and unaffected by that session's convergence-loop change). Root cause confirmed via direct reconstruction (not assumed): `GdrReindenter.compute` derives each touchable line's indent `level` as `braceLevel + pbLevel`, where each axis is that axis's running open/close depth (`GdrBraceDepthCounter`/`GdrParenBracketDepthCounter`) at the point of that line -- for genuinely well-formed (balanced) input this can never go negative, since a closer can't out-number its openers up to any point in valid nested code. The crash reproduces when the source has more closing brackets than opening ones by that point -- i.e. actually malformed/unbalanced input (confirmed with a minimal repro: a Kotlin one-true-brace `if`/`else` body containing a trailing-lambda fluent chain, `listOf(...).filter{}.map{}.forEach{}`, with one extra stray `}` closing the `if`-block twice) -- `GdrBraceDepthCounter`'s running depth goes to -1 partway through the file and stays negative for every subsequent line in that scope, so `GdrReindenter` computes a negative `level`/`columns`, which `GdrRewriter.rewrite` passes straight to `GdrRewriter.spaces(int count)`, whose `new StringBuilder(count)` throws `NegativeArraySizeException` for a negative `count`. Confirmed via a direct harness against `GdrReindenter.compute` (printed the exact line/level where it first goes negative) and via the full CLI path (`--standalone`) reproducing the identical stack trace under `curly-general-scope-reindent=on` alone (multipass off), matching the original session's note that it's a base single-pass bug, not multipass-specific. **Fix (narrow guard, per the task's own explicit guidance for a malformed-input-only edge case):** `GdrReindenter.compute` now clamps `braceLevel`/`pbLevel` to `>= 0` independently (before summing, so one axis's legitimate positive level can't be cancelled by the other axis's bogus negative one) before constructing each line's `GdrIndentTarget` -- for well-formed input this is a no-op (levels are already always >= 0), and for malformed input it means an over-closed line/scope reindents to column 0 rather than crashing (formatting output for that malformed case is not guaranteed correct, only guaranteed not to crash, matching the task's accepted scope for this kind of guard). Added a second, purely defensive backstop in `GdrRewriter.spaces` itself (clamps a negative `count` to 0) in case any other future caller ever passes a negative value. **Validation:** re-ran the exact minimal repro post-fix -- no crash, file formats (imperfectly, as expected for malformed input) instead of throwing; also re-ran a second variant with two levels of extra nesting around the same double-close shape -- no crash. `make test`: 244/244 forward + idempotency, unaffected (no fixture added -- this is a malformed-input-only guard with no well-formed-input behavior change, matching how `RDD_KEY_229`/`RDD_KEY_240` documented similar edge findings without a fixture). `curly-general-scope-reindent`/`curly-general-scope-reindent-multipass` remain `off` by default, unchanged in `Config.java`. Full implementation: `GdrReindenter.compute` (`src/com/jxmake/formatter/gdr/GdrReindenter.java`), `GdrRewriter.spaces` (`src/com/jxmake/formatter/gdr/GdrRewriter.java`). |
| RDD_KEY_243 | Resolved this file's Open Question on how to fix the base single-pass `RDD_KEY_229` ordering bug: coordinator chose option (A) as documented (not (B), the pipeline-code change) but scoped narrowly -- not "make the base path always iterate internally" (which STATE_CURLY_GDR.md's Open Questions entry flagged as redundant with the existing multipass key, RDD_KEY_233/RDD_KEY_234), but "document that the already-shipped `curly-general-scope-reindent-multipass=on` is the fix/workaround for this gap," since the 2026-08-03 validation session already empirically confirmed multipass resolves every RDD_KEY_229-shape non-idempotency found across every corpus tested (javaparser-core-generators 13/43->0/43, javaparser-core 93/576->0/576, angular TS cluster-5 2/3 files fixed, JSONEncoderLite.java, serge-sans-paille/frozen 7/20->0/20). No source code changed -- this is a documentation-only resolution. `README.md` restructured: new "General scope-depth reindentation (GDR)" subsection under Configuration/"Config file format" (mirroring the existing HTML5-tc-gap-level subsection's pattern) now documents the joined-one-true-brace-style gap with an inline code example (no external dogfood file/repo names) and states plainly that `curly-general-scope-reindent-multipass=on` resolves it at the cost of extra passes; "Known Limitations" restructured to group entries by language family (curly-brace, tag-based, indent-based) instead of by effect size alone, and entries tied to a config key (GDR, html5-tc-gap-level, normalize-comment-start-case) now point back to their Config-file-format subsection instead of duplicating the explanation; the relative-delta-reindent and single-word-comment entries were also reworded to use inline code examples instead of naming real-world dogfood repos/files (ASTParser.java, tool/JSONEncoderLite.java, javaparser/javaparser, WordPress/wordpress-develop, web-platform-tests/wpt, alexandersandberg/html5-elements-tester -- all removed from README.md prose; STATE_CURLY_GDR.md/RDD_LOG.md remain the authoritative place for those real-corpus specifics). This file's "Fix the base single-pass ordering bug" checklist item is now resolved via this documented workaround rather than left blocked; STATE_CURLY_GDR.md's Open Questions entry removed. |
| RDD_KEY_244 | Considered and rejected: making `curly-general-scope-reindent` (and/or `-multipass`) `on` by default instead of staying permanently opt-in. Rejected on two independent grounds, not just "not yet proven safe": (1) purpose mismatch -- GDR is a narrow fix-up tool for flush-left/machine-generated/copy-pasted-with-lost-indentation source, not a general formatting rule; ordinarily hand-written source (the overwhelming majority of real-world input this formatter runs on) already has consistent indentation, so GDR has no benefit there and a default-on pre-pass would rewrite every line of every file for no gain on the common case; (2) blast-radius inversion -- every other pass in this formatter is deliberately scoped narrow specifically to keep regression risk low across the ~20+-real-corpus-validated baseline ("never touch indentation unless a specific rule requires it"); GDR default-on would invert that invariant project-wide. Combined with the still-unresolved `curly-general-scope-reindent-multipass` second-order-oscillation risk (RDD_KEY_240/RDD_KEY_241 -- absent on every corpus tested so far, not proven impossible), a residual-risk feature is only acceptable to leave standing when it stays the enabling user's own informed opt-in choice, not something imposed on every user by a changed default. Conclusion: `curly-general-scope-reindent`/`curly-general-scope-reindent-multipass` are intended to remain permanently opt-in -- this was not a deferred "eventually flip the default" task, so it is removed from this job's outstanding-risk tracking rather than left as a TODO. No source code changed; defaults remain `off`/`off` in `Config.java`, unchanged. |
| RDD_KEY_245 | Tier-4 architectural TODO "call-wrap/collapse vs. declaration-alignment/padding fits-check ordering" (shared JS_TS/C_CPP_JAVA root cause, highest file-count impact item, 28/601 TS + 23/5394 angular + lodash `SwitchRule`-adjacent case) -- 2026-08-06 investigation session, NO code change landed, deferred again with a refined (still incomplete) root-cause finding. Task explicitly named `ScopePipelineCurly.processScope`'s outer-first-then-recurse double-pass as the suspected shared root cause (per STATE_JS_TS.md's prior 2026-08-05 Open Questions entry). Built a fresh minimal repro (`/tmp/mini.ts`, the `commandLineParser.ts`-derived `pathOptions`/`optionMap`/`watchOptionMap` shape) and instrumented `ScopePipelineCurly.processScope` directly with entry-point debug prints at the span-recursion decision point (`isNamedScope`/`hasTopLevelNewline` branch, ~line 1829). **Contrary to the prior session's write-up, this session's own instrumentation shows only ONE recursion level touches these declarations for this specific repro** (the function body itself; the `pathOptions` object-literal's own `{...}` is never found as a separate `splitTopLevelSpans` "Span" at all, since it's a value-position brace nested inside a declaration's initializer, not a control-flow/function/class body) -- so the "double-pass re-aligns from two different intermediate states" mechanism, while confirmed real elsewhere in the codebase (and plausibly the true cause for OTHER files in the 28/601 and 23/5394 counts), is NOT what breaks this particular repro. Traced the actual mechanism for this repro instead: `JsTsDeclarationAlignmentRule.spansMultipleLines`'s flat `parenDepth`/`braceDepth` counters bail (row excluded from its alignment group, forcing a group split) on ANY `NEWLINE` while `braceDepth > 0`, even when that newline is strictly inside a `(`/`[` pair that itself happens to be nested inside the row's own object-literal-initializer `{...}` (i.e. a previous round's `MiscRuleCurly.enforceCallLineBreaking` call-wrap of a nested call argument list, one level deeper than the already-existing paren-only carve-out this method's own javadoc documents). Implemented and real-code-verified the exact bracket-kind-stack fix (`Deque<Character>` innermost-enclosing-bracket check, bail only when the innermost bracket is `{` or the stack is empty, tolerate `(`/`[` regardless of enclosing brace nesting) previously described-but-reverted in STATE_JS_TS.md's 2026-08-05 entry -- **confirmed via direct testing this session that this fix alone does NOT resolve `/tmp/mini.ts`'s round1/round2 divergence** (same negative result as before, now independently reconfirmed rather than assumed). Further instrumentation showed the actual divergence is NOT in the alignment-grid grouping decision at all: `pathOptions`'s declaration keeps identical column width across both rounds either way; the real divergence is that round2 renders the object-literal's closing `}` onto its OWN line (`\n    };`) where round1 keeps it trailing inline (`... };`) -- a closing-brace-placement decision made by some pass other than `ScopePipelineCurly.processScope`'s span recursion (confirmed not that, per the single-recursion-level finding above) and other than `JsTsDeclarationAlignmentRule`'s grouping (confirmed via the reverted-again bracket-stack fix not changing the symptom) -- most likely inside `MiscRuleCurly.enforceCallLineBreaking`/`renderCallCandidate`'s own multi-line-source rendering path (STATE_JS_TS.md's separate "braceless if/else" Open Questions entry already names this same file/method region, ~line 1250-1330, as the locus of a structurally similar "which pass gets to see the final stable per-line width" problem). Not traced further this session -- out of the reasoning-effort budget available. Disposition: no code change landed; the bracket-stack `spansMultipleLines` refinement was written, single-repro-tested, and reverted again (not committed) rather than landed as an unverified partial change, consistent with the 2026-08-05 session's own posture. Re-affirms STATE_JS_TS.md's existing convergence hypothesis ("this could be the same shared architectural problem as the braceless-if/else `enforceCallLineBreaking` rejoin-fits-check issue") but narrows it further: for at least this repro shape, `ScopePipelineCurly.processScope`'s double-pass is NOT the culprit, and the true locus is most likely `MiscRuleCurly.enforceCallLineBreaking`'s closing-bracket placement on its multi-line-source render path, not declaration-alignment grouping at all. Left OPEN. **Next session:** do not re-attempt the `ScopePipelineCurly.processScope` outer/recurse double-pass theory for this specific repro shape without first re-confirming it actually recurses twice (this session's instrumentation says it does not, for this shape) -- instead instrument `MiscRuleCurly.enforceCallLineBreaking`'s closing-`)`/`}` placement decision on `/tmp/mini.ts`'s `pathOptions` statement directly, comparing round1 vs round2 inputs at that call, the same way this session instrumented `processScope`. `make test` unaffected (244/244, no source change committed). |
| RDD_KEY_246 | Direct follow-up to `RDD_KEY_245`'s "next session" pointer (instrument `MiscRuleCurly.enforceCallLineBreaking`'s closing-bracket placement on `/tmp/mini.ts`) -- 2026-08-06 second session, two fix attempts tried and reverted, no code change landed, but the true root cause is now identified precisely (superseding `RDD_KEY_245`'s tentative locus). `DBG_FC`-gated instrumentation in `FormatterCurly.format` at "after `ScopePipelineCurly.process`" and "before/after the first `enforceCallLineBreaking` call" showed the round1/round2 divergence already exists BEFORE `enforceCallLineBreaking` ever runs, ruling out `RDD_KEY_245`'s locus entirely; direct code reading of `enforceCallLineBreaking`/`renderCallCandidate` confirms it structurally cannot be responsible (it only ever replaces `[openParenIdx, closeIdx+1)`, never text after the call's own closing `)`). **Actual root cause:** `ScopePipelineCurly.applyOversizedAggregateInitClosingBracePass`, called once early inside `processScope` well before Phase 1's `enforceCallLineBreaking`, decides whether to move a dangling `}` onto its own line by checking whether the aggregate initializer's `{...}` already contains an embedded `NEWLINE` token -- on round1 (fresh format) no such newline exists yet since the nested call hasn't been wrapped yet, so the pass no-ops and `}` stays fused inline; on round2 (reformat) the previous round's call-wrap newline is already present, so the pass fires and splits `}` onto its own line -- round1 != round2. Same "decision made before a later pass's newline exists" family as the precedent fixes already named in STATE_JS_TS.md (`enforceComplexityPadding`, `enforceAttributeAndSpliceBracketPadding`, `enforceInitializerBraceSpacing`), just a pass not previously suspected. **Attempt 1 (narrow re-run of just this one pass a second time, reverted):** added `ScopePipelineCurly.applyOversizedAggregateInitClosingBraceFixup` (a public wrapper re-running only this pass), called once more from `FormatterCurly.format` right after the first `enforceCallLineBreaking` call -- fixed `/tmp/mini.ts`'s `}` placement, but revealed `JsTsDeclarationAlignmentRule.spansMultipleLines`/`parseDeclaration`'s grouping decision for the same row is ALSO made against the stale pre-call-wrap shape on round1 vs the post-call-wrap shape on round2 (round1 keeps the row grouped with wider padding, round2 excludes it with narrower padding) -- same bug family, second pass affected, narrow fixup insufficient. **Attempt 2 (full second `scopePipeline.process(text)` re-run in `FormatterCurly.format`'s Phase 1, reverted):** re-derives every per-scope pass's decision against the post-call-wrap shape; made `/tmp/mini.ts` fully idempotent (matching the previously-established stable fixed point), but `make test` (built via the proper `make jar`/`make test` path, not an ad-hoc `javac`) showed real new forward-pass regressions unrelated to the repro -- `real_code_regressions_100.ts` collapsed an already-correct `} // interface ParserOptions` (closing brace + trailing comment, previously on its own line) back onto the same line, plus failures in `real_code_regressions_144.kt` and several Java/`cpp26` fixtures. Re-running the whole five-pass pipeline a second time in the same round is not safe unconditionally -- at least one pass treats a second same-round invocation as "re-collapse this already-finalized span" rather than a true no-op refinement for trailing-comment-after-`}` shapes. **Disposition:** both attempts reverted; `FormatterCurly.java`/`ScopePipelineCurly.java` restored to pre-session `HEAD`, `.jar` rebuilt from clean `HEAD` via `make jar`, `make test` reconfirmed clean (244/244 forward + idempotency, zero regressions). No dogfood corpus (TS repo/angular/lodash) run -- both attempts were disqualified by the local fixture suite first. **Testing pitfall noted:** a single very-long `make _test_serial`/`make test` terminal capture can be silently truncated by the calling tool, hiding real `FAIL` lines among hundreds of `PASS` lines with no visible marker -- always redirect to a log file and `grep -n "^FAIL"` it directly for a suite this size; this cost significant time this session (an apparently-clean run was actually hiding 6-14 genuine failures depending on which build was tested). **Next session:** try re-running only the closing-brace pass plus the declaration-alignment grouping/padding pass a second time (the two passes shown to need the post-call-wrap shape) while leaving assignment/signature/getter-setter single-pass, to avoid Attempt 2's trailing-comment regression -- this narrower middle ground was not tried this session (budget spent confirming the narrowest and widest ends of the spectrum both fail, in different ways). `make test` unaffected at session end (244/244, no source change committed). |
| RDD_KEY_247 | `python-import-sort`/`python-import-blank-lines` were documented in README.md/STATE_PYTHON3.md as real config keys but never wired into `Config.java` (not in `ALL_KEYS`, no fields/getters, no CLI/file parsing, not in `GROUPS`) -- first noticed while building the server's `/properties` endpoint. `python-import-sort` was unambiguous to wire: it now gates `ScopePipelineIndent.applyImportSort`'s call site exactly like `java-import-sort`/`js-import-sort` gate their own passes -- when off, the entire §3 pass (both reordering and blank-line normalization) is a complete no-op. `python-import-blank-lines` initially hit a real ambiguity (STATE_COMMON.md's ambiguity protocol): `flushImportGroup` only ever replaced a group's own `[start,end)` line range with the same lines reordered, never touching the gap *between* groups -- unlike JS/TS's `enforceImportOrdering`/`renderImportSegment`, whose `blankLines` param actually inserts/normalizes blank-line count between rendered groups. Stopped, documented as an Open Question, asked the user. **Resolution (same day):** coordinator decided to implement it for real, mirroring the JS/TS shape. New `ScopePipelineIndent.applyImportGroupBlankLines`/`isBlankLine`, scoped narrowly to the one unambiguous case for Python's own bucket-less, adjacency-based grouping: two consecutive recognized import groups at the SAME depth, separated ONLY by blank physical line(s) (no comment, no depth change, no other statement in between), get their blank-line count normalized to the configured `pythonImportBlankLines` value; a gap containing a comment or spanning a depth change is left untouched. `applyImportSort` restructured to track each flushed group's own `[startIdx, endIdx, depth)` range (`groupRanges`) so the new pass can find each inter-group gap precisely. `ScopePipelineIndent` gained a 5-arg constructor (`lang, indentWidth, lineLength, pythonImportSort, pythonImportBlankLines`) threaded from `FormatterIndent.formatOne`; existing 2-/3-arg constructors default to `true`/`1` for backward compatibility. `Config.java` gained both keys' fields/getters, `ALL_KEYS` entries, CLI/file parsing (`parseBoolean`/`parseInt`, same pattern as `java-import-sort`/`java-import-blank-lines`), a new `Python 3` group in `GROUPS` (between `JS/TS` and `HTML5`, matching README.md's section order) and `describeOne` cases; the stale "pre-existing gap" comment on `GROUPS` was removed. Validated: `make test` 245/245 forward + idempotency (new local fixture `test/py_import_blank_lines_{inp,out}.py`, verified against the actual JAR before activation -- 2 adjacent same-depth import groups with a 2-blank-line gap collapse to 1 per the default, each group also sorted, while a 2-blank-line gap NOT between two import groups, before a following `def`, is left untouched); manual smoke test via `JXMAKE_CODE_FORMATTER_PYTHON_IMPORT_SORT=off` (full no-op, confirming gating) and `_PYTHON_IMPORT_BLANK_LINES=0` (collapses the inter-group gap to zero while leaving the unrelated gap at 2, confirming the config value threads through at a non-default setting); `/properties` verified via a live server + `curl` -- new `"Python 3"` group present between `"JS/TS"`/`"HTML5"` with both keys' correct defaults/allowed-values. `STATE_COMMON.md`'s "Server mode: 3rd endpoint" section and README.md's Server Wire Protocol section, both of which noted this as a known gap, were updated to remove the now-resolved caveat. |
| RDD_KEY_248 | Third session on the Tier-4 call-wrap/collapse vs. declaration-alignment/padding fits-check ordering idempotency bug (RDD_KEY_245/246), picking up `RDD_KEY_246`'s untried "narrower middle ground" pointer: re-run only `ScopePipelineCurly.applyOversizedAggregateInitClosingBracePass` + `applyDeclarationsPass` (closing-brace pass first, so declarations sees the corrected shape) a second time, not the full `process()`, not just the closing-brace pass alone. **Landed successfully this session.** Implementation: `processScope` gained a `closingBraceAndDeclarationsOnly` boolean threaded through its own recursion (a new 5-arg overload; the old 4-arg signature now just calls it with `false`) -- when `true`, the top-of-scope pass sequence is swapped to just those two passes in closing-brace-first order, skipping `applyAssignmentsPass`/`applySignaturePass`/`applyGetterSetterPass` entirely, while every span-recursion/indent-resolution mechanism below stays byte-identical between both modes so the fixup walks the same tree shape as a real pass. New public entry point `ScopePipelineCurly.reapplyClosingBraceAndDeclarationsPass(String)` (mirrors `process(String)`). `FormatterCurly.format` now keeps the `ScopePipelineCurly` instance it constructs (was previously anonymous/inline) and, for JS/TS only, calls the new reapply method right after the first `enforceCallLineBreaking` call in Phase 1. First cut of this (recursing through the FULL `processScope`, including its trailing-gap force-reindent step that snaps a child scope's closing-brace gap to a freshly-recomputed `effectiveSpanIndent`) reproduced the EXACT SAME regression as `RDD_KEY_246`'s Attempt 2: `real_code_regressions_100.ts`'s `} // interface ParserOptions` lost its indent (2sp -> 0sp) -- proving the regression's true source is that force-reindent step re-deriving indentation from THIS round's already-formatted physical text (which by this point already has other Phase 1 passes' blank-line-insertion/Allman-conversion baked in, a different shape than what `findParentIndent` saw during the original `process()` call), not the two token-level passes themselves. **Fix:** skip the trailing-gap force-reindent step entirely when `closingBraceAndDeclarationsOnly` is true -- the first `process()` call already force-reindented every trailing gap correctly once; the narrower second run only needs its own two passes' own splices, not a second independent re-derivation of indentation. With that one extra gate, `real_code_regressions_100.ts` matches its expected output again, `make test`/`make test-quiet` is 492/492 (245 fixture pairs incl. the new one below, forward + idempotency, zero FAIL), and `/tmp/mini.ts` (the `commandLineParser.ts`-derived repro) is fully idempotent. Dogfood-validated against both real-code corpora named in this bug's original file-count impact: `microsoft/TypeScript` (`/tmp/ts-dogfood/TypeScript`, `src/` only, 601 `.ts` files per STATE_JS_TS.md's existing scope) round1/round2 mismatches dropped from a freshly-reconfirmed 31/601 baseline (re-measured this session, close to but not identical to the file's previously-documented ~29/601 -- corpus/tool drift since 2026-07-31, not a regression) to 20/601 with the fix, a strict subset (`comm -13` empty, zero new regressions, 11 files newly idempotent including `commandLineParser.ts` itself); `angular/angular` (`/tmp/angular`, the same 5394-file `.ts` scope from the existing dogfood entry) dropped from a freshly-reconfirmed 17/5394 baseline to 15/5394, again a strict subset with zero new regressions (2 files newly idempotent, including `packages/compiler/src/jit_compiler_facade.ts`). `lodash/lodash`'s cached checkout (`/tmp/lodash`, `/tmp/round1-lodash`) was found empty/stale this session (prior session's temp files evidently cleaned) and was not re-cloned to re-verify given this task's already-large corpus-validation cost -- the fix's mechanism (JS/TS-only, gated on `lang.isJs || lang.isTs`) is not lodash-specific, and the two much larger corpora already give strong, consistent, zero-regression evidence; flagged here for a future session to re-confirm if convenient rather than as a blocking gap. New permanent fixture `test/real_code_regressions_179_{inp,out}.ts` (the `commandLineParser.ts`-derived minimal repro, registered in the Makefile's `INP_FILES` and `test/README.txt`, ahead of "How Tests Are Run"). Neither `curly-general-scope-reindent`/`-multipass` (STATE_CURLY_GDR.md) nor any other existing config flag was touched -- this fix is unconditional for JS/TS, no new config key. **Assessment for future sessions:** the "re-run some narrower subset of passes a second time" strategy family, dismissed as too narrow (Attempt 1) or too broad (Attempt 2) in `RDD_KEY_246`, turns out to work once the actual mechanism responsible for the Attempt-2-style regression (the shared trailing-gap force-reindent step, not the token-level passes chosen) was correctly isolated and excluded -- the family itself was sound, the prior sessions' diagnosis of WHY Attempt 2 failed was incomplete. Given the strong dogfood results (2 corpora, 5995 combined files, zero regressions, double-digit file-count improvement on both), this is landed as a real fix rather than deferred. |
| RDD_KEY_249 | Fourth session on the JS/TS braceless if/else "cluster 5" call-wrap rescue idempotency bug (README's `hasBreakableCall`/`refuseUnrescuableCollapse` bullet, previously investigated 2026-08-05 with no code change), continuing directly from that session's "next session" pointer into `MiscRuleCurly.enforceCallLineBreaking`'s `containsNewline` rejoin branch. Minimal repro `/tmp/mini2.ts` (self-contained variant of the `format_date.ts`-derived repro already on file, no switch statement needed): `if (offset === 0) return 'Z'; else return ( (zone >= 0 ? '+' : '') + padNumber(hours, 2, minusSign) + ':' + padNumber( Math.abs(zone % 60), 2, minusSign ) );` reproduces round1 != round2. `DBG_CLB`-gated instrumentation (removed before revert) in the rejoin branch's `candidateLen` fits-check showed the true mechanism: the rejoin check's `prefix`/`suffix` are built from `lineStartIndex`/`effectiveLineEndIndex`, which look back/forward only to the nearest `NEWLINE` token -- blind to a sibling call earlier/later on the SAME logical statement (joined by `+`, not this candidate's own arguments) once that sibling has already been wrapped onto multiple physical lines by a prior pass or prior round. Each of the two `padNumber(...)` candidates in the repro measures its own local-physical-line-only fits-check independently; round1 finds both individually "fit" (92 and 61 chars, under the 100 limit) and rejoins both, producing a single 145-char line (already an internal round1 violation); round2 (fed that one-liner, retokenized with a different upstream indent shape from the `else`/`if` alignment pass) finds only the first candidate's local check over limit (104), leaving it wrapped while the second still rejoins -- an inconsistent partial rejoin, hence round1 != round2. **Attempt (built, tested, then reverted):** added `logicalStatementStart`/`logicalStatementEnd` helpers (scan back/forward to the nearest true statement boundary -- `;`/`{`/`}`/file bounds -- which a mid-statement wrap's own `NEWLINE` never is) and rebuilt the rejoin `prefix`/`suffix` via `collapseToOneLine` over that wider statement-spanning range instead of the local physical line. This fully fixed `/tmp/mini2.ts` (round1 wraps both calls correctly and round2 matches, `diff` empty) but regressed `real_code_regressions_81_inp.ts` and `real_code_regressions_93_inp.ts` on `make test-quiet`: both fixtures' previously-correct, genuinely-idempotent fully-rejoined form (each already over `lineLengthLimit` once combined, but stable both rounds under the ORIGINAL per-candidate-local check) got needlessly split back into an unwanted multi-line wrap, because the new statement-wide check now judges the whole statement as too long and refuses every rejoin on it -- the exact same "does the post-wrap/rejoined line fit under the limit" trap the 2026-08-05 session's `hasBreakableCall` width-gate attempt already hit and reverted for the identical `real_code_regressions_81` reason, now independently reconfirmed from the `enforceCallLineBreaking` rejoin side of the pipeline instead of `BlockStructureRule`'s collapse-decision side. Reverted cleanly (`git checkout -- src/com/jxmake/formatter/rules/MiscRuleCurly.java`); baseline reconfirmed clean at 246/246 forward + idempotency, zero regressions from the revert. No dogfood corpus run this session (disqualified by the local fixture suite before reaching that stage). **Assessment:** a blanket statement-wide over/under-limit gate cannot distinguish the two pulling-in-opposite-directions cases (`_81`/`_93` want "over limit is fine, rejoin anyway, as long as it's round-stable"; `/tmp/mini2.ts` wants "if over limit, refuse consistently both rounds") because what actually differs between them is not the combined width itself but why `_81`/`_93`'s per-candidate local checks already happen to be round-stable under the pre-existing, unmodified `lineStartIndex`/`effectiveLineEndIndex`-based check while `/tmp/mini2.ts`'s are not -- likely because `_81`/`_93`'s sibling candidates don't actually share one physical line pre-wrap the way `/tmp/mini2.ts`'s two `padNumber` calls do. **Next session:** instrument that round-stability difference directly rather than retrying another blanket gate-shape variant; a fix scoped to "only widen the rejoin measurement when this candidate's own local physical line was itself produced by a still-open sibling wrap" (not a blanket statement-wide widening covering every candidate unconditionally) is the untried, more surgical framing and the most promising next starting point. Deferred per this bug's established Tier-4 difficulty (same family as `RDD_KEY_245`/`RDD_KEY_246`/`RDD_KEY_248`'s three-session sibling-cluster history); no fixture added, no config flag touched, no regression left in the tree. |
| RDD_KEY_250 | Sixth session, landed the fix for the JS/TS braceless if/else rejoin idempotency bug first precisely diagnosed in RDD_KEY_249's fifth-session writeup: `FormatterCurly.format` calls `MiscRuleCurly.enforceCallLineBreaking` (rejoin fits-check, ~line 247/286) before `BlockStructureRule.alignBracelessElseIfChain` (~line 361) inserts its column-alignment padding on the `else` branch. Round1's rejoin fits-check measures the `else` prefix pre-padding, undercounts width, wrongly rejoins a call -- the padding pass then pushes the final joined line over `lineLengthLimit` anyway (an internal round1 self-violation, confirmed via `DBG_CLB2`/`DBG_CLB3`-gated `System.err` instrumentation in a prior and this session respectively). Round2, fed that already-padded text, measures correctly and refuses the rejoin -- hence round1 != round2. Fixed via a narrow re-run, same fix shape as RDD_KEY_248's `reapplyClosingBraceAndDeclarationsPass`: right after `alignBracelessElseIfChain` in `FormatterCurly.format`, re-run `enforceCallLineBreaking` twice (a single pass only wraps the first over-limit candidate on a line with more than one, mirroring why the original ~247/~286 pair itself calls it twice for multi-candidate convergence) followed by `enforceComplexityPadding` (re-tightens/loosens the plain-space join `enforceCallLineBreaking` leaves behind, same reasoning as the pre-existing ~283-284 pair). Verified against `/tmp/mini2.ts` (`function formatOffset(offset: number, zone: number, hours: number, minusSign: string): string { if (offset === 0) return 'Z'; else return ( (zone >= 0 ? '+' : '') + padNumber(hours, 2, minusSign) + ':' + padNumber( Math.abs(zone % 60), 2, minusSign ) ); }`): round1/round2 now byte-identical. `make test-quiet` stayed at 246/246 forward + 246/246 idempotency both before and after. Explicitly re-verified `real_code_regressions_81_inp.ts`/`_93_inp.ts` (the two fixtures burned by both of RDD_KEY_249's session's prior reverted attempts) individually via `--out`: both still forward-match their `_out.ts` and are still idempotent -- confirms the fix is disjoint from what broke those two before, as RDD_KEY_249 predicted (neither fixture has a braceless-else chain, so `alignBracelessElseIfChain` no-ops on them and the new re-run block is a no-op too). Debug instrumentation (`DBG_CLB3`-env-gated) fully removed before landing. New permanent fixture `test/real_code_regressions_180_{inp,out}.ts` (the `formatOffset` repro), registered in the Makefile's `INP_FILES` and `test/README.txt`. |
| RDD_KEY_251 | Seventh session on "Non-idempotent switch-case re-indent on internally-inconsistent generated source" -- RESOLVED for the nested-switch-in-switch failure mode specifically (2026-08-04/2026-08-07 sessions' Tier-4-escalated gap). Both candidate next steps left by the 2026-08-07 writeup were implemented and validated on top of the same reused base (the two already-proven-safe fixes: the boundary-guard skip for the case body's own closing-`}` line, comparing the line's first *significant* token's index against `braceClose` rather than the newline-derived `lineStart`; and skipping any continuation line begun while a paren/bracket opened on an earlier line is still unclosed). **Approach (a) -- treat a nested switch as opaque:** `applyDepthDerivedBodyIndent`'s per-line scan, on encountering a nested `switch(...) { ... }`, jumps straight to its matching closing brace without deriving or overriding anything inside it (net-zero depth/paren-depth change, since a switch's own braces are balanced), letting that nested switch's own independent `SwitchBlock` pass -- already guaranteed by `pickInnermostNeedingWork`'s innermost-first ordering to run to completion first -- be the sole owner of its region. Verified: the minimal repro (new `test/real_code_regressions_181_{inp,out}.java`, distilled from `ASTParser.java`'s `case THROWS: { ... while(true) { switch(...) { case COMMA: ... } ... } }` shape) converges in one iteration and is idempotent; the full production `javaparser/javaparser` `ASTParser.java` (cached at `/tmp/javaparser_gdr`, ~5500 lines) now TERMINATES (previously a confirmed 2000+-iteration non-converging 2-cycle) and idempotency diff dropped from the pre-existing-bug baseline's 369 differing lines to 7, all in shapes unrelated to switch-case (a pre-existing if/else general-reindent gap and an unrelated call-wrap line-length gap, confirmed present identically in the unmodified baseline JAR run on the same file, i.e. not introduced by this fix). `make test`: 248/248 forward + idempotency (was 247/247), zero regressions. **Approach (b) -- thread one shared depth accumulator through nested switches instead of two independent recomputations:** implemented as a direct recursive variant (on encountering a nested switch, instead of skipping, recurse into its own case bodies using the same running depth/unit, anchored off the nested switch's own line's already-computed absolute indent) -- but the nested switch's `SwitchBlock` is *still* independently discovered and processed by `pickInnermostNeedingWork` on its own turn (unchanged), so its own independent recomputation (still derived from `indentBefore`-measured `switchIndent`/`caseIndent` text, not threaded through the shared accumulator) continued to disagree with the outer recursive pass's writes -- reproduced the exact same never-converging failure, now as a hang on even the minimal repro (previously the minimal repro was approach (a)/(b)-agnostic and converged fine under the OLD relative-delta code; only production input triggered it). A structurally sound (b) would additionally need `pickInnermostNeedingWork`/`formatNonInlineSwitches`'s own SwitchBlock-discovery loop to recognize and skip any switch nested inside another non-inline switch's case body (deferring it entirely to the outer recursive pass, never processing it as an independent top-level target) -- a materially larger architectural change than (a), same risk class as the not-yet-attempted `curly-general-scope-reindent` redesign (STATE_CURLY_GDR.md) per the 2026-08-07 writeup's own risk assessment, and not attempted further this session given (a) already fully resolves the production case with a small, well-contained, well-tested diff. **Decision: landed approach (a).** It is simpler (one opaque-skip branch, no new recursive helper, no change to the existing per-switch discovery/iteration engine), lower-risk (doesn't touch `pickInnermostNeedingWork`/`formatNonInlineSwitches`'s architecture at all), and empirically both terminates and produces correct STYLE.md-compliant depth-derived output on every tested shape, including the exact production file that originally motivated the gap. Known Gap entry updated to RESOLVED in `STATE_C_CPP_JAVA.md`; `README.md`'s "Known Limitations" bullet for this gap removed (list renumbered), mirroring the pattern of the prior JS/TS braceless-collapse bullet removal. |
| RDD_KEY_252 | Kotlin dogfood cluster **D3**, third session (2026-08-07), explicit go-ahead given per the "D3 fold" section's own gate -- attempted the prescribed direction (real Kotlin statement-boundary tracking inside `MiscRuleCurly.renderCallCandidate`'s no-newline fits-check, replacing the volatile `lineStartIndex` anchor, Kotlin-gated) instead of toggling GDR (already refuted by RDD_KEY_235). **Two sub-attempts within this session, both built, tested, and reverted; no code landed.** Sub-attempt 1: `kotlinStatementStartIndex` scans backward from `nameIdx` at the SAME brace/paren depth as `nameIdx` (skipping tokens at a different depth as "still part of the current statement, just nested"), stopping at a depth-0 `;`, or at a depth-0 `{`/`}`, or at a depth-0 NEWLINE that is not a "continuation" (checked via new `isKotlinLineContinuationBoundary`, a trailing/leading operator-set heuristic covering binary operators, `,`, `=`-family, `.`/`?.`/`->`/`..`/`::`, and `as`/`is`/`in`). Regressed 9 fixtures (`real_code_regressions_{27,33,44,45,46,62,156,165,169}_inp.kt`) at `make test`: under-measured because treating EVERY depth-0 `{` as a statement boundary is wrong for a trailing-lambda call argument (`_items.filter { it !is _FunctionItem || calledFunctions.contains(it.func.funcName) }`) -- the `{` of `.filter { ... }` is not a new statement, it's nested content of the *same* statement, so stopping there dropped the real `val liveItems = _items.filter {` prefix from the width measurement, wrongly judging an over-limit line as fitting. Sub-attempt 2 (same session): added `isKotlinControlFlowOrDeclBraceOpen` to distinguish a real block-opening `{` (`if (...) {`, `while (...) {`, `for (...) {`, `when (...) {`, `catch (...) {`, `else {`/`try {`/`finally {`/`do {`/`init {`, `class X {`/`object X {`/`interface X {`/`companion {`) from a lambda/trailing-lambda argument `{` (walks back past the opener's own matching `)` to the keyword before the matching `(`, or past a bare identifier to the keyword two tokens back) -- only the former now counts as a boundary. This traded the failure mode rather than resolving it: regressed a *different* set of 10 fixtures (`real_code_regressions_{19,23,37,43,44,62,149,156,157,165}_inp.kt`), this time over-measuring/over-wrapping and even corrupting indentation (`real_code_regressions_19_inp.kt`: `loadRecovery(this)` and `.setCancelable(false)` both wrongly exploded onto 3 lines with a broken continuation-indent column) -- the classifier's own heuristics (e.g. any bare identifier before `{` with no recognized decl-keyword two tokens back defaults to "not a boundary", any chained `.method()` continuation, etc.) still misjudge enough real Kotlin shapes (fluent chains, nested control flow, decl forms not enumerated) to flip other candidates' fits-checks the wrong way. Reverted both sub-attempts in full (`git checkout -- src/com/jxmake/formatter/rules/MiscRuleCurly.java`); also reverted two unrelated build/test-harness side effects surfaced as dirty by the `make jar`/`make test` runs (`code-formatter-1.0.0.jar` rebuild-timestamp diff, `test/README.txt`'s RDD_KEY_251 entry getting incidentally re-wrapped by a whitespace-only line-width difference) -- neither was an intentional edit this session. `make test` reconfirmed clean at 248/248 forward + idempotency both before and after, zero net change. **Assessment, matching and reinforcing all three prior sessions' (RDD_KEY_221/226/235) conclusion:** a token-scan heuristic for "is this depth-0 `{`/NEWLINE a real statement boundary" is fundamentally whack-a-mole -- each refinement that fixes one shape's under/over-measurement regresses a different shape, because the true answer requires actually parsing the Kotlin expression/statement grammar (distinguishing trailing lambdas from block bodies, multi-line fluent chains from adjacent statements, `when`-arm bodies, etc.) rather than a handful of enumerated keyword/operator lookback rules layered onto a backward token scan. **Next session should not attempt another heuristic variant of this same shape** -- the two sub-attempts here already demonstrate the heuristic-refinement space doesn't converge with bounded effort. A real fix likely needs either (a) a proper lightweight Kotlin statement/expression boundary parser (tracking genuine grammar constructs, not lookback keyword sets) built once and reused, or (b) revisiting after `STATE_CURLY_GDR.md`'s own architecture matures further (its structural-depth pre-pass already gets brace/paren depth right but -- per RDD_KEY_235 -- has no visibility into a single pipeline pass's own sibling-candidate interactions, so D3's fix cannot simply reuse GDR's existing per-line depth counters as-is; it would need genuinely new statement-boundary infrastructure, not just calling into what GDR already has). No fixture added (no fix landed). D3 remains open. |
| RDD_KEY_253 | Kotlin dogfood cluster **D3**, fourth session (2026-08-07, same day as RDD_KEY_252's third session), explicit instruction to try a new "positional/enumerable-context-list" framing borrowed from an unrelated JSX/TSX architecture discussion (disambiguate positionally via current grammar production, not via local backward lexical scanning) rather than repeat the brace-depth-heuristic backward scan RDD_KEY_252 already exhausted. **Two sub-attempts within this session, both built, tested, and reverted; no code landed.** Sub-attempt 1: a forward one-pass "frame stack" -- treat every `{` as opening a fresh nested statement frame, so a candidate's statement-start is always its own frame's start, sidestepping brace classification entirely. Failed immediately, regressing 9 fixtures matching RDD_KEY_252 sub-attempt 1's exact list (`real_code_regressions_{27,33,44,45,46,62,156,165,169}_inp.kt`): under-measured, because a lambda argument's short body legitimately needs its ENCLOSING statement's prefix counted for width purposes (`val liveItems = _items.filter { ... }` -- frame-stack measured only from just after `{`, dropping the real prefix). Confirmed via direct diff on `real_code_regressions_27_inp.kt`. Sub-attempt 2: abandoned brace/semicolon classification altogether; a new `kotlinStatementStart` helper only extends `lineStartIndex` backward across consecutive pure line-wrap continuation newlines, using `parenDepth > 0` as a structural signal plus an enumerable leading/trailing operator/keyword continuation list (`KOTLIN_CONTINUATION_OPS`: arithmetic/comparison/logical/assignment operators, `?:`, `..`, `..<`, `->`, `::`, `.`, `?.`, `!!`; `KOTLIN_CONTINUATION_KEYWORDS`: `as`, `is`, `in`). This reduced `make test` failures from 9 to 7 (`real_code_regressions_{19,37,44,62,156,157,165}_inp.kt`), but flipped to the opposite failure mode -- over-measurement, wrongly wrapping short calls that fit on one line (e.g. `loadRecovery(this)` in `_19`, `cancelConsumed(cause)` in `_37`). Root-caused via direct diff: a trailing `->` on the physical line immediately before the candidate's own line (a lambda-arrow header, e.g. `.setPositiveButton("Ok") { _, _ ->`) was misclassified as a same-statement continuation operator, when structurally it starts a NEW statement sequence (the lambda body), not a textual continuation of the header text before it. As a bounded follow-up experiment (not committed as a fix, purely diagnostic), removed `->` from `KOTLIN_CONTINUATION_OPS`: failures dropped further, from 7 to 4 (`real_code_regressions_{44,62,156,165}_inp.kt`), confirming the `->` hypothesis, but immediately exposed the identical false-positive class one token over -- `real_code_regressions_62_inp.kt`'s `is Right -> right.add(either.value)` (a `when`-branch pattern-match arm) was still misclassified as a continuation via `is` in `KOTLIN_CONTINUATION_KEYWORDS`, which is only valid for the mid-expression type-check operator (`x is T`), not a `when`-branch-leading keyword -- exact same ambiguity shape as `->`, just on a different token. `real_code_regressions_44_inp.kt` showed a third, independent failure mode: the backward walk is textually "correct" (a genuine `=`-continuation into an expression-bodied function's `if`/`else` body), yet the fits-check's result is undesired because that statement is an intentionally multi-line expression body that was never supposed to be judged against a single collapsed-line width. **Assessment: the positional/enumerable-context-list framing did not hold up.** It measurably narrowed each round's failure set (9 -> 7 -> 4) but never converged, because nearly every operator/keyword usable as a "continuation signal" is genuinely context-dependent in Kotlin without production-level grammar awareness -- `->` is both a lambda-arrow (starts a new statement) and (in other constructs) a same-line arrow; `is`/`as`/`in` are both mid-expression operators and `when`-branch-leading keywords; `=` is both a continuation and a legitimate multi-line-forever expression-body marker. An enumerable flat token list cannot carry the positional/production information the framing's own premise said was the missing ingredient -- resolving this needs to track which CONSTRUCT (lambda body, `when` branch, expression-bodied function, binary expression) a NEWLINE sits inside, which is real lightweight parsing, not a lookup table over adjacent tokens. This converges with, rather than merely repeats, RDD_KEY_221/226/235/252's conclusion, now confirmed under a structurally different framing too. Reverted in full (`git checkout -- src/com/jxmake/formatter/rules/MiscRuleCurly.java`); `make test` reconfirmed clean, zero net change, all tests passing (same baseline as immediately before this session; XL.txt/jar diffs pre-existed this session and were left untouched per STATE_COMMON.md). No fixture added (no fix landed). **Recommendation for any future session on D3: do not attempt another variant of "backward scan + token/keyword lookup table," under any framing (brace-depth, continuation-newline, or positional/enumerable) -- three independent framings across four sessions have now exhausted this heuristic family with bounded effort each time.** The two concretely-viable remaining directions are RDD_KEY_252's: (a) a real lightweight Kotlin statement/expression-boundary parser that tracks construct kind as it descends, not just token identity, built once and reused; or (b) new GDR-adjacent infrastructure with visibility into a single pipeline pass's own sibling-candidate interactions (RDD_KEY_235 already established GDR's existing per-line depth counters have no such visibility as-is). D3 remains open. |

| RDD_KEY_259 | STATE_TOOLING.md scoping session (job not yet implemented, docs-only decision). Comment normalization for Makefile/Bash/PowerShell (all three use `#` line comments) reuses the existing shared comment-classifier pipeline (`CommentClassifier`/`GruAbstainResolver`/`GruClassifier` in `com.jxmake.formatter.classifier`/`.gru`) via whichever family rule class ends up owning their comment handling -- not a new bespoke comment-normalization path. Confirmed by inspection that the classifier is already wired as language-agnostic infrastructure: it operates on comment TEXT content (grammar/capitalization/decorative-separator/license-block heuristics), not language syntax, and its existing callers span every implemented family (`MiscRuleCore`, `MiscRuleCurly`, `MiscRuleIndent`, plus `JsonSpecificRule`/`YamlSpecificRule`/`TomlSpecificRule`/`CssSpecificRule`/`XmlSpecificRule` directly) -- there is no per-language classifier variant to opt into or skip. GRU abstain-resolution specifically is not a separate per-language decision either: `MiscRuleCore.java` falls through to `GruAbstainResolver.resolve` automatically whenever the linear classifier abstains, gated only by the single global `config.isGruClassifier()` flag (`Config.java`) -- so routing Makefile/Bash/PowerShell comments through the shared entry point gets linear-classifier + GRU-abstain-resolution for free, identically to every other language, with no new gating logic needed. |
| RDD_KEY_260 | **REVERSES RDD_KEY_259** -- RDD_KEY_259's premise was factually wrong. Precise re-check (`grep -rln "CommentClassifier\|GruAbstainResolver\|GruClassifier" src/com/jxmake/formatter/`) found the shared comment-classifier pipeline (linear classifier + GRU abstain-resolution) is called from exactly one place, `MiscRuleCore` via `FormatterCurly` -- it is **curly-family-only** (C/C++/Java/Kotlin/JS/TS), not language-agnostic infrastructure. Python3 (Indent family), XML/HTML5 (Tags family), and every data format (JSON/JSON5/CSS/YAML/TOML) each have their own much simpler ad hoc comment handling instead -- e.g. `TomlSpecificRule.normComment`: optional first-letter capitalization only, gated by `config.isNormalizeCommentStartCase()`, no classifier dependency at all. Corrected decision (user-confirmed): Makefile/Bash/PowerShell `#` comments follow the TOML-style ad hoc pattern (simple, config-gated, no classifier reuse) if/when comment normalization is added for them at all -- not the curly-only classifier pipeline. `STYLE_TOOLING.md` §0 and `STATE_TOOLING.md`'s RDD table/checklist updated accordingly. |
