Makefile and PowerShell get plain first-letter-cap with no list (Makefile comments rarely need one; PowerShell's own keyword/cmdlet surface is too broad to usefully curate one for this narrow beautification-only job) -- user-confirmed. No linear-classifier or GRU training-corpus change made or needed: this is a purely mechanical/deterministic word-lookup, not a classifier decision, so `tools/gru`'s corpus-acquire step does not apply. Local fixtures `test/{bash,makefile,powershell}_combined_{inp,out}.*` updated: each `_out` fixture's copyright-header trailing period stripped and a few lowercase comment openers capitalized (Bash's `grep`/tool-name comment lines deliberately still lowercase, proving the exception list). `make test` reconfirmed clean (251/251 forward + idempotency) after the `_out` fixture updates. |
| RDD_KEY_262 | User-requested follow-up ("wire `normalize-comment-end-period` to JSON5/TOML/YAML/CSS/others not yet wired"). Precise re-check (`grep`ping every `Formatter*.java`/`*SpecificRule.java` for `isNormalizeCommentEndPeriod`) found it was wired ONLY into the curly/indent/tags families (`MiscRuleCore`) plus, as of RDD_KEY_261, Makefile/Bash/PowerShell -- every "SimpleBraced"/ad-hoc data format (JSON/JSON5, CSS, TOML, YAML) AND, previously unnoticed by the user's own framing, XML/HTML5 (`XmlSpecificRule.normComment`) had NEVER had it wired at all, despite already having `normalize-comment-start-case` wired since RDD_KEY_190-era implementation. Fixed for all six: JSON/JSON5 and CSS (both "SimpleBraced", RDD_KEY_190) share a new `FormatterSimpleBraced.stripCommentEndPeriod(commentText)` static helper -- delimiter-aware (`//`/`/* */`-prefixed `commentText`, same shape `capitalizeCommentStart` already takes), strips a sole trailing `.` immediately before the closing delimiter (or absolute end for `//`), dot-counted across the whole comment (so a multi-line `/* */` block token, which can embed real newlines as one token, is handled correctly) -- same "only if it's the only `.` anywhere" rule as everywhere else, ellipsis (`...`) survives for free. TOML/YAML's `#`-comment shape exactly matches Makefile/Bash/PowerShell's (leading `#`, no closing delimiter), so their `normComment` reuses `ToolingCommentNormalizer.stripSoleTrailingPeriod` directly (made package-private, not `private`, specifically to allow this reuse across the two `rules`-package families) rather than duplicating the same ~15 lines a third time. XML/HTML5's `normComment` operates on already-delimiter-stripped, already-trimmed, guaranteed-single-line `text` (multi-line `<!-- -->` comments freeze verbatim before ever reaching `normComment`, per RDD_KEY_232) -- simplest case, also reuses `ToolingCommentNormalizer.stripSoleTrailingPeriod` directly. HTML5's `<style>`/`<script>` splice paths (`XmlSpecificRule.renderScriptOrStyle`) also updated to thread the new flag through to the spliced `CssSpecificRule`/JS `Config` overrides, so a `<style>`/`<script>` block's own end-period normalization matches its enclosing HTML file's setting instead of silently defaulting off. All five rule-class constructors (`JsonSpecificRule`/`CssSpecificRule`/`TomlSpecificRule`/`YamlSpecificRule`/`XmlSpecificRule`) gained a new trailing `boolean normalizeCommentEndPeriod` overload (existing shorter constructors delegate with `false`, preserving old call-site/test compatibility); all five `Formatter*.java` dispatchers updated to pass `config.isNormalizeCommentEndPeriod()`. No new config keys (reused the existing global one, same posture as RDD_KEY_261). 5 pre-existing YAML fixtures (`test/real_code_regressions_{71,72,73,75,83}_out.yaml`) needed regeneration -- each had a copyright-header comment ending in a sole `.` that is now correctly stripped; regenerated from the live JAR, idempotency-verified, then copied over. `make test`: 251/251 forward + idempotency, zero regressions elsewhere (JSON/JSON5/CSS/TOML/XML/HTML5 local fixtures all had zero sole-trailing-period comments already, so none needed updating). |
| RDD_KEY_263 | Fixed the long-deferred "`utils.ts`/`fp/_baseConvert.js` switch-case fallthrough non-idempotency" gap tracked in STATE_JS_TS.md's "Known open issues" since the `vuejs/core`/`lodash/lodash` dogfood passes. Reproduced against real downloaded files (`vuejs/core`@`b5f8518` `packages/compiler-sfc/src/script/utils.ts`, `lodash/lodash`@`a666ba5` `lodash.js` -- the latter, not `fp/_baseConvert.js` as the older write-up's file reference implied; `initCloneByTag` lives in `lodash.js` itself, `fp/_baseConvert.js` never contained it): only `lodash.js`'s `initCloneByTag` typed-array fallthrough case (`vuejs/core`'s `utils.ts` switch is too short in its current shape at that pinned commit to overflow `lineLengthLimit` and trigger the bug, though the same root cause applies to it in principle). Root cause: `FormatterCurly.format`'s first `switchRule.formatNonInlineSwitches` call (~line 285, phase 1) decides whether a switch needs STYLE.md #13's blank-line-around-multiline-case-body treatment via the pure predicate `isNonInline` ("does any case body span more than one physical source line") -- evaluated *before* `switchRule.alignInlineSwitches`'s case-grid collapse (phase 3, ~line 300) and the `miscRule.enforceCallLineBreaking` passes (phase 1 ~line 286/376-377) have run. A grid-aligned case whose padded line overflows `lineLengthLimit` only becomes genuinely multi-line (its trailing call wrapped across lines) as a *result* of those later passes -- invisible to the first `isNonInline` check on a fresh format (round1 stays single-line pre-wrap at check time, so no blank lines inserted), but visible on a reformat of round1's own output (already-wrapped multi-line shape from the start), which correctly inserts the blank lines -- round1 != round2. Fix: added a second `switchRule.formatNonInlineSwitches` call in `FormatterCurly.format` near the end of Phase 4 (right after the final `enforceComplexityPadding`, before Phase 5's file-header-structure passes), after every pass capable of turning a case body multi-line has settled. Safe/idempotent: `isNonInline`/`needsWork` are pure predicates re-evaluated against the current token stream each call, so a switch already correctly blank-line-annotated (or never needing it) is a no-op; the second call only ever inserts blank lines into gaps (`ensureBlankLineInGap`), never touches `alignInlineSwitches`'s own grid/column-alignment output, matching the observed round1-vs-round2 diff shape exactly (only two inserted blank lines, grid content unchanged). Shared `SwitchRule`/`FormatterCurly` code (C/C++/Java/Kotlin/JS/TS curly family) -- fix applies uniformly, not JS/TS-specific; STATE_C_CPP_JAVA.md's "Known Gaps" cross-referenced/updated accordingly. New permanent fixture `test/real_code_regressions_183_{inp,out}.js` (minimized `initCloneByTag` shape, registered in Makefile's `INP_FILES` and `test/README.txt`). `make test`: 253/253 forward + idempotency (was 252/252 before the fixture), zero regressions across every curly-family language's existing fixtures. Both real downloaded files (`utils.ts`, `lodash.js`, and `fp/_baseConvert.js` for completeness) now round1==round2 byte-identical. |
| RDD_KEY_264 | XML/HTML5 multi-line `<!-- -->` banner-shape comment normalization (user-directed follow-up: give `<!-- -->` the same treatment curly gives `/* */` -- single-line vs. already-conventional multi-line banner shape). Previously RDD_KEY_232 froze EVERY multi-line `<!-- -->` comment byte-for-byte verbatim regardless of shape; that's only equivalent to curly's non-banner-shape branch. New `XmlSpecificRule.tryBannerShape(raw)` mirrors `MiscRuleCore.reformatMultiLineBlockComment`'s shape check (every continuation line must start with `*` after stripping leading whitespace) adapted for `<!--`/`-->` markers living outside the raw interior (unlike curly's `/*`/`*/` embedded in the first/last physical lines) -- the line immediately before the closing `-->` is exempt from the `*`-prefix check and contributes no content line since it's just that marker's own leading indentation, not itself a continuation line. When the shape matches: capitalize the first content line (respecting the same `isSingleWordDirective`/`isMarkupFragmentDirective` exclusions as the single-line path), strip a sole trailing `.` across the whole comment, and reindent at render time to `indent(depth)` + " * " per line, with the closing `-->` flush at the comment's own `indent(depth)` (deliberately NOT offset by an extra space the way curly's `*/` is -- user explicitly rejected the curly-style alignment space, decided the closing marker just needs to line up with the comment's own indent, nothing more). When the shape doesn't match, falls back to the pre-existing RDD_KEY_232 freeze-verbatim behavior unchanged. New `Node.commentBannerLines` field (non-null iff banner-shaped) drives a new render branch ahead of the existing `commentVerbatim` check. Two pre-existing fixtures whose copyright header already happened to use the `* `-per-line banner style (`real_code_regressions_112_out.html`, `real_code_regressions_125_out.html`) were re-verified against the live JAR and found byte-identical to their prior committed content (the closing `-->` was already flush, no change needed once the alignment-space idea was dropped). New fixture `test/html_multiline_comment_banner_{inp,out}.html` (banner-shaped comment gets capitalize + sole-trailing-period-strip + reindent). `make test`: 254/254 forward + idempotency, zero regressions. Scoped narrowly to XML/HTML5's `<!-- -->` per the user's decision #2 (data-formats job) -- JSON5/SimpleBraced `/* */` banner-shape parity, tooling/`#`-comment chain-grouping, and Python3 comment normalization from scratch (decisions #1/#3/#4 of the same brief) were NOT attempted in this session due to scope -- left as open items in each job's own state file. |
| RDD_KEY_265 | JSON/JSON5/CSS "SimpleBraced" comment normalization gains curly's grouping shape (data-formats job, decision #1 of the 2026-08-08 brief also covering RDD_KEY_264). New `FormatterSimpleBraced.normalizeComment`/`normalizeCommentTrivia`/`normalizeLineCommentChain`/`normalizeBlockComment` (+ private `tryBannerShape`, mirroring `MiscRuleCore.reformatMultiLineBlockComment`/`XmlSpecificRule.tryBannerShape` but with no classifier/keyword-exclusion gate, since JSON/CSS have no language keywords a comment could start with needing protection): consecutive standalone `//` comments (JSON5 only -- confirmed via the tokenizer that JSON/CSS never lex `//`) with no blank line between now chain-group like curly's `//` (only the first comment's start is capitalized; a sole trailing `.` is stripped only if it's the only one across the whole chain, and only from the chain's last comment). A multi-line `/* */` block comment already in the conventional ` * `-per-line continuation-marker banner shape gets the same curly-style single-unit treatment (capitalize only the first content line, strip a sole trailing `.` across all content lines); any other multi-line shape (e.g. wrapped prose whose content starts right on the opening `/*` line, with no `* `-per-line continuation) falls back to the pre-existing whole-comment-scan behavior (capitalize the opening line's first letter, strip a sole trailing `.` across the whole raw text) rather than being left untouched -- an early version of this fix wrongly left non-banner multi-line comments completely unnormalized, caught by `test/css_comments_*` regressing (a genuine 2-sentence, non-banner multi-line comment lost its capitalization entirely) before landing. `JsonSpecificRule.collectTrivia` now tracks a parallel raw-text + blank-before-this-comment list and defers normalization to `normalizeCommentTrivia` once the whole leading-trivia span is collected (previously normalized each comment token independently, in isolation, as it was collected); `CssSpecificRule` only needed the block-comment banner-shape path (CSS never lexes `//`, so no chain-grouping is possible there). User-clarified test-fixture judgment call during this session: two adjacent standalone `//` comments with no `.` anywhere are correctly merged into one chain (only the first capitalized) -- `test/json5_comments_out.json5` regenerated for this (pre-existing fixture's second leading comment, "second leading comment, stacked", has no period and is not an independent sentence, so grouping+lowercase-preserving is correct); a multi-line `/* */` comment that DOES contain multiple genuine sentences (each ending in `.`) must keep each sentence's own capitalization, which is exactly what the whole-comment-scan fallback (not the banner-shape path, since that fixture's continuation line has no leading `*` marker) already does. New fixture `test/json5_comment_banner_{inp,out}.json5` (banner-shaped `/* */` comment demonstrating the new capitalize-first-content-line-only treatment). `make test`: 254/254 -> 255/255 forward + idempotency, zero regressions. New fixtures `yaml_comment_chain_{inp,out}.yaml`, `toml_comment_chain_{inp,out}.toml`; 5 pre-existing YAML fixtures (`real_code_regressions_{71,72,73,75,83}_out.yaml`) regenerated for the newly-correct chain-grouped capitalization of their existing adjacent comment blocks (user regenerated/verified these directly). `make test`: 255/255 -> 257/257 forward + idempotency, zero regressions. |
| RDD_KEY_267 | Makefile/Bash/PowerShell `#`-comment chain-grouping parity with curly (tooling job, decision #3 of the 2026-08-08 brief, sharing `ToolingCommentNormalizer.normalizeChain` with the data-formats job's YAML/TOML implementation, RDD_KEY_266). All three previously called `ToolingCommentNormalizer.normalize` on each `#` comment independently as it was tokenized/collected (RDD_KEY_261-era, no grouping); now defer to `normalizeChain` across each run of strictly consecutive standalone comment lines (own-line `#...`, not a trailing comment after code). Makefile (`MakefileSpecificRule.parseBlock`'s `COMMENT_LINE` branch): line-oriented already, simplest case -- on hitting a comment line, scans forward collecting every immediately-following comment line (loop naturally stops at the first non-comment line, including blank lines), normalizes the whole run via `normalizeChain`, re-emits. Bash and PowerShell (`BashSpecificRule`/`PowerShellSpecificRule` `runPassA`) are character-level tokenizers that normalize/emit each comment inline as soon as its closing newline is hit, with no forward lookahead -- grouping requires deferring normalization until the whole file has been scanned. Solution: a new `Frame.standalone`/`Frame.lineNo` pair computed when a `#` comment frame is pushed (`standalone` = everything before the `#` on its source line is whitespace-only, found by scanning backward to the previous newline; `lineNo` = 0-based source line index, found by counting newlines before that point) drives a new deferred path -- a standalone comment's raw body is stored in a new `ChainEntry` list together with a unique placeholder marker (a control-character-delimited `CHAINn` token so no `CHAINn` prefix can collide with a longer `CHAINn`-plus-digits token during later substring replacement) emitted into the pass-A buffer in the body's place instead of normalized text; a non-standalone (trailing) comment is still normalized immediately as before, never deferred, since it can never participate in a chain. After pass A finishes, a new `resolveChainEntries` groups the collected entries into chains wherever `lineNo` is strictly consecutive (a gap -- from a blank line, a code line, or a trailing comment sitting between two standalone ones -- naturally breaks the chain since the gap shows up as a non-plus-one jump in `lineNo`), calls `normalizeChain` per chain, and substitutes each placeholder for its final text via `String.replace`. Also applied to the last comment in a file lacking a trailing newline (the end-of-scan flush, previously always normalizing immediately, now takes the same standalone-check-and-defer branch). Bash keeps its existing `NO_CAPITALIZE_TOOLS` word-exception list threaded through `normalizeChain`'s capitalization step; Makefile/PowerShell pass `null` (plain cap only), matching RDD_KEY_261's original per-language posture. Existing fixtures `makefile_combined_out.mk`, `bash_combined_out.sh`, `powershell_combined_out.ps1`, and `real_code_regressions_182_out.ps1` all needed regeneration: each has a 4-line standalone comment block (blank `#`, a copyright line ending in a literal `.`, an SPDX line with no `.`, a trailing blank `#`) whose old per-comment-only normalization incorrectly stripped the copyright line's period (each comment line's own period count was evaluated in isolation, and the copyright line alone has exactly one `.`); the new chain-grouped normalization correctly leaves it alone, because the chain's dot-count-across-the-whole-chain is still 1, but the strip step only ever touches the chain's last comment (the trailing blank line), which has no trailing `.` to strip -- exactly mirroring curly's own "strip only from the chain's last comment" semantics, and fixing a real (if minor) latent bug in the RDD_KEY_261 implementation rather than just adding new behavior. User regenerated/verified these fixtures directly from the live JAR. `make test`: 257/257 forward + idempotency (unchanged count from RDD_KEY_266 -- no new fixtures added this round, only existing ones corrected), zero regressions. This closes out decision #3 of the 2026-08-08 brief; only decision #4 (python3 comment normalization from scratch, STATE_PYTHON3.md) remains. |

A reformat of that already-widened output measures the wider text from the start and drops the target to its own line — round1 != round2. **Fix:** pulled `enforceUnionIntersectionSpacing`/`enforceTypeColonSpacing` (TS-only) forward to run immediately before `enforceDecoratorOverflowCascade` (not merely before `enforceCallLineBreaking`, where the file's other pulled-forward width-affecting passes already sit) — same fix shape as the existing `enforceComplexityPadding`/`enforceAttributeAndSpliceBracketPadding`/`enforceInitializerBraceSpacing` precedent already in `FormatterCurly.format`, just anchored one pass earlier since `enforceDecoratorOverflowCascade` is the first width-driven pass in the pipeline, not `enforceCallLineBreaking`. The original Phase 4 calls of both passes are left in place too (idempotent no-ops on anything this earlier call already normalized). Both fixes are structurally lower-risk than RDD_KEY_270's family: neither touches `ScopePipelineCurly.process`/`reapplyClosingBraceAndDeclarationsPass`/the `closingBraceAndDeclarationsOnly` gate — (a) is entirely inside `JsTsSpecificRule.tryParseClassField`'s own token scan, (b) is a pure Phase-1 pass-reordering in `FormatterCurly.format`, independent of `ScopePipelineCurly` entirely — so RDD_KEY_246's exact failure mode (a regression via that shared step) cannot recur here, verified by inspection (grep for `ScopePipelineCurly`/`closingBraceAndDeclarationsOnly` in the diff, zero hits) rather than merely assumed safe. Both fixes verified via A/B rebuild: reverted (`git checkout` on the two touched files), rebuilt, reproduced both documented symptoms byte-for-byte on both real repro files; restored, rebuilt, confirmed both real repro files fully idempotent (round1==round2) and `js_ts_syntax_check.sh` clean on both. `make test`: 260/260 -> 261/261 forward + idempotency (260 pre-existing + 1 new combined fixture), zero regressions anywhere in the existing suite. New fixture `test/real_code_regressions_186_{inp,out}.ts` (a minimized `Sample`/`Widget` class pair combining both bugs in one file, registered in the Makefile's `INP_FILES` and `test/README.txt`) — confirmed via the same A/B rebuild to reproduce both bugs pre-fix and stay idempotent post-fix. | RDD_KEY_272 | Tooling job, pure refactor (no behavior change) -- the RDD_KEY_267 `#`-comment chain-grouping deferred-placeholder mechanism (`ChainEntry` record class, per-comment placeholder-marker generation/tracking, and the `resolveChainEntries` grouping/normalize/substitute method) had been implemented twice, nearly identically, once each in `BashSpecificRule.runPassA` and `PowerShellSpecificRule.runPassA` -- both character-level tokenizers needing the same deferred-until-end-of-scan mechanism for the same reason (no lookahead), unlike Makefile's line-oriented tokenizer, which does a simple forward lookahead instead and never needed this mechanism at all. Confirmed real by reading both files in full: the `ChainEntry` class (13 lines), the two placeholder-creation call sites (mid-scan newline handler + end-of-scan flush), and the 22-line `resolveChainEntries` method were structurally identical between the two files, differing only in the marker text passed through -- both actually already used the same control-character-delimited `CHAIN<n>` marker scheme (an initial pass with the Read tool appeared to show PowerShell using an undelimited plain `CHAIN<n>` marker, which would have been a distinct, previously-unknown collision-risk bug, but this was confirmed to be a Read-tool display artifact stripping the BEL control character rather than a real difference -- `git show HEAD:...PowerShellSpecificRule.java` confirmed the on-disk bytes already matched Bash's delimited scheme, so no such bug existed and none was introduced by this refactor). Extracted into a new package-private nested class `ToolingCommentNormalizer.ChainCollector`: constructor takes the caller's own marker prefix/suffix so each language's exact existing marker text is preserved unchanged; `defer(body, lineNo)` allocates and stores an entry, returning its placeholder; `resolve(transformed, normalizeStartCase, normalizeEndPeriod, noCapitalizeWords)` performs the grouping/`normalizeChain`/substitution, delegating to the already-existing `ToolingCommentNormalizer.normalizeChain`. `BashSpecificRule`/`PowerShellSpecificRule` each now hold one `ChainCollector` field in `runPassA`, replacing their own `ChainEntry` class, `chainEntries` list, `chainSeq` counter, and `resolveChainEntries` method entirely -- both placeholder call sites now call `chainCollector.defer(...)`, and the final substitution calls `chainCollector.resolve(...)`. Makefile's simpler lookahead-based `parseBlock` was untouched (nothing to extract there). `ToolingCommentNormalizer` is a shared cross-job file (also used by the data-formats job's YAML/TOML `#`-comment normalization, RDD_KEY_266/`c064018`/`0e8da9e`) -- this change only adds a new nested class and does not alter any existing method's signature, so it stays fully backward-compatible with those other callers with no changes needed on their side. `make test`: 261/261 forward + idempotency both before and after the refactor (identical count, output confirmed byte-identical against the pre-refactor baseline via the full test suite -- pure refactor, no fixture changes). |
| RDD_KEY_273 | JS/TS job, `microsoft/TypeScript` 2026-08-09 dogfood-reconfirmation shape #1 (braceless if/else-if body-column-alignment padding going stale across a second format pass, tracked in XL.txt Tier 3) -- root-caused via `JXFMT_DEBUG_ELSEIF`-env-gated `System.err` instrumentation (same pattern as the `watchPublic.ts` fix) added to `BlockStructureRule.alignBracelessElseIfChain`'s body-padding loop. All 3 flagged files (`compiler/builder.ts`, `compiler/moduleNameResolver.ts`, `services/findAllReferences.ts`) are CRLF-original source. This method splits its working text on `"\n"` only (not `"\r\n"`), so every line retains a trailing `'\r'` while formatting CRLF-original text -- `Main.applyLineEndings`'s own comment already documents that CRLF/LF normalization happens exactly once, at the very end, precisely because "the internal formatting pipeline is not guaranteed to have stripped every original '\r'". That stray extra character skews every length computation this method makes (`end`, `target`, `body.length()`), which is invisible almost everywhere except the exact `lineLengthLimit`-boundary guard deciding whether to apply body-column padding to a branch: on round1 (still-CRLF text mid-pipeline) a branch's padded width computes to exactly the limit (guard passes, no pad applied) or one-under after the `\r` is subtracted; on round2 (fed round1's own already-LF-only output, one byte narrower per line) the same computation lands on the other side of the same boundary, flipping the guard's decision -- non-idempotent. Confirmed directly, not just theorized: debug prints showed identical `end`/`target`/`spaces` values both rounds but `bodyLen` differing by exactly 1 (44 vs 43), traced to a literal trailing `\r` byte in the round1 line via `od -c`. **Fix:** strip any trailing `'\r'` from each split line immediately after `alignBracelessElseIfChain`'s initial `split("\n", -1)`, before any measurement -- safe to simply drop rather than restore, since `Main.applyLineEndings` independently re-derives the final output's line-ending style from the *original* file text, never from `'\r'` bytes surviving inside the internal pipeline (confirmed by reading that method: its `"preserve"` branch calls `detectDominantLineEnding(original)`, not on any internal formatted string). One-line-loop change, `BlockStructureRule.java`, no new config key. Verified: A/B rebuild (reverted the fix via `git checkout`, rebuilt, reproduced the exact stale-padding symptom on both the new minimal fixture and `compiler/moduleNameResolver.ts` directly; restored, rebuilt, confirmed both idempotent) plus direct re-verification against a fresh `microsoft/TypeScript` shallow clone (`/tmp/typescript_reconfirm`) -- all 3 originally-flagged files (`builder.ts`, `moduleNameResolver.ts`, `findAllReferences.ts`) now format idempotently (round1==round2). `make test`: 271/271 -> 272/272 forward + idempotency (271 pre-existing + 1 new fixture), zero regressions. New fixture `test/real_code_regressions_195_{inp,out}.ts`, deliberately CRLF-encoded (`.gitattributes` marks it `-text`, same precedent as `real_code_regressions_147_inp.ts`) since the bug is CRLF-input-specific; distilled from `compiler/moduleNameResolver.ts`'s `if(options.resolvePackageJsonExports) features |= ...` / `else if(... === false) features &= ~NodeResolutionFeatures.Exports;` pair. Debug instrumentation (`JXFMT_DEBUG_ELSEIF`-env-gated) fully removed before landing. This CRLF-trailing-`\r`-skews-length-guard mechanism is a plausible shared root cause for some/all of the other 3 still-open Tier-3 shapes (all their flagged files are also CRLF-original, per `file` on the fresh clone) -- see the Tier 3 shapes 2/4 follow-up findings for confirmation on a per-shape basis, not assumed here. |
| RDD_KEY_274 | JS/TS job, `microsoft/TypeScript` 2026-08-09 dogfood-reconfirmation shape #2 (class-field `:`-type-annotation column-alignment group splitting apart across a second format pass, tracked in XL.txt Tier 3) -- root-caused via `JXFMT_DEBUG_CFG`-env-gated `System.err` instrumentation added to `JsTsSpecificRule.flushClassFieldGroup`/the field-processing loop in `rewriteClassFieldGroups`/`blankLineBetween`. Both flagged files (`server/editorServices.ts`, `server/project.ts`) are CRLF-original source, same corpus as RDD_KEY_273, but this bug's actual mechanism turned out unrelated to CRLF/`'\r'` -- confirmed by direct debug-print comparison: `fromIdx`/`toIdx` token-range boundaries were identical between round1 and round2 except for one extra token at the end of the range, and the raw token dump showed the difference was a genuine extra NEWLINE token, not a stray `'\r'` character. Concretely: `readonly configFileExistenceInfoCache: Map<...> = new Map();` followed by `/** @internal */ readonly throttledOperations: ThrottledOperations;` -- in the ORIGINAL source (and therefore round1's input) the leading comment sits on the *same physical line* as the field it documents (space-separated, no NEWLINE between them), so the two fields' gap contains exactly 1 NEWLINE (the one ending the first field's line) and `blankLineBetween`'s old "count total NEWLINE tokens in the gap, blank if >= 2" logic correctly judged no blank line, keeping both fields in one alignment group (round1 pads `throttledOperations`'s column to match `configFileExistenceInfoCache`'s wider one). But `flushClassFieldGroup` unconditionally renders every leading comment on its *own* line (comment, NEWLINE, indent, field) regardless of whether the source had it inline -- so round1's own rendered output moves the comment to its own line, adding a NEWLINE that was never in the original. Round2, tokenizing that already-rendered text, now finds 2 NEWLINEs in the same logical gap (one after the previous field's `;`, one after the now-own-line comment) and `blankLineBetween`'s old count-based check wrongly judges this a genuine blank line, splitting the two fields into separate one-field groups -- each falls back to its own natural (narrower, unpadded) column width, non-idempotent. **Fix:** redefined `blankLineBetween` to require two NEWLINE tokens strictly *back-to-back* (only WHITESPACE tokens permitted between them, any other token type -- in particular a COMMENT -- resets the "just saw a NEWLINE" state) rather than merely counting total NEWLINEs anywhere in the gap; this matches a genuine blank source line (two adjacent NEWLINEs, at most indentation whitespace between) while correctly not matching a comment's own forced line break (a NEWLINE, then a COMMENT token, then another NEWLINE -- the COMMENT in between breaks the back-to-back requirement). Verified: A/B rebuild (reverted via `git checkout`, rebuilt, reproduced the exact stale-column-collapse symptom on both the new minimal fixture and `server/editorServices.ts`/`server/project.ts` directly; restored, rebuilt, confirmed all idempotent) plus direct re-verification against the fresh `microsoft/TypeScript` shallow clone (`/tmp/typescript_reconfirm`) -- both originally-flagged files now format idempotently (round1==round2). `make test`: 272/272 -> 273/273 forward + idempotency (272 pre-existing incl. RDD_KEY_273's fixture + 1 new fixture), zero regressions. New fixture `test/real_code_regressions_196_{inp,out}.ts`, a minimized `Foo` class distilled directly from `server/editorServices.ts`'s `configFileExistenceInfoCache`/`throttledOperations` pair (no CRLF needed -- this bug reproduces on plain LF input, confirming it is a distinct root cause from RDD_KEY_273 despite sharing the same flagged-file corpus). Debug instrumentation (`JXFMT_DEBUG_CFG`-env-gated) fully removed before landing. |
| RDD_KEY_275 | JS/TS job, `microsoft/TypeScript` 2026-08-09 dogfood-reconfirmation shape #4 (closing `}` non-idempotently gaining a stale `// if` trailing-annotation comment across a second format pass, tracked in XL.txt Tier 3, flagged file `services/codefixes/fixMissingTypeAnnotationOnExports.ts`) -- investigated via A/B bisection rather than a full independent root-cause trace. The comment-adding logic lives in `BlockStructureRule.decideComment`/`countContentLines`, which counts `NEWLINE` tokens between a block's open/close braces against a `closingCommentMinLines` threshold -- not the same raw-split-on-`"\n"`-string-length mechanism RDD_KEY_273 fixed, so this was not assumed to be the same bug. Root-cause tracing was blocked by minimization difficulty: small hand-crafted snippets, and even a slice of the exact affected function plus its neighbors wrapped in an isolating `function outer(){}`, did not reproduce the bug -- it needs broader real-file context. Line-range bisection directly against the real CRLF-original source (`/tmp/typescript_reconfirm/src/services/codefixes/fixMissingTypeAnnotationOnExports.ts`, 1176 lines) found the bug reproduces on the whole file and remains reproducible on a self-contained 100-line excerpt, lines 1050-1150 (a set of complete standalone functions, no unclosed braces) -- narrower ranges starting past line ~1060 lose reproduction. A/B rebuild on that excerpt conclusively isolated the cause: restoring `BlockStructureRule.java` to its pre-RDD_KEY_273 state (`git show d96e42e:...BlockStructureRule.java`) reproduces the exact `}` -> `} // if` diff; restoring the current RDD_KEY_273-fixed state (already committed, no further code change made here) makes the same excerpt idempotent. No new code change was needed or made for this shape -- RDD_KEY_273's CRLF trailing-`\r` strip in `alignBracelessElseIfChain` already fixes it as a side effect. The precise causal chain from that fix into `decideComment`/`countContentLines`'s NEWLINE-count threshold was not separately hand-traced (evidence-over-reasoning: A/B bisection is direct empirical evidence even without a fully walked mechanism) -- plausibly an earlier width-sensitive pass inside the same enclosing `if(typeNode){...}` block was itself CRLF-skewed by the same stray `\r`, changing that block's internal NEWLINE count across the `closingCommentMinLines` boundary between round1 and round2, independently of the if/else-if padding guard RDD_KEY_273 directly targeted. Verified: `make test` 273/273 -> 274/274 forward + idempotency (273 pre-existing incl. RDD_KEY_273/274's fixtures + 1 new fixture), zero regressions. New fixture `test/real_code_regressions_197_{inp,out}.ts`, a 100-line CRLF-encoded excerpt (lines 1050-1150) of the real `fixMissingTypeAnnotationOnExports.ts` (`.gitattributes` marks it `-text`, same precedent as `real_code_regressions_147_inp.ts`/`real_code_regressions_195_inp.ts`) -- exists purely to lock in this second symptom's coverage of the already-landed RDD_KEY_273 fix, not to introduce new logic. |
| RDD_KEY_276 | JS/TS job, `microsoft/TypeScript` 2026-08-09 dogfood-reconfirmation Tier-3 shape #3 (an interface's intersection-type closing brace `};` shifting from column 0 to indented on round2, tracked in XL.txt Tier 3, flagged file `compiler/types.ts`) -- root-caused via minimal repro isolation, not debug prints (bug was reproducible on a 3-line minimal `interface`). `compiler/types.ts`'s `JSDocAugmentsTag`/`JSDocImplementsTag` interfaces each have a field literally named `class` (`readonly class: ExpressionWithTypeArguments & { readonly expression: Identifier | PropertyAccessEntityNameExpression; };`) -- `class` is a reserved word but a legal TS property name. `JsTsSpecificRule.classBraceKind(tokens, braceIdx)` walks backward from a `{` looking for a `class`/`interface` KEYWORD token at depth 0 to classify the enclosing construct; the tokenizer tags `class` a KEYWORD by text alone regardless of whether it's used as a declaration keyword or a property name, so walking backward from the field's own nested inline object-type-literal `{ readonly expression: ...; }` brace, the scan hits the field's own name token `class` and misclassifies that nested brace as a class-declaration body. That misclassification feeds the nested brace into `enforceClassFieldAlignmentGrid`'s `allClassOpens` list, triggering the class-field alignment-grid rewrite on the object-type-literal's interior (`readonly expression: ...`) as if it were a top-level class field list -- producing corrupted, non-idempotent output (stray inline padding after the nested `{`, closing `};` mis-indented one level short) that differs between round1 and round2 because the alignment-grid rewrite's own byte-for-byte-vs-grid-rewritten decision is sensitive to exactly how mangled round1's own output already is. **Fix:** added `isFieldNameKeywordUsage(tokens, kwIdx)`, checking whether the token immediately following a candidate `class`/`interface` KEYWORD token is `:` or `?` (field-name usage) rather than an identifier (the construct's declared name) -- `classBraceKind`'s two branches now `continue` (keep walking backward) instead of returning a kind when that shape is seen. First attempt used `isPunct(t, ":")`, verified via `JXFMT_DEBUG_CBK`-env-gated `System.err` instrumentation (temporarily added, then fully removed) to never actually match -- `:` tokenizes as an `OP`-type token in this codebase's tokenizer, not `PUNCT`; corrected to `isOp(t, ":")` (kept `isPunct(t, "?")` as a fallback alongside `isOp(t, "?")` for the optional-marker case, since its own token type wasn't independently confirmed and this stays conservative either way). Verified: minimal 3-line repro round-trips idempotently pre/post fix (confirmed non-idempotent before, idempotent after); the full real `compiler/types.ts` (CRLF-original, ~9800 lines) also confirmed idempotent post-fix (was non-idempotent pre-fix, diff at the exact `JSDocAugmentsTag`/`JSDocImplementsTag` `};` lines matching the originally-reported symptom). `make test`: 274/274 -> 275/275 forward + idempotency (274 pre-existing incl. RDD_KEY_273/274/275's fixtures + 1 new fixture), zero regressions. New fixture `test/real_code_regressions_198_{inp,out}.ts`, a minimized `JSDocAugmentsTag`-shaped interface distilled directly from `compiler/types.ts` (plain LF, not CRLF-specific -- confirmed a distinct root cause from RDD_KEY_273/274/275's cluster despite sharing the same dogfood pass). Debug instrumentation (`JXFMT_DEBUG_CBK`-env-gated) fully removed before landing. |
| RDD_KEY_277 | Python3 job, §3 Import Ordering coverage gap closed (tracked in XL.txt TIER 9): `ScopePipelineIndent.classifyImport` previously rejected all three multi-physical-line import shapes outright (safely excluded from sort/grouping, but a real coverage gap on common real-world style) -- single-line multi-module `import a, b`, parenthesized `from X import (...)` (possibly spanning many physical lines), and backslash-continued `import a, \` / `b, c`. Extended `classifyImport` to parse and classify all three: the `import` branch now loops over a comma-separated dotted-name list (each optionally `as alias`) via `readDottedName`/`advancePastDottedName`, returning the pre-existing single-module `PyImport` shape when only one name is found (unchanged behavior) or the new multi-name resortable shape (`nameListStart`/`nameListEnd`/`unitTexts`) otherwise; the `from` branch detects a leading `(` via `matchBracket` and, if present, bounds the name-list scan to the parenthesized span and verifies well-formedness (a lone trailing comma before the close paren is tolerated, anything else bails safely via `return null`). A new `nextSignificantSkipBackslash` helper (skips a literal `\` OP token, which -- unlike WHITESPACE/NEWLINE/COMMENT -- is not one of `isGapToken`'s transparently-skipped types) is used at both branches' comma-continuation sites so a backslash-continued list's next name is found instead of mistaken for unrecognized trailing content. Two safety-critical fixes surfaced during real-code testing: (1) `advancePastDottedName` returns `-1` when the dotted name is the line's last significant token (e.g. the final module in `import c, a`) -- naively falling back to `line.end` for that unit's end index spliced the RawLine's terminating NEWLINE token's text into the last unit, corrupting the rebuild (`import c, a` -> `import a\n, c`); fixed by scanning backward via `prevSignificant(tokens, line.end - 1, unitStart - 1) + 1` instead. (2) A real `django` corpus file (`django/db/models/__init__.py`'s `from django.db.models.fields.related import (  # isort:skip` line) had its deliberately-unsorted, comment-headed multi-line import list flattened and resorted, destroying the `# isort:skip` marker's positional significance -- root cause: the new comment-detection guard (`containsComment`, which disables only the internal name-list resort, not cross-statement group movement, when any comment is found inside the parenthesized span) was scanned over `[nameListStart, closeIdx)`, which starts AFTER the comment's actual position (the comment sits right after the opening `(`, before any name); fixed by introducing `parenOpenIdx` and scanning `containsComment(tokens, parenOpenIdx, closeIdx)` -- the whole parenthesized span -- instead. Verified: existing `make test` unchanged at 275/275 forward + idempotency both before and after (271->275 across this session's other RDD_KEYs); a synthetic fixture covering all three shapes plus a per-name-commented parenthesized clause formats correctly and idempotently; a 2927-file `/tmp/django` corpus dogfood (zero crashes) found and fixed both bugs above, and a final re-run after both fixes found exactly 2 remaining non-idempotent files, both isolated via A/B testing (pre-change stash vs. post-change build on the same minimized input) to be **pre-existing, unrelated comment-normalization-classifier non-determinism** (a capitalization flip on a `# isort:skip` trailing comment, and a trailing-period-stripping flip on a `# RemovedInDjango70Warning.` trailing comment inside a parenthesized import) -- the first reproduces identically on the pre-change codebase in isolation (confirmed pre-existing, not caused by this change); the second only reproduces post-change because the parenthesized clause it lives in was previously frozen/untouched by the old reject-outright behavior and is now, correctly, exposed to the separate (already-flaky, out-of-scope for this job) comment-grammar classifier pass for the first time -- no import name/module content is lost or corrupted in either case, only an unrelated pass's comment-styling output differs between rounds. New fixture `test/py_import_multiline_inp/out.py` (feature fixture, not a regression fixture -- this closes a documented coverage gap rather than fixing a bug in previously-shipped behavior). |
| RDD_KEY_278 | **RESOLVES a regression in RDD_KEY_101/the `37c806a` `kotlin_content_diff.java` closing-comment-tolerance fix** -- `CONTROL_FLOW_CLOSING`'s inclusion of `when` in its "keyword + at most one trailing word" loose regex was too narrow (not too loose as first hypothesized), not too permissive: a `when (val x = expr)` capture-form subject is a multi-word expression (e.g. `when val accessDenied = error.suppressedExceptions.single()`, verbatim per `KotlinSpecificRule.formatWhenExpressions`'s own `subject = literalSlice(...).trim().replaceAll("\\s+", " ")`), so the one-trailing-word clause rejected this real, always-emitted closing comment as an unexplained addition -- a false MISMATCH, confirmed against `PathRecursiveFunctionsTest.kt`/`PathTreeWalkTest.kt`/`coreRuntime.kt` (all three had a genuine `// when val ... = ...` closing comment flagged as bogus "present in formatted, missing from original"). First fix attempt (widen `when`'s trailing clause to accept any text) was itself wrong in the other direction: it let a WRONG subject name (e.g. `// when val wrongName = x` on an actual `when (val accessDenied = x)`) slip through uncaught -- verified via negative control before landing, so reverted before commit. Landed fix instead removes `when` from the generic loose regex entirely and adds a new `collectWhenClosingComments`/`whenClosingComments`, mirroring `namedConstructClosingComments`'s already-verified-per-declaration precedent: walks the ORIGINAL file's real `KtWhenExpression` nodes, extracts the raw text between each one's own `(`/`)` (or bare `"when"` for a subject-less `when {}`), and only that construct's own real subject variant is consumed per when-expression -- a wrong/corrupted `when` closing comment is still flagged. Verified: all 4 originally-reported repro files now pass this specific check clean (2 fully OK, 2 have separate pre-existing unrelated declaration-content mismatches, unaffected by this fix either way); `37c806a`'s original positive case (`Promise.kt`) still passes; local `test/kt_combined_inp/out.kt` and `test/kt_comments_inp/out.kt` unaffected (same pre-existing unrelated mismatches as documented in `37c806a`'s own entry); two new negative controls (a wrong-name multi-word `when(val ...)` closing comment, and a wrong one-word bare-`when` closing comment) both still correctly flagged. No `test/` fixture files modified; no formatter source changed, `tools/verifiers/kotlin_content_diff.java` only. |
| RDD_KEY_279 | Kotlin job, OPEN ITEM (2026-08-10) resolved: trailing-comma drop on a multi-line call/list-literal's "preserve original line groups" render path (STYLE_KOTLIN.md §7.2 -- a trailing comma must be preserved exactly as written, never added or stripped). Root cause was generic (not Kotlin-specific) in `MiscRuleCurly`: `groupByOriginalLine` silently drops a dangling trailing empty group produced by a source trailing comma before `)` before the caller ever sees it, and `renderCallPreserveGroups`/`renderCallDropped`/`renderCallOnePerLine` each independently withhold a comma on the very last rendered item unconditionally -- correct for C/C++/Java (not idiomatic there) but wrong for Kotlin. Fixed by adding `hasTrailingComma(paramsSlice)` (checks the raw slice's last significant token before any splitting discards the signal) and gating a `keepTrailingComma` exception on `lang.isKotlin` in all three render methods -- `renderCallDropped` appends `,` to the collapsed one-line args text when set; `renderCallOnePerLine` includes it in each line's trailing-comma condition; `renderCallPreserveGroups` widens its `isVeryLast` comma-suppression check to `!isVeryLast || keepTrailingComma`. All three changes are additive/gated -- C/C++/Java's `lang.isKotlin` is always false, so their existing behavior (never emit a final trailing comma) is completely unchanged. Verified: `make test` 276/276 -> 277/277 forward + idempotency, zero regressions (confirmed 276/276 identical both immediately before and immediately after this change, isolating the +1 to the one new fixture). New fixture `test/real_code_regressions_199_{inp,out}.kt` covers a multi-line call with a trailing comma (preserved), a multi-line call without one (still not added), and a `listOf(...)` list-literal with a trailing comma (proves list-literals share the same call-shaped render path, no separate fix needed). Manual repro also confirmed against a standalone `.kt` file via `--diff` before the fixture was written. |

| RDD_KEY_282 | Python3 job, STATE_PYTHON3.md's §9 "Gaps" list (3 of 4 documented gaps) closed: (1) `ScopePipelineIndent.isDefHeaderLine`'s `multiPhysicalLine` early-`false` bail was simply removed -- it only ever inspects the header's own leading token (`def`, optionally preceded by `async`), which is unaffected by how many physical lines the wrapped parameter list itself spans, so the bail was stricter than the underlying check actually required; no new handling needed, just the removal. (2) semicolon-chained statements: new `lineHasTopLevelReturnSegment` (§9.1 -- true iff ANY bracket-depth-0 `;`-delimited sub-statement of a line leads with `return`, not just the line's own first token; a qualifying line still gets its blank line inserted before the WHOLE physical line, since this pass only ever separates two existing `RawLine`s and never splits one physical line into two) and `lastSemicolonSegmentStart` (§9.2, via the rewritten `isUnconditionalExitLine` -- finds the LAST top-level `;`-delimited sub-statement's leading token, matching "last statement of the preceding block" semantics exactly, rather than the line's first token). (3) a §8-compact preceding block (`if x: return y`) ending in an exit statement: `isUnconditionalExitLine` now also delegates to the pre-existing `classifyCompactSingleStatementHeaderColon` to narrow its search to the body span past the header colon when the preceding line is itself a qualifying compact header, rather than inspecting the header keyword. One real bug found via `make test` (not just static reasoning, per STATE_COMMON.md's real-code-testing methodology): the first version of `lastSemicolonSegmentStart` initialized its "last semicolon seen" tracking variable to `-1` unconditionally; when a line's own significant span doesn't start at absolute token index 0 (essentially always true) and contains no `;` at all, `nextSignificant(tokens, lastSemi + 1, end)` incorrectly scanned from absolute token index `0` instead of the caller's own `start`, silently picking up whatever token happened to sit at the front of the file -- caught by a pre-existing fixture (`real_code_regressions_79_out.py`) losing its already-correct blank line before `elif e: continue`; fixed by initializing to `start - 1` instead of `-1`. (4) `try`/`except`/`finally` blank-line placement -- deliberately left as a scoped-out follow-up, not attempted: STYLE_PYTHON3.md §9.2's own text names only `elif`/`else`; extending the rule to `except`/`finally` would require first deciding what "the preceding block's last statement" even means for a `try` body (whose normal-exit path never reaches `except` at all), which is a genuine new design question requiring a style-doc decision, not a mechanical extension of the existing elif/else recognizer the other three gaps were. Verified: `make test` 282/282 -> 283/283 forward + idempotency (283/283 both immediately before -- after the `lastSemicolonSegmentStart` off-by-one fix -- and immediately after landing the one new fixture, isolating the +1). New fixture `test/py_control_flow_blank_line_gaps_{inp,out}.py` demonstrates all three fixed gaps: a multi-physical-line `def` header's `return`, a semicolon-chained `return` mid-function, a semicolon-chained preceding line before `elif`, and a §8-compact preceding `if`/`elif` before `elif`/`else`. |
| RDD_KEY_283 | C/C++/Java job, STATE_C_CPP_JAVA.md's previously-NOT-SCHEDULED item "`using` alias declarations not column-aligned" implemented: `DeclarationAlignmentRuleCurly`'s generic `parseDeclaration` path always rejected `using Name = Type;` because its `typeKeywords.contains(firstType.text)` guard only accepts KEYWORD-typed "types" from a fixed set, and `using` (a KEYWORD) is never in it -- the inverted `using Name = Type;` grammar genuinely doesn't fit the existing `typeTokens`/`name`/`initTokens` declaration model's forward `Type name = init;` shape, so it fell through untouched (no corruption, matching the item's original note). Added an isolated, additive parsing branch: new `Declaration.isUsingAlias` boolean field (10-arg constructor; old 9-arg delegates with `false`), and `parseUsingAlias` (called from `parseDeclaration` right after the modifiers loop, guarded by `lang.isCpp`) which recognizes `using IDENT = <tokens>;`, stores the alias name in `Declaration.name`, the aliased type's tokens in `initTokens` (reusing the existing field rather than adding a new one), and a dummy singleton `typeTokens` (just the `using` keyword token itself, never rendered) solely so `ScopePipelineCurly.applyDeclarationsPass`'s identity-based splice-back anchor lookup (`first.typeTokens.get(0)`) has a real token to find -- an empty `typeTokens` list there throws `IndexOutOfBoundsException`. `groupDeclarations` breaks a group whenever `isUsingAlias` changes between consecutive declarations, so alias groups never merge with plain-declaration groups or vice versa. Rendering dispatches to a new dedicated `renderUsingAliasGroup`, a simple 3-column `ColumnGrid` (`using` / name / `= Type;`) that aligns the `=` column across the group, independent of the generic declaration renderer's type/pointer-star column logic. One real bug found via `make test` per STATE_COMMON.md's real-code-testing methodology (not just static reasoning): the feature initially collided with the C++26 pack-indexing/variadic-template-declaration shapes already covered by pre-existing fixtures (`cpp26_core`/`cpp26_comments`) -- `template<typename... T> using Name = T...[...]` -- because `parseUsingAlias`'s generic re-render bypassed two separate, pre-existing raw-token-stream passes that own that spacing (`CppSpecificRule.enforcePackIndexingSpacing`'s "no space before `[` after `...`" rule and a separate variadic-template `typename...` tight-join rule), producing `template<typename ... T>` and `T...[offsets[i]]` corruption; fixed with a narrow bail-out in `parseUsingAlias` that returns null (leaving the statement fully untouched, the established "leave untouched when unsafe to collapse/re-render" posture per RDD_KEY_225's precedent) whenever any `...` operator token appears in the declaration's `templatePrefix` or aliased-type tokens. Verified: `make test` 283/283 -> 284/284 forward + idempotency, zero regressions (confirmed via a clean `rm -rf target/classes` rebuild against true git HEAD producing an identical baseline result, and separated stdout/stderr `make test` redirects to avoid a merged-stream log-truncation false read). New fixture `test/cpp_using_alias_{inp,out}.cpp` demonstrates: adjacent plain `using Foo = int;`/`using Bar = ...;`/`using LongerName = ...;` aliases of differing name length aligning on `=`, a `template<typename T> using Vec = std::vector<T>;` singleton group left as its own group (not merged with the file-scope plain-alias group above it), and a function-local `using Local = double;` unaffected by outer-scope grouping. |
| RDD_KEY_284 | C/C++/Java job, follow-up to RDD_KEY_283: extended `using`-alias column alignment to cover the C++26 pack-indexing/variadic-template shapes (`template<typename... T> using Name = T...[N];`) that RDD_KEY_283 had deliberately bailed out of untouched. Root cause of why the bail was overly broad: the generic re-render (`renderInitTokens`/`renderTemplatePrefix`) has no `...`-adjacency spacing awareness, but `CppSpecificRule.enforcePackIndexingSpacing` runs as a whole-file pass in `FormatterCurly`'s Phase 4, strictly AFTER declaration alignment, re-tokenizing whatever text alignment produced regardless of which pass emitted it -- so the aliased-type half of the bail was unnecessarily conservative; only the `template<...>` prefix half genuinely has no downstream fix-up pass for its `typename...` tight-join spacing. Fix: instead of bailing, both `Declaration.aliasRawTypeText` and a new `Declaration.templatePrefixRawText` (nullable, set only when the corresponding span contains a `...` token) now carry the EXACT original raw text (via `rawSliceBetweenUnfiltered`, already used for the pre-existing multiline-brace-body check) for `renderUsingAliasGroup` to emit verbatim instead of regenerating -- sidestepping the spacing-correctness question entirely for the prefix (nothing is regenerated, so nothing can be gotten wrong) while leaning on the existing downstream pass for the aliased type. One real bug found via `make test` against the `cpp26_comments` fixture (not just static reasoning): a standalone comment sitting between a `template<...>` prefix and the `using` keyword itself (`template<typename... T>\n// note\nusing Name = ...;`) has nowhere to live in the `Declaration` model -- `body` is already comment-filtered by the time `parseUsingAlias` sees it, so accepting the statement silently dropped that comment on `render`'s full-span regeneration. Fixed by detecting any `COMMENT_LINE`/`COMMENT_BLOCK` token in the raw unfiltered gap between the end of `templatePrefix` and the `using` keyword and bailing (leaving the whole statement untouched) whenever one is found. Also fixed a trailing-same-line-comment spacing bug caught in the same pass: `renderUsingAliasGroup` was joining the comment with a single space (`" " + comment.text`) instead of the two-space convention used everywhere else in this codebase for a same-line trailing `//` comment (e.g. `JsTsSpecificRule`'s `sb.append("  ").append(...)` call sites, STYLE.md's own `// single-level -- pad` examples) -- a single-declaration group with no sibling to force grid padding was rendering `= T...[0]; // comment` instead of the correct `= T...[0];  // comment`. Verified: `make test` unchanged at 284/284 forward + idempotency (no new fixture -- this refines RDD_KEY_283's existing `cpp_using_alias_{inp,out}.cpp` fixture's own scope only implicitly; the pre-existing `cpp26_comments_{inp,out}.cpp` fixture's expected output was updated in place to reflect the new, more-complete alignment behavior, verified byte-identical against the formatter's actual real output). |
| RDD_KEY_285 | CPP26 job, `stephenberry/glaze` dogfood follow-up: fixed `json_patch_test.cpp`'s round1/round2 idempotency failure (general line-breaking logic, not C++26-specific per se, but the trigger shape -- `glz::generic(2.0)` -- is a C++26-adjacent reflection-library call). Root cause: `ScopePipelineCurly.applyOversizedAggregateInitClosingBracePass`'s `hasNewlineInside` scan (meant to detect a genuinely oversized aggregate init, e.g. a byte/word table spanning many source lines, and move its dangling `}` onto its own line) counted any NEWLINE token inside the outer `{...}` at any brace depth, including one strictly inside a nested call's own already-wrapped argument list (e.g. `glz::generic(\n  2.0\n)`, wrapped elsewhere by `MiscRuleCurly.enforceCallLineBreaking`) -- a fresh single-line source has no such newline when this pass runs (round1 correctly skips it), but reformatting round1's own output (round2) sees the call's already-wrapped newline and wrongly treats the whole init as oversized, moving `}` onto its own line -- non-idempotent; classic pass-ordering bug, same family as `real_code_regressions_76`'s `enforceAttributeAndSpliceBracketPadding` fix and the `PowerShellSpecificRule.java`/`applyAssignmentsPass` ordering bug (`STATE_COMMON.md`). Fix: gate the newline check on paren/bracket depth (only a NEWLINE at paren/bracket depth 0 counts), mirroring the existing `ScopePipelineCore.hasTopLevelNewline` technique used elsewhere in this codebase for the identical "is this newline from the statement's own multi-line shape, or from a nested call's own wrap" distinction. First attempt applied this unconditionally across every curly-family language and regressed `test/real_code_regressions_179_inp.ts` -- a JS/TS object literal (`pathOptions = { ..., getNormalizedAbsolutePath(\n ...\n), ... };`) whose expected output relies on the pre-existing behavior of moving `}` onto its own line even though its only newline comes from a nested wrapped call, structurally indistinguishable from the glaze C++ shape to a language-agnostic paren/bracket-depth gate. Per the user's own suggestion mid-session, re-scoped the whole paren/bracket-depth-tracking branch to `lang.isCpp` only (every other language's `parenDepth` stays permanently 0, so the gate is unconditionally satisfied there -- JS/TS/Java/Kotlin behavior byte-for-byte unchanged, `real_code_regressions_179` unaffected). Verified via real-code-testing methodology (debug reproduction, not static reasoning alone): re-cloned `stephenberry/glaze` fresh (`/tmp/glaze`'s prior checkout had been cleaned by the system), reproduced the exact round1/round2 diff on the real `tests/json_test/json_patch_test.cpp` and a minimal local repro, closed both cleanly after the fix, and re-ran round1/round2 over the entire `tests/json_test/` directory (21 files) with no further diffs. `make test`: 284/284 forward + idempotency, zero regressions. New fixture `test/cpp26_nested_call_wrap_{inp,out}.cpp`. |
| RDD_KEY_286 | C/C++/Java job (shared mechanism, all curly/tag/indent/tooling families): extended the `JXM_CFMT_CFG` in-file directive (RDD_KEY_167) with a `--lang` pseudo-key (`//% JXM_CFMT_CFG --lang=cpp` / `/*% JXM_CFMT_CFG --lang=cpp */`), matching the CLI `--lang` flag's name and accepted values. Motivation: a per-invocation `--lang` flag on the CLI, or `lang=` query param in server mode, forces every file in that invocation/request to one language; this instead lets a single source file force its own language permanently, addressing two real cases -- (1) a `.h` file that is actually C++ (README.md's Known Limitations item, curly-brace family #6, previously scoped to only C++26 §5 reflection rules, broadened this session to cover every C++-only rule across the whole `cpp` pipeline); (2) a templated source file whose extension `Lang.infer` can't recognize at all (e.g. `.java.in`/`.java.inc`), without needing `--lang` remembered on every future invocation. Implementation: `InFileConfig.parse` special-cases the literal key `"--lang"` -- exempt from the ordinary `Config.isKnownKey` per-file-applicable check (it isn't a `Config` key at all), instead validated against `Lang.isRecognized` with the same error-message shape as the CLI's own `--lang` validation; its value is left in the returned map under key `"--lang"` for the caller to extract before passing the remaining map to `Config.resolve`. `InFileConfig.parse`'s empty-result case changed from `Collections.emptyMap()` to a fresh mutable `LinkedHashMap` so every caller can unconditionally call `.remove("--lang")` on it without an `UnsupportedOperationException` special case for the empty-directive path. `Main.java`'s `processFile` reordered to read the file and run `InFileConfig.parse` before deciding the language (previously language was decided from `explicitLanguage`/extension before the file was even read) -- precedence is in-file `--lang` first, then CLI `--lang`, then extension inference, matching every other `JXM_CFMT_CFG` key's "always wins" precedence (Configuration section's tier 7). `ServerMode.FormatHandler.handle` reordered the same way (read body / parse in-file config before deciding `language` from the `lang` query param or `Lang.infer(path)`) so a direct `/format` caller (not going through `Main`'s CLI, which already resolves and forwards the final language) gets the same precedence; `Main.delegateToServer`'s own client-side language resolution already happens before delegation, so it forwards the correct (in-file-aware) language to the server unchanged. New fixture `test/in_file_config_lang_{inp,out}.h`: a `.h` file (default-infers to C) with `--lang=cpp` in the directive, proving the override via the empty-parameter-list rendering flip (C's `bar(void)` -> C++'s `bar()`). `make test`/`make test-server`: 285/285 -> 286/286 forward + idempotency, zero regressions. `README.md` updated: new "Language override (`--lang`)" subsection under "In-file config overrides", a cross-reference added to the CLI `--lang` section, the Configuration precedence list's tier 7 entry, and Known Limitations curly-brace-family item 6 broadened from "C++26 §5 reflection rules" to the whole C++-only rule surface plus a mention of the new directive as an override mechanism. |
| RDD_KEY_287 | Python3 job: root-caused and fixed the "§8 join-threshold behavior" non-determinism previously noted only as a one-line symptom name in `STATE_PYTHON3.md` (seen but never diagnosed during `psf/black`/`click`/`flask`/`django` round1->round2 idempotency dogfood re-runs). Root cause: `applySingleStatementBody`'s `if`/`for`/etc. join path, `classifyCaseLine`'s §7 `case` virtualJoin path, and `flushCaseGroup`'s post-alignment length guard each measure a candidate joined line's fits-check (`physicalLineLength(...) <= lineLength`) against only the header+body text -- but `tryQualifyJoinBody`'s `bodyContentEnd` comes from `trimEndIdx`, which treats a trailing `COMMENT_LINE` as a gap token and excludes it from the trimmed span. Since the join's own `Replacement` only spans up to `bodyContentEnd`, a body's trailing comment (deliberately retained verbatim per the §8 2026-08-11 extension) survives untouched immediately after the joined text on the same physical output line -- so the fits-check silently undercounts the comment's own width. Round1 could therefore accept and emit a joined line that is actually over `lineLength` once the comment is counted; round2 then sees that already-compact, over-limit line and correctly reverses it via the separate compact-overflow-expand check (`classifyCompactSingleStatementHeaderColon`'s branch), which DOES measure the whole physical line (comment included) -- a genuine non-convergent flip-flop (not the cosmetic comment-classifier-capitalization non-determinism it was originally lumped in with), confirmed via minimal repro (`if total_length == max_length and i != last_index: break  # Not at sentence end...`, from `pallets/click`'s `utils.py`) and an A/B build from commit `9eb1bd4` (the commit landing the §8 body-trailing-comment-retention extension, well before this session's or the recent §4/§6 signature-wrap work) reproducing the identical flip-flop, proving it was pre-existing and not introduced by recent changes. Fixed narrowly in `ScopePipelineIndent.java` only (3 call sites, all measuring `joined + trailingSuffix` -- the untouched comment/whitespace suffix computed via `verbatimLineText(tokens, bodyContentEnd-or-equivalent, bodyLine.end)` -- instead of `joined` alone): `applySingleStatementBody`'s join branch, `classifyCaseLine`'s virtualJoin computation (gained a `bodyLineEnd` field on `CaseLine` to carry the body `RawLine`'s own end through to `flushCaseGroup`), and `flushCaseGroup`'s per-member pre-commit length-budget loop. Re-ran the full dogfood re-check against `/tmp/click`+`/tmp/flask` (161 files) and `/tmp/django` (2927 files) post-fix: the 4 files that previously flip-flopped on this bug (`click/src/click/parser.py`, `click/src/click/utils.py`, `flask/src/flask/app.py`, `flask/src/flask/helpers.py`) and the 3 django files with the same cause (`django/utils/flatpages/middleware.py`, `django/core/management/commands/loaddata.py`, `docs/lint.py`) are now idempotent; every remaining round1/round2 diff across both re-runs (2 click/flask files, 14 django files) is the separately-tracked, out-of-scope comment-classifier trailing-period-normalization drift, confirmed by direct diff inspection of each. New identity-pass fixture `test/real_code_regressions_202_{inp,out}.py` (covers both the plain `if`/`for` join path and the `case` virtualJoin path). `make test`: 289/289 -> 290/290 forward + idempotency, zero regressions. |
| RDD_KEY_288 | Python3 job: root-caused and fixed a round1/round2 non-idempotency bug found via self-hosting dogfood (formatting this repo's own `tools/verifiers/{html,python,toml}_content_diff.py`, each containing a hand-column-aligned `if`/`elif`/`else` one-liner chain like `if cond1: stmt1` / `elif cond2:                  stmt2` / `else:                  stmt3`). STYLE_PYTHON3.md §8's single-statement-body compactness decision is per-branch/independent (no group-uniformity requirement, unlike §7's explicit case-group all-or-nothing rule), so each of `if`/`elif`/`else` in a chain is meant to compact or expand purely on its own fits-check against `line-length`. Root cause: `ScopePipelineIndent.applySingleStatementBody`'s "already compact" branch (the `classifyCompactSingleStatementHeaderColon` path, for a header+body already written on one physical line) measured the RAW verbatim source span (`verbatimLineText(header.start, header.end)`) against `lineLength` to decide overflow -- but that raw span includes any stale whitespace between the header's `:` and the body statement, such as a hand-written column-alignment padding run. A padded `elif`/`else` line that comfortably fits `lineLength` once normalized to a single space was therefore wrongly judged as overflowing, forcing it to expand into full block form on round1 (alongside a genuinely-overflowing sibling `if` branch in the same chain, which correctly expands on its own merits) -- round1 over-expanded the whole chain. Round2, reformatting round1's now-block-form output, correctly re-joins the padding-free `elif`/`else` headers back to compact via the separate join path (which measures normalized `headerText + " " + bodyText`, not raw verbatim text), leaving `if` alone (still genuinely over length) -- round1 and round2 disagree, a genuine non-convergent flip-flop, not cosmetic drift. Same bug family/lesson as RDD_KEY_287 (a fits-check measuring the wrong text state instead of what the line will actually render as). Fixed in `ScopePipelineIndent.applySingleStatementBody`'s compact-colon branch: compute `headerText`/`bodyText` first, measure the normalized `headerText + " " + bodyText` against `lineLength` instead of the raw verbatim span, and (new) collapse any stale padding down to the canonical single space even when no expansion is needed, so a fits-but-padded compact line is corrected on the very first pass rather than only indirectly via an expand-then-rejoin round trip. Reproduced and verified against the real triggering files directly (not just reasoning): `tools/verifiers/{html,python,toml}_content_diff.py` all round1/round2-idempotent after the fix, matching STYLE_PYTHON3.md §8's per-branch independent-eligibility rule (the `if` branch legitimately stays block-form since it alone exceeds `line-length` even normalized; `elif`/`else` become compact one-liners with normalized single-space spacing). New fixture `test/real_code_regressions_204_{inp,out}.py`. `make test`: 312/312 -> 313/313 forward + idempotency, zero regressions (one unrelated pre-existing failure on `test/real_code_regressions_172_inp.ts`, from a concurrently-running session's in-progress `BlockStructureRule.java` edit, was not caused by and is untouched by this fix -- confirmed by reverting only `ScopePipelineIndent.java` and reproducing the same `.ts` failure with this session's edit absent). `tools/verifiers/{html,python,toml}_content_diff.py` themselves left unreformatted in `tools/` per the ground rule that bulk-adopting a reformat is the user's call, not this session's. |

| RDD_KEY_289 | C/C++/Java job: self-hosting dogfood (formatting this repo's own `src/**/*.java` with itself, comparing round1 against the current `src/` tree) found 2 real bugs, combined into one fixture, `test/real_code_regressions_205_{inp,out}.java`. (a) `src/com/jxmake/formatter/rules/JsTsSpecificRule.java`'s `renderTemplateHoleInterior` has a hand-written trailing closing-brace comment, `} // while !closed`, on the `while(!closed) { ... }` loop nested inside that method's own outer `while( i < interior.size() )` loop. `BlockStructureRule.decideComment`'s FOR/WHILE/SWITCH case only calls `extractVariable` (to build a same-kind-nested-loop disambiguating label, e.g. the existing `// for i`/`// for e` convention) when `f.sameKindNested` is true -- true here since both loops are `Kind.WHILE`. `extractVariable` delegates to `extractSingleIdentifier` for WHILE/SWITCH (unlike FOR's dedicated `extractForVariable`), which has an explicit 2-token case for a negated single-identifier condition (`!identifier`, i.e. `!closed`) but returned only `sig.get(1).text` (the bare identifier "closed"), discarding the `!` token entirely. `addClosingComments`'s emission path (`comment != null` when `existingCommentIdx >= 0`) then unconditionally replaces the *entire* existing comment text with the freshly-decided label via `replaceTokens.put(existingCommentIdx, "// " + comment)`, regardless of what the original comment said -- so a hand-written `} // while !closed` silently became `} // while closed` on every reformat, exactly inverting its documented meaning (a real corruption, not cosmetic, since the comment is prose a human reads to understand loop-exit intent). Fixed by preserving the leading `!` in `extractSingleIdentifier`'s return value (`"!" + sig.get(1).text` instead of `sig.get(1).text`) -- the resulting label (`while !closed`) matches the original hand-written comment exactly, and the fix is generic (not special-cased to this one file), so it also correctly labels a future genuine same-kind-nested `while(!flag)`/`switch(!x)` construct that currently has no existing comment to preserve. (b) `src/com/jxmake/formatter/tokenizer/TokenizerCurly.java`'s `skipBalancedBraceHole` method has a correctly flush-aligned `if( Token.isPunct(t, "{") ) ++braceDepth; else if( Token.isPunct(t, "}") ) { ... }` pair (both at column 12, matching STYLE.md §12's flush if/else-if alignment convention) -- this method's own signature currently sits on one physical line exceeding `line-length` (100 default), so it wraps to multi-line on reformat, which is how this bug was originally surfaced during real-code testing, but the root cause turned out to be entirely unrelated to signature-wrapping (a minimal repro with a short, unwrapped signature reproduces identically). Root cause: `BlockStructureRule.alignBracelessElseIfChain` operates on the whole-file text AFTER tokenization/re-rendering, detecting an `if`/`else if`/`else` chain purely via line-text prefix matching (`lines[i].regionMatches(indentLen, "if(", 0, 3)`, then `"else if(", 0, 8` / `"else ", 0, 5` for each subsequent line at the same indent) -- with no check anywhere in the chain-detection loop that a matched member's *body* (the text after its condition's closing paren) is actually braceless. This method's own name, its javadoc, and RDD_KEY_129's design decision ("mixed/braced chain left untouched") all assume it only ever runs on an all-braceless chain (produced upstream by `BlockStructureRule.alignBracelessElseIfChain`'s sibling collapse passes, gated on `chainAllBranchesCollapsible` requiring every branch to qualify) -- but a hand-written source file can independently contain a *mixed* chain that was never produced by collapse at all: `skipBalancedBraceHole`'s `if(...) ++braceDepth;` is a genuine one-line braceless statement as originally typed, immediately followed by a sibling `else if(...) { ... }` that is genuinely, multi-line braced. The line-text scan matched both as chain members (both start with the right keyword prefix at the right indent), then proceeded to left-pad the `if` line's keyword by `"else if".length() - "if(".length()` = 5 columns (the same delta the method uses to column-align a genuine braceless chain's keyword prefixes) -- corrupting the `if` line's indentation from column 12 to column 17 on every reformat, a genuine non-cosmetic indentation regression (idempotent at the corrupted value, so `make test`'s idempotency check alone would never have caught it -- only comparing against the known-correct hand-written original did). A body-column-padding step later in the same method (aligning each branch's body to a shared column past the condition) also ran against the braced `else if` branch's `{` body with no braced-body check, though its effect was masked in this particular repro by the already-max keyword width leaving its own padding a no-op. Fixed with two coordinated changes in `alignBracelessElseIfChain`: (1) a new early bail-out, immediately after the chain-detection loop builds `chain` (before the keyword left-padding loop that mutates `lines[]`) and before any mutation happens, that re-scans each non-bare-else chain member (using the same condition-closing-paren depth-tracking scan the later body-column loop already performs) and bails the *entire* chain untouched (`i = j; continue;`) the moment any member's body starts with `{` (via a new small helper, `startsBracedBody(String line, int from)`, skipping any run of spaces from `from` and checking the next character); (2) a symmetric, narrower defense-in-depth check inside the later per-branch `prefixEnd`/`target` computation loop (using the already-parsed `closeParen` there) that also sets `ok = false` and bails if reached for a still-braced member, in case a future code path builds `chain` differently and skips step (1). A bare terminal `else { ... }` was deliberately EXCLUDED from both braced-body checks (checked, not just assumed) -- it is a pre-existing, deliberately supported shape this same method already column-aligns (verified against the pre-existing `test/real_code_regressions_172_out.ts` fixture's `else                   {` line, which failed once when an earlier draft of this fix over-broadly also bailed on a braced bare-`else`, and was restored to pass by narrowing the check to only `if`/`else if` members). Verified via minimal isolated repro matching `skipBalancedBraceHole`'s exact shape (before fix: `if` mis-indented to column 17 vs. `else if`'s column 12; after fix: both column 8/12 as authored, byte-identical round1/round2) and via the new combined fixture. `make test`: 313/313 -> 314/314 forward + idempotency, zero regressions (313 was this session's actual starting baseline -- a concurrent Python3-job session had already landed `real_code_regressions_204` and its own unrelated fixture between this session's initial 312/312 baseline read and this fix, per that session's own RDD_KEY_288 entry; no file-level conflict, confirmed via `git log`/`git status` showing that session's commit already landed cleanly before this session's own commit). `src/com/jxmake/formatter/rules/JsTsSpecificRule.java` and `src/com/jxmake/formatter/tokenizer/TokenizerCurly.java` (the two files that surfaced these bugs) were confirmed to reformat cleanly (idempotent, corruption-free) after the fix, but left untouched in `src/` itself -- adopting a bulk reformat of `src/` is the user's own call per this session's ground rules, not performed here. |
